**Semantic before after.** No catalog change is proposed, so the item's semantics are unchanged: paint_refresh_recommended keeps kind modernization, ontology_basis dated_style, subject 'interior paint color', state 'dated or highly personalized colors', name 'Paint Refresh Opportunity', its description, embed_text and support_any ['repaint','paint refresh','dated paint','bold color'], and empty deny_any/null require_any. What changes is outside the catalog: Terra's reading of the existing disjunction. Today Terra collapses 'dated OR highly personalized' into a hue test on the second limb and answers 'the colour is neutral, therefore not supported' (four rationales, all four rejecting on personalization; 29c5c08e concedes 'neutral beige' and still rejects). The intended reading is that either limb suffices and that the 'dated' limb is decided on convention-4 concrete tells - an identifiable era-specific finish (builder gold-tan/beige or taupe-brown trim, popcorn ceiling, period-typical scheme) or a visibly worn, yellowed, patched paint surface - so that a neutral hue is not by itself disqualifying and, symmetrically, a clean current neutral with no tell is not billable.

**Retrieval effects.** None. No embed_text, name, description, support_any or deny_any is touched, so no item's rank moves anywhere, and the offline-unverifiable embedding risk called out in the brief is not incurred. This matters because paint_refresh_recommended already ranks 1-3 on every issue in this cluster: there is nothing retrieval could improve.

**Economics fit.** No economics change and none would be natively authorable: cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope are never settable through the decisions file for a carryover or a split successor. The item keeps work_item_code PAINT_INTERIOR, tier optional, severity 1, scope cosmetic, trade paint_drywall. min_photo_evidence lives inside estimate and is untouched, so the evidence gate is unaffected.

**Package route effects.** route_override stays null (its only legal value would be no_action, which is wrong for a billable repaint) and no routing metadata is authorable for a carryover in any case. Context for the recommended calibration: recovering these conditions feeds package_affinity entries where paint_refresh_recommended is package_support in bedroom_modernization, kitchen_modernization and living_modernization. Support role never creates a package - it can only strengthen one that a driver already opened - and package_strength is computed at runtime, so the calibration cannot open new packages by itself. Expect a small increase in modernization-package support conditions on properties that already have a modernization driver, and no route or tier movement.

**Product policy.** Not affected. The trade bucket is paint_drywall, which is not product-quarantined; the electrical quarantine that forces non_catalog_action elsewhere is irrelevant here, and this cluster's non_catalog_action outcome comes from the evidence, not from a quarantine.

**Expected benefit.** Four real, human-confirmed billable conditions on four distinct properties (three canary, one production) currently lost at Terra with reason_code verdict_unsupported would be recoverable, plus the reviewed positive uses that already work would keep working. The benefit is measured the way the scope direction asks: fewer lost real conditions, with no new hallucinations - the recipe is explicitly tell-based, so the three current neutrals that were correctly rejected stay rejected and the one reviewer-refuted over-bill should flip to rejected. Because the fix is a verifier-side calibration rather than a catalog edit, it also generalises to the other dated_style claims Terra reads the same way instead of being one item's patch.

**Regression risk.** The dominant risk is that a tell list written loosely degenerates back into a hue test in the permissive direction and starts billing every warm-toned wall. rc_4abeff09724f proves that failure mode is already live on this item under the current wording, so the calibration must be validated against all eight controls below before it is adopted, and specifically must keep rc_ef3234842cb9 (plain mid-grey) and rc_c34e48007ce2 (current greige) rejected. A second risk is double-billing in rooms where the era tell belongs to another item - the rc_64a78239f11f rooms (tan over grooved panelling under a popcorn ceiling) are the case in point, and the reviewer could not settle them; the calibration should require a tell attributable to the PAINT (era colour, worn/yellowed/patched finish) rather than borrowing datedness from panelling, ceiling texture or cabinetry, which have their own items. Third, this cluster's own two weaker units (8f96473f, 29c5c08e) sit close to the convention-4 line, so a calibration tuned to recover them specifically would be tuned past the line; the bar survives without them and the recipe should be tuned to the two strong ones.

**Gap description.** No coverage gap is declared. paint_refresh_recommended exists, is scene-open in all three scenes involved, was retrieved at rank 1-3 on every issue, was correctly selected by 2d on every issue, and carries the affirmed 'dated' disjunct in its atomic_claim.

**Unresolved questions.** Truth conflict on rc_4abeff09724f: frozen v1.1 truth says claim exact / work warranted / supported_billed and Terra said 'supported', while the Phase B reviewer adjudicated the same photo refuted ('freshly painted mid-tone blue-grey, a current neutral'). Both are pinned facts and neither is changed here. Which one governs decides whether the recommended Terra calibration should be scored as fixing an over-bill or as introducing a miss, so the reconciliation owner should rule on it before the calibration is measured., Boundary question with CAP-001: on the two weaker units the reviewer's supporting evidence is as much deterioration as era - 8f96473f is a dull, dingy, unevenly discoloured finish and 29c5c08e carries a streaky patch-discoloured hall wall. If deterioration rather than era is the operative tell, the natural home for those observations is peeling_or_discolored_paint ('peeling, bubbling, or visibly aged finish'), which sits in CAP-001 and was kind-gated against modernization-kinded observations there. Nobody should act on this without a ruling, because the two clusters currently pull in opposite directions on the same physical surface., Should the tell list live in Terra's prompt/rubric or in the atomic_claim? This cluster recommends the prompt, on the grounds that the claim already says 'dated' and that the control set shows two-sided error. If a later reviewer decides Terra cannot be calibrated without claim-side tells, the op is natively expressible (paint_refresh_recommended is a reclassified carryover, so /successors/0/atomic_claim with a refreshed /atomicity_rationale and /expected_effects/routing is settable), but it would need fresh human review of the three refuted/unclear controls first., rc_64a78239f11f leaves untested the case where dated colour, grooved wood panelling and popcorn ceiling co-occur. Until that is adjudicated, the corpus has no reviewed answer to whether paint_refresh_recommended should fire alongside dated_wood_paneling and popcorn_or_acoustic_ceiling_texture in the same room or defer to them.

**Human disposition.** _blank_ (approved / approved_with_modification / rejected / deferred / reclassified_non_catalog)

### CAP-011 — Porch roof/soffit sagging that Terra could not assess

Status `non_catalog_action` · outcome `non_catalog_action` · confidence `medium` · items `soffit_or_porch_ceiling_failed`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows visible waviness or sagging at the porch roof connection (a structural or framing signal) even though the soffit or porch ceiling panels themselves are not clearly visible, so a claim about soffit/ceiling panels cannot be assessed while the observation is real.

**Cluster.** subject: porch roof to house connection (fascia line, shingle line and the junction boards), and the soffit / porch ceiling panels underneath it; mechanism: waviness and sagging of the porch-roof fascia/shingle line seen from the street, with a band of dark, ragged, out-of-plane deteriorated boards where the porch roof meets the main roof; the underside soffit / porch ceiling panels are not visible from this viewpoint; kinds: defect; scenes: exterior; commitments: object, location. UNCHANGED from the provisional cluster, and deliberately kept as a singleton. One human unit, one case, one issue. Convention 2 keeps it one evidence unit. No other unit in the exterior family shares the physical subject: CAP-009 is porch/patio walking surfaces, CAP-002/018/019 are cladding, masonry and door casing, CAP-010 is fencing, CAP-012 is a gable opening. Merging it into any of those would pool a roof-connection structural signal with finish-level cladding wear and would manufacture a bar out of subjects that share only the scene - exactly the pooling defect the family structure step dismantled in CAP-002. It stays alone and it stays at bar none.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions subject, billability, safety.

**Diagnosis.** primary `pass_2d_selection`; contributing overlapping_or_ambiguous_ontology. The catalog is NOT at fault and I am not asking for a change to it. soffit_or_porch_ceiling_failed is the one item in the exterior family whose divergence scan is completely clean - promised_not_claimed [] and claimed_not_promised [] - meaning its retrieval surface and its atomic claim describe the same thing. Its atomic_claim ('soffit or porch ceiling panels' / 'sagging, loose, or missing panels') is precise, correctly split from its degradation sibling soffit_or_porch_ceiling_weathered, and it did exactly what a good claim should do: it refused to certify an object the photo does not show. Convention 1 asks which surface failed, and the answer here is neither. The residual catalog-shaped observation is an ontology one, and it is a near miss rather than a defect: three exterior items sit adjacent to a porch-roof connection (soffit_or_porch_ceiling_failed for the panels, damaged_or_unsafe_deck_or_porch for the porch structure, roofline_water_damage_suspected for the eave/fascia line but only under a moisture-evidence basis), the boundary between them is drawn on retrieval text rather than on claim text, and nothing names framing deflection as such. That is worth documenting for a future pass. It is not worth a change proposal on one unit, and at bar none it could not carry one anyway.

**Expected benefit.** The benefit is documentary and it is routed away from the catalog: one pass_2d_follow_ups entry that names a reachable, kind-compatible, scene-compatible better item at rank 3 with a concrete lexical hypothesis for why rank 1 won; one recorded near-miss observation that the porch-assembly boundary between three exterior items is drawn on retrieval text rather than claim text; one recorded pattern of Terra returning cannot_assess on distant, safety-adjacent structure and the condition landing in inspection rather than being lost; and one recorded metadata observation that rotted_subfloor_or_structural_framing, the catalog's only framing concept, omits 'exterior' from its scene_groups. Each is a note or a lane referral, none is a catalog edit.

**Regression risk.** Zero from this judgment, since no ops are proposed. The risk this judgment AVOIDS is the real point: with 0 positive uses, 0 correct rejections and 0 hallucination annotations for soffit_or_porch_ceiling_failed, any edit to it would be shipped blind - a regression_controls list would be vacuously complete and would prove nothing. Widening its subject toward the porch roof assembly would also make it easier to fire on any street view with a wavy roof line, the exact failure no control here could detect.

**Gap description.** Recorded as a hypothesis that I tested and did NOT declare - see coverage_gap.rationale. Shape of the near miss, for the record: no catalog item names framing deflection at an exterior roof connection as such. soffit_or_porch_ceiling_failed covers the panels beneath, damaged_or_unsafe_deck_or_porch covers the porch structure but reads on retrieval as walking surfaces, roofline_water_damage_suspected covers the eave/fascia but only under a moisture-evidence basis, and rotted_subfloor_or_structural_framing - the only framing concept in the 128 - has no 'exterior' scene group and is a severity-5 STRUCTURAL_REPAIR concept in any case. I conclude the rank-3 item's atomic_claim is adequate, so this is a 2d selection story and not missing coverage. It is also not declarable: a declared coverage gap on a non-deferred outcome requires a met bar, and with no legitimate split parent it would force migration_system_gap. Both reasons point the same way and the first is the real one.

**Diagnosis.** primary `downstream_tier_projection_routing_package_costing`; contributing catalog_wording_or_specificity, missing_catalog_coverage, terra_verification. The catalog is NOT the owner, and that is the whole finding. Asking what went wrong operationally rather than semantically flips the answer. Operationally nothing was lost: the boarded opening is real, work is warranted (human truth 'misnamed_but_warranted', work 'warranted'), Terra supported it, and it was accepted for work. What went wrong is what it was billed AS. boarded_up_entry_or_window carries severity 4, scope 'replace', trade_bucket trim_doors_windows, tier work, bills true, work_item_code DOOR_WINDOW_REPLACE and an embed that says 'Replace and restore door or window assembly'. A nailed board panel over an attic-level gable opening was therefore priced as replacing a door or window assembly. Every lever that would correct that is unauthorable: severity, scope, trade_bucket and route_override are not settable on a carryover (the decisions entry is change_type 'unchanged' with one successor and no legitimate split parent), and cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity and package_role are never authorable on any entry by construction. Meanwhile both natively authorable moves are affirmatively wrong. Widening /successors/0/atomic_claim from subject 'entry or window opening' to any wall or gable opening is native (the lead's dry runs confirm the shape is expressible) but it would make the severity-4 DOOR_WINDOW_REPLACE billing MORE legitimate on exactly the cases where it is least appropriate, and it would open the item to interior near-misses it already retrieves: at redfin_11000447 it ranked 1 on 'The wood header or trim area at the stair opening is exposed' (2d resolved nothing) and ranked 3, 4 and 5 on three interior wiring/electrical-cutout observations that resolved to visible_electrical_risks. Adding deny_any for gable/attic via overrides is also native but would suppress a real, human-warranted condition that no item in the catalog covers. So the smallest correct lever is not a catalog change at all: record the severity/scope/work-item mismatch as an economics and work-item policy gap, record the absent gable/attic-opening concept together with the absence of a legitimate split parent to create one, carry the reviewer's own note, and change nothing in the catalog. Secondary catalog-side observations recorded but not proposed: the atomic_claim over-commits on subject (unsupported_commitment_types ['object']); the rendered claim_text is malformed - 'entry or window opening opening boarded over', with 'opening' duplicated because subject 'entry or window opening' concatenates with state 'opening boarded over'; and the divergence scan gives promised_not_claimed ['damage_break', 'door', 'sag_structural'], i.e. the retrieval surface promises damage and structural sag that the claim never asserts. A one-unit cluster at bar 'none' must not propose those edits.

| rejected owner | why |

But under the 2026-09-03 rule the operative question is 'right item unavailable' versus 'right item reachable but another selected', and this is the first case. The frozen candidate list holds eight items: boarded_up_entry_or_window (1), soffit_or_porch_ceiling_failed (2), damaged_or_unsafe_deck_or_porch (3), fence_broken_or_leaning (4), roof_shingles_missing_or_curling (5), damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim (6), roofline_water_damage_suspected (7), paving_heaved_or_trip_hazard (8). Every one of the other seven is a worse fit than the one chosen, and no gable/attic-opening item exists anywhere in the 128 to have been retrieved. 2d picked the best available item. There is therefore no pass_2d_follow_ups entry and pass_2d_follow_ups is empty; the divergence from the frozen attribution is flagged as an unresolved question and the attribution record is left untouched. |
| upstream_pass_2a_observation | 2a is not implicated and is frozen regardless. The 2a prose named the subject accurately and neutrally - 'The upper-gable opening is boarded' - and tied it to attic ventilation, framing and insulation, i.e. the correct domain, with no word suggesting a door, entry or window. 2b joined exact at bullet_index 13 preserving the wording verbatim, and 2c kept the bullet unchanged in p2c_surviving. Nothing upstream of 2d introduced the wrong subject. |

Widening legitimises the severity-4 replace billing and admits known interior false positives (see the ownership argument). Narrowing or denying gable/attic suppresses a real warranted condition no other item covers. The cluster is also at bar 'none' with a single unit on a single property and zero reviewed controls, which independently blocks native_decisions_proposal. Recorded as a contributing cause only. |
| migration_system_gap | Tempting and half true, but it mislocates the owner. Severity and scope really are unauthorable on this carryover - that part is a migration-authoring limit. But we would not want severity 4 / scope 'replace' changed on THIS item: for a genuinely boarded-up door or window those values are correct. The fields that are wrong for a gable panel are the economics fields (work_item_code, cost, estimate), which are never authorable on any entry, carryover or split, by design - they are a pricing and work-item policy surface that sits outside the migration system rather than a gap in it. Calling this a migration-system gap would point the lead at the decisions-file authoring surface when the fix belongs to work-item policy plus a design decision about whether a 'generic presence' opening concept should exist. |

That is a real, recordable defect and it is listed as a contributing cause. It is not the primary owner because correcting Terra would only have suppressed the condition, and the condition is real and warranted; the harm that actually landed is the billing, not the verification verdict. |

**Semantic before after.** No semantic change is proposed. boarded_up_entry_or_window keeps kind 'defect', atomic_claim subject 'entry or window opening' with state 'opening boarded over' and ontology_basis 'asserted_failure', name 'Boarded-Up Entry or Window', its description, its embed_text, its five support_any phrases, its empty deny_any and its null require_any, all byte-identical. The two semantic defects found are recorded, not repaired: the subject over-commits relative to what the photo shows (unsupported_commitment_types ['object']), and the rendered claim_text 'entry or window opening opening boarded over' duplicates 'opening' because the subject string ends in the same word the state string begins with. Neither is repaired here because a one-unit cluster at bar 'none' may not author ops, and because the claim edit that would fix the first defect is affirmatively harmful.

**Retrieval effects.** None, by construction: embed_text, name, description, support_any and deny_any are all untouched, so no item's rank moves anywhere. This matters because embed_text, when non-empty, is the entire embedding input for an item and its effect cannot be verified offline - there is no offline embedding store - so any edit to it would be an unverifiable change on a cluster with zero controls. The retrieval facts recorded for the lead: the item ranked 1 on this exterior observation on its own merits, and it also ranks 1 to 6 on five unrelated interior observations at redfin_11000447 (stair header, three electrical, one flooring seam), none of which 2d resolved to it. Those near-misses are the regression surface any future proposal would have to protect, and none of them has been reviewed.

**Economics fit.** This is the finding. The condition is genuinely billable - human truth work 'warranted', Terra supported, accepted true, disposition accepted_for_work, reason_code route_work - so billability itself is correct and is not one of the mismatched dimensions. What is wrong is everything about HOW it bills. The item carries severity 4, scope 'replace', tier 'work', trade_bucket 'trim_doors_windows' and work_item_code DOOR_WINDOW_REPLACE, with an embed_text that states the intended repair as 'Replace and restore door or window assembly'. Applied to a nailed board panel over an attic-level gable opening, that prices a small exterior trim or vent repair as a door/window assembly replacement at the second-highest severity. package_affinity, pricing_status and cost_model are all null, so nothing downstream moderates it. Not one of these fields is authorable: severity, scope, trade_bucket and route_override are non-authorable on a carryover entry, and work_item_code, cost, estimate, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope and estimate_scope_reason are never authorable on any entry. That is why the correct action is a policy record rather than a catalog edit.

**Package route effects.** None from this finding, since nothing changes. For the record of what the defect does today: the item routes to tier 'work' with route 'work' and reason 'work_default', route_override is null, package_affinity is null and package_role is unset, so the condition enters the estimate as a standalone work item under DOOR_WINDOW_REPLACE in the trim_doors_windows trade rather than joining any exterior package. Setting route_override to 'no_action' - the only legal value - would be the natural way to make this a detect-but-do-not-bill presence condition, and it is exactly the field a carryover cannot author; it is also the wrong global answer, because a genuinely boarded-up door or window should keep billing. Recorded as part of the policy gap.

**Product policy.** No product-policy constraint applies to this item. trade_bucket is 'trim_doors_windows', which is not quarantined; the frozen billability record gives bills true, route 'work', reason 'work_default', with no quarantine flag. The electrical product quarantine is not implicated for this item, though it is worth noting that three of the five interior near-misses this item retrieves at redfin_11000447 are electrical observations that resolved to visible_electrical_risks - a further reason not to widen its subject, since a widened claim could pull quarantine-domain observations onto a billing trade.

**Expected benefit.** Nothing improves in the catalog, and that is the intended result. The benefit is that a genuine, previously unrecorded billing defect is written down with its owner correctly identified, together with the proof that no catalog lever can fix it. Concretely the record hands the lead three things: (1) an economics and work-item policy gap - a non-door, non-window opening resolving to boarded_up_entry_or_window is costed as a severity-4 door/window assembly replacement, and every corrective field is unauthorable by construction; (2) the absent gable/attic-opening concept, verified against all 128 items, together with the absence of any legitimate split parent that could house it, so the design question is stated rather than silently converted into an illegitimate split; (3) the reviewer's 'generic presence' note translated into what it would actually require. It also prevents a plausible-looking harm: the one native edit this cluster invites - widening the atomic_claim - would have made the billing defect worse and admitted five known interior near-misses.

**Regression risk.** Zero regression risk from the action itself, because no file changes and no op is authored. The risk this finding manages is the risk of the alternative. A claim widening would carry high risk and be untestable: the cluster has zero reviewed controls (0 positive_uses, 0 agreements, 0 correct_rejections, 0 hallucination annotations for this item), so there is no card against which a broadening could be regression-checked, while the frozen retrieval record already shows five interior observations at redfin_11000447 sitting inside this item's top-8, one of them at rank 1. A deny_any narrowing would carry the opposite risk: it would suppress a real, human-warranted condition that no other item in the catalog covers, converting a mis-billed condition into a lost one, which is the failure mode the programme weights most heavily. Leaving the catalog untouched preserves the capture and loses nothing.

**Gap description.** Two gaps, both recorded as non-catalog actions. GAP 1 - economics and work-item policy. When an observation whose true subject is neither a door nor a window resolves to boarded_up_entry_or_window, it is billed at severity 4, scope 'replace', trade trim_doors_windows, work_item_code DOOR_WINDOW_REPLACE, against an intended repair of 'Replace and restore door or window assembly'. The corrective fields are unauthorable by construction: severity, scope, trade_bucket and route_override are not settable on a carryover (decisions entry legacy_id boarded_up_entry_or_window, change_type 'unchanged', one successor at index 0, no siblings), and work_item_code, cost, estimate, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role and estimate_scope are never settable on any entry. Owner: work-item and pricing policy, not the migration decisions file. GAP 2 - absent concept with no legitimate split parent. No item among the 128 expresses a gable, attic or vent opening; the nearest neighbours (exhaust_fan_missing_or_broken, soffit_or_porch_ceiling_failed, soffit_or_porch_ceiling_weathered, exposed_sheathing_or_missing_siding) are all different subjects, and the frozen candidate list offered nothing closer. A new item can exist only as an extra successor of a legitimate split entry, and boarded_up_entry_or_window is a coherent single-claim carryover that provides no legitimate split. Owner: catalog design, pending a second reviewed property. Recorded alongside these, for completeness and without any proposed edit: the malformed claim_text 'entry or window opening opening boarded over' (duplicated 'opening'), the divergence-scan finding promised_not_claimed ['damage_break', 'door', 'sag_structural'], the item's all-seven scene_groups breadth, and Terra's affirmation of a subject its own rationale contradicts.

**Unresolved questions.** Whether the boarded panel closes a gable vent/louver opening or a covered attic window cannot be settled from photo_010.jpg. Its wide, short proportions and attic-level placement above the porch roofline favour a vent, and the reviewer records no sash within it. Either way it is neither an entry nor a habitable-room window, so the subject mismatch stands; but the correct severity and scope for the true subject remain unknown, which is part of why no severity or scope target can be named even in the policy record., The frozen attribution assigns this case to stage 2d with high confidence. Under the 2026-09-03 'right item unavailable versus right item reachable' rule I read it as unavailable, so I raise no 2d follow-up. This divergence is flagged for the lead; the attribution record and every adjudication are left untouched., Whether the reviewer's 'should be generic presence' should become a real catalog shape - a low-severity, low-scope presence condition for any boarded or covered opening - is a design question, not an evidence question. It needs an authorable severity/scope surface (which a carryover does not have), a work-item code that is not DOOR_WINDOW_REPLACE (never authorable), and at least a second reviewed property. It cannot be answered from one unit., boarded_up_entry_or_window lists all seven scene_groups. Whether that breadth is intentional was never adjudicated, and it is the reason the item reaches interior stair-header and electrical-cutout observations. scene_groups is unauthorable on a carryover, so this is recorded as an observation for the lead rather than as a proposal.

**Human disposition.** _blank_ (approved / approved_with_modification / rejected / deferred / reclassified_non_catalog)

### CAP-019 — Exterior door casing and jamb trim routed to a siding-fading item

Status `non_catalog_action` · outcome `non_catalog_action` · confidence `medium` · items `exterior_siding_discoloration_fading`, `exterior_door_paint_failure`

**Claim under test.** An exterior door casing / surround / jamb trim observation is routed to exterior_siding_discoloration_fading, whose atomic_claim commits to 'exterior siding finish', while exterior_door_paint_failure - reachable and kind-compatible - claims only 'exterior door paint' even though its description and embed_text already promise door frame, trim and moisture damage at the door base.

**Cluster.** subject: Painted exterior door casing / surround / jamb trim on the dwelling front - not the siding field, and not the door leaf itself (the reviewer records the door as sound).; mechanism: Thick, uneven, unfinished paint on the door surround with paint slopped onto the adjacent stone along the right jamb, patchy coverage down the jamb and at the door bottom, and a grey dirty streak at the sill. That is rough/unfinished finish work plus soiling, NOT fading, chalking or uneven discoloration, and it is on trim rather than on a siding field that does not exist on this house.; kinds: degradation; scenes: exterior; commitments: subject, mechanism. Split out of the provisional CAP-002, which pooled three physical subjects (painted siding fields, masonry, exterior door casing) under one abstract exterior-envelope claim and drew a two_independent_human bar from two units that share only the exterior scene. Convention 2 forbids one constituent rescuing another, so the door-casing unit and the item pair it turns on move here while the stone-masonry unit and brick_weathered_or_discolored move to CAP-018. This cluster is defined by ONE routing pattern, seen on both of its units: a Pass 2a observation about painted exterior TRIM is resolved by 2d to exterior_siding_discoloration_fading, a siding-field item, while exterior_door_paint_failure sits higher on the same frozen candidate list with kind_ok and scene_ok true. exterior_door_paint_failure is implicated (it is not a worklist item; divergence_scan in_worklist = false) because that item's atomic_claim is the other half of the mechanism under test - it is the candidate the routing passed over, and the only candidate lever anyone would reach for.

**Reshape.** from CAP-002 (narrower): Narrower than the provisional CAP-002 claim, which tested mechanism and material commitments across siding, trim and masonry on five units. This claim tests one commitment pair (subject and mechanism) on the two units where painted exterior trim was routed to a siding-field item. Both units keep their original CAP-002 review_row_id and their adjudications are unchanged: rc_7b23faa0bd8f stays supports_claim, rc_a35f1b3a231e stays refuted. Unit partition across the split is 5 = 1 (CAP-002) + 2 (CAP-018) + 2 (CAP-019); item partition gives exterior_siding_discoloration_fading to this cluster and adds exterior_door_paint_failure, so no worklist item is dropped or doubled.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions subject, mechanism, billability, route.

**Transferred units.** runtime:canary:redfin_166147710:20260817_224909_2f979a60:oc1_b2e96c2b1020d48b ← `CAP-002:runtime:canary:redfin_166147710:20260817_224909_2f979a60:oc1_b2e96c2b1020d48b`: Adjudication unchanged (refuted, human_review, independent, property redfin_166147710, case rc_a35f1b3a231e, photo_006.jpg and photo_019.jpg present). Role is deliberately kept as 'support' rather than 'counterexample': it was reviewed as a support row under CAP-002, it refutes this cluster's claim on its own facts, and keeping the role preserves mixed_signal = true so the conflict is forced into the open rather than hidden behind a relabelled role. It functions as the in-cluster negative control. Reviewer reason, verbatim: 'The white siding reads dingy with a grey cast, streaks and uneven tone, most clearly in the overcast photo, which is uneven discoloration, the mechanism the item names, so the cluster claim's second half does not hold.' **Diagnosis.** primary `pass_2d_selection`; contributing catalog_wording_or_specificity, overlapping_or_ambiguous_ontology. The catalog does not own the loss, and it does own two wording defects that this evidence cannot warrant fixing. Ownership of the loss: for issue 299f90fb6266878f ('White-painted trim appears unfinished in spots.') the frozen candidate list is soffit_or_porch_ceiling_weathered#1, exterior_door_paint_failure#2, baseboard_wear_scuffs#3, shed_exterior_paint_failure#4, exterior_siding_discoloration_fading#5, fence_weathered#6, brick_weathered_or_discolored#7. The reachability scan records exterior_door_paint_failure at rank 2 with kind_ok true and scene_ok true, and 2d (resolution_path 'llm', routing_reason 'exact_kind') selected rank 5 - a siding-field item, on a solid limestone rowhouse with no siding anywhere. Terra then said, correctly, that no siding fading was visible, and the condition was excluded (accepted false, reason_code verdict_unsupported) even though the frozen human truth grades it exact/warranted. The scope direction is explicit that 'right item reachable but another was selected' is a pass_2d_follow_ups entry with the catalog unchanged, and that a 2d selection failure must never be compensated by broadening the catalog. What the catalog does own, and what this outcome records rather than acts on: (a) exterior_siding_discoloration_fading's retrieval surface over-promises - its description opens 'Exterior siding OR TRIM shows fading, chalking, staining, or uneven discoloration' while its atomic_claim binds Terra to subject 'exterior siding finish' (divergence promised_not_claimed = ['trim_casing','wear_scuff']); under convention 1 the surface that failed is the retrieval surface, since that description is what 2d reads and it is a standing invitation to route trim here; (b) exterior_door_paint_failure's atomic_claim under-covers its own retrieval surface - description 'worn, chipped, or peeling around edges, panels, OR TRIM' and embed_text 'Moisture-damaged, swollen, or rotting wood at bottom of exterior door or DOOR FRAME. Deteriorated door sill or threshold.' against an atomic_claim of subject 'exterior door paint' (divergence promised_not_claimed = ['trim_casing','moisture_rot','damage_break']). Both are real, both are catalog-owned, and neither can be acted on at bar none - which is why this is a non_catalog_action that records them, not a proposal.

The reviewer confirms 'The Pass 2a observation ... matches the image.' There is no 2a error to be out of scope for, and no Pass 2a hallucination card exists on either implicated item. |

**Semantic before after.** No operation is proposed, so nothing changes. exterior_siding_discoloration_fading keeps atomic_claim {subject: 'exterior siding finish', state: 'fading, chalking, or uneven discoloration', ontology_basis: 'visible_deterioration'}, kind degradation, severity 1, scope cosmetic, trade exterior_siding_trim, deny_any ['rotted','missing siding','cracked siding'], change_type 'reclassified' with a single successor that keeps the parent id. exterior_door_paint_failure keeps atomic_claim {subject: 'exterior door paint', state: 'worn, chipped, or peeling finish', ontology_basis: 'visible_deterioration'}, kind degradation, severity 1, scope cosmetic, trade trim_doors_windows, deny_any ['shed'], change_type 'reclassified' with a single successor that keeps the parent id. For the record the lead has independently verified that setting exterior_door_paint_failure /successors/0/atomic_claim (with /atomicity_rationale and /expected_effects/routing in the same set) classifies native and dry-runs clean - that is expressibility only and is explicitly NOT a warrant; this cluster declines to author it.

**Retrieval effects.** None. No name, description, embed_text, support_any, deny_any or require_any is touched on any item, so no rank changes anywhere and no offline-unverifiable embedding edit is incurred. This matters because the most natural-looking fix - trimming the word 'trim' out of exterior_siding_discoloration_fading's description so it stops inviting trim observations - would not by itself move ranks (embed_text is set and is the entire embedding input, so description does not reach the embedder) but WOULD change what the 2d selection prompt reads, on an item with exactly one reviewed success and on the strength of one unit. Declining it is deliberate.

**Economics fit.** Untouched and unauthorable. cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope and estimate_scope_reason are never authorable in the decisions file for either item. Nothing was billed on the deciding case in any event: Terra returned unsupported, accepted false, disposition excluded, reason_code verdict_unsupported. Context only: both items are severity 1 / scope cosmetic / tier work and share work_item_code PAINT_EXTERIOR, so correcting the routing would move roughly the same dollars under a different trade bucket.

**Package route effects.** None under this outcome. route_override stays null on both items (its only legal value is no_action and neither item is a no_action candidate). exterior_siding_discoloration_fading keeps package_affinity {exterior: {package_role: package_support, package_type: exterior_repair}} and exterior_door_paint_failure keeps package_affinity null. Recorded as a consequence of the mis-routing rather than of any change: a trim observation correctly routed to exterior_door_paint_failure would no longer contribute support to an exterior_repair package, which is a real (if small) route difference and part of why the mismatch is classed operationally_consequential rather than equivalent.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. The implicated trades are exterior_siding_trim and trim_doors_windows; neither is the product-quarantined electrical trade, so non_catalog_action is not forced on policy grounds here - it is reached on the evidence and on the scope direction's 2d rule.

**Expected benefit.** No catalog benefit is claimed; this is a deliberate decision not to change the catalog. What the outcome delivers is three recorded artefacts. (1) Two pass_2d_follow_ups entries naming a reproducible selection pattern - painted exterior trim resolving to the rank-5 siding-field item while exterior_door_paint_failure sits at rank 1 or 2 with kind_ok and scene_ok true - on two properties, two lanes, with the cost shown to depend on whether the property happens to have siding. (2) Two catalog wording observations in mirror image: exterior_siding_discoloration_fading advertises trim on the surface 2d reads and excludes it on the surface Terra reads, and exterior_door_paint_failure promises door frame, trim and door-base moisture on its retrieval surfaces while claiming only door paint. (3) An explicit statement that the obvious lever cannot be authored on this evidence, on two independent grounds - the bar is none, and exterior_door_paint_failure has no reviewed control family at all (1 label, 0 attribution cases, 0 controls, 0 regression-register rows), so a regression check on it would be vacuous.

**Regression risk.** Zero by construction - no bytes change and no control can move. The risks this outcome avoids are the substantive point. Widening exterior_door_paint_failure's claim to cover casings would put rc_91bb03de4429 ('The white door jamb casing is intact with only grime and small paint chips at its base', adjudicated refuted) at plausible risk of flipping to supported, with no reviewed success family on that item against which to detect the damage. Narrowing exterior_siding_discoloration_fading to exclude trim would be worse: this cluster's own refuted unit shows that item working correctly on a house with siding, and its single reviewed success rc_0b6ca8cfa337 is a pure siding-field case that any narrowing would have to preserve. Both levers would also be a catalog change made to compensate for a 2d selection failure, which the scope direction forbids outright.

**Unresolved questions.** The choice between this non_catalog_action and deferred_insufficient_evidence is the one soft call in the file, and the lead should know exactly what it costs. That is one overlapping-ontology finding, and the lead may prefer to record it once at family level rather than repeat it in CAP-002 and CAP-019. A selector that skips its own top candidates to reach a rank-5 siding item on a trim observation looks like a systematic pull toward the broadest exterior finish concept, which would be worth measuring across the corpus rather than inferring from two cases., Recorded disagreement with a frozen record, changing nothing: the frozen attribution for rc_7b23faa0bd8f is stage 'terra' at medium confidence, and it exonerates 2d on the premise that 'the v1.1 truth basis grades the resulting claim axis as exact'. Steven's convention-6 ruling (the subject is door casing/surround or jamb trim, not siding, do not relabel) is inconsistent with that premise. I treat the adjudication as fact and the attribution as reasoning, and name 2d as primary cause. The lead may wish to flag the v1.1 claim=exact axis on this case as superseded by the reconciliation ruling, since other analyses may still be leaning on it.

### CAP-021 — Shower valve trim resolved to an interior millwork item while the exact bathroom item ranked first

Status `non_catalog_action` · outcome `non_catalog_action` · confidence `high` · items `outdated_bathroom_finishes`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows a bathroom plumbing-fixture component (shower valve escutcheon and tub spout, tarnished and rust-streaked) resolved to an interior-millwork catalog item, while a materially more exact bathroom item with the same kind and scene was reachable at rank 1 on the same issue.

**Cluster.** subject: bathroom plumbing-fixture trim on redfin_11079485 photo_005.jpg: the shower valve escutcheon and lever handle, the tub spout, and the bare shower arm above the tub surround; mechanism: dull, tarnished and rust-streaked plumbing trim on an old bathroom (rust drips run down the tile below the spout, the shower arm is corroded at the wall), described at 2a as 'The shower trim is old.' and resolved to dated_interior_trim, a trim_doors_windows millwork item whose claim is about the grade of the baseboard/casing package, not about plumbing hardware; kinds: modernization; scenes: bathroom; commitments: object, mechanism. Split out of CAP-006 as a one-unit cluster because the failure mechanism is categorically different from the rest of that cluster. Every other CAP-006 unit is a bathroom_fixtures_tile component resolved to a sibling bathroom_fixtures_tile item (surround vs fixtures, vanity finish vs vanity damage) - wrong component inside one trade family, where the candidate levers are sibling-boundary wording and kind gates. This unit is a plumbing-fixture subject resolved across trade families into trim_doors_windows millwork on a shared head-word ('trim'), where the exact bathroom item was already ranked first and the lever, if any, would be a retrieval guardrail on a different item entirely. Pooling the two mechanisms hid the fact that this unit needs no catalog change at all and was also the only reason CAP-006 implicated dated_interior_trim, an item CAP-008 owns and analyses on its own evidence. The split therefore both isolates a distinct mechanism and removes a double-implication; it costs CAP-006 nothing, because that cluster's two_independent_human bar is carried by redfin_125779232 and redfin_11000447, neither of which moves.

**Reshape.** from CAP-006 (narrower): CAP-006's claim is 'a bathroom component ... whose component or mechanism differs from what the resolved catalog item claims'. This claim is strictly narrower: it adds that the resolved item lies in a different trade family from the subject (plumbing/bath fixtures -> interior millwork) AND that a materially more exact same-kind, same-scene item was reachable at rank 1 on the very same issue. Every unit satisfying the narrower claim satisfies the broader one, so the reviewer's adjudication transfers without reinterpretation; the reviewer's own reason and lineage note assert both added conjuncts verbatim. Controls are read from CAP-006.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions subject, mechanism, severity, repair, route, billability.

**Diagnosis.** primary `pass_2d_selection`; contributing overlapping_or_ambiguous_ontology. The catalog does NOT own this failure, and the argument is a reachability argument rather than a wording argument. On issue 7569c5ab9b09e455 the frozen candidate list was outdated_bathroom_finishes (1), dated_overall_decor_style (2), dated_interior_trim (3), dated_bathroom_flooring_style (4), dated_electrical_outlets_switches (5), dated_bathroom_vanity_light (6), dated_window_treatment_valance (7), exhaust_fan_dated (8). The item the reviewer says was correct was ranked FIRST, was kind-compatible (modernization/modernization) and scene-compatible (bathroom), and 2d nevertheless selected rank 3 through the llm path (routing_reason exact_kind, resolution_path llm, no lexical shortcut fired). Retrieval did its job; selection did not. The 2026-09-03 scope direction is explicit that this is the 'right item reachable but another was selected' case, which is a pass_2d_follow_ups entry with the catalog left alone, and Steven's reconciled ruling on rc_3a19f759f2d9 (deck weathering: the right concept was reachable, therefore a 2d specificity question, not missing coverage) is the governing precedent for exactly this shape. The contributing ontological fact is real but is not the owner: dated_interior_trim carries the bare token 'trim' in support_any and lists 'bathroom' among its scene_groups, so an item about millwork is eligible in bathroom scenes on a head-word that in the trades also names valve hardware ('shower valve trim'). That cross-family homograph is what put a millwork item into a plumbing candidate list at all. But it only reached rank 3, below the right answer, so it cost nothing on its own - the loss happened at the selection step that had the right answer in front of it. Narrowing that item's retrieval surface would also be a change to CAP-008's item made on this cluster's single unit, which is precisely the 'compensate for a 2d selection failure by editing the catalog' move the brief forbids. Finally, the catalog surface that Terra saw behaved correctly: Terra was handed dated_interior_trim's atomic_claim and rejected it with an accurate rationale ('The visible shower hardware does not clearly show a dated or builder-grade interior trim package'). No catalog field misled any stage.

Its retrieval-side divergence (divergence scan promised_not_claimed ['dated','door','window']) is a real defect, but it is CAP-008's fact on CAP-008's evidence, and it is not what lost this condition. |

| downstream_tier_projection_routing_package_costing | Rejected as owner. Nothing downstream ran on this issue: Terra returned unsupported, the condition was disposed 'excluded' with reason_code verdict_unsupported, so projection, tiering, routing and packaging never saw it. Downstream is however where the CONSEQUENCE would have landed, which is why the mismatch is classified consequential (see alters_eligibility_package_economics). |
| product_policy_or_quarantine | Rejected. No quarantined trade is involved. The subject's trade is plumbing / bathroom_fixtures_tile, the resolved item's is trim_doors_windows; the electrical product quarantine touches none of them, so nothing forces non_catalog_action on policy grounds. The outcome is non_catalog_action on the merits of the 2d follow-up, not by policy. |

**Semantic before after.** Not applicable: no ops, outcome non_catalog_action, so every item keeps its current semantics verbatim and no dry run was run. Recorded so the lead can see exactly what is being left alone. outdated_bathroom_finishes stays kind modernization / ontology_basis dated_style with atomic_claim subject 'bathroom finishes', state 'dated tile, vanity, or fixtures', severity 3, tier work, scope replace, scene_groups ['bathroom']. dated_interior_trim (not implicated here; CAP-008's item) stays kind modernization / ontology_basis low_grade_material with atomic_claim subject 'interior trim', state 'plain, thin, or builder-grade trim package', severity 1, tier optional, scene_groups ['kitchen','bathroom','bedroom','living_areas','utility']. plumbing_fixture_stained_or_corroded stays kind degradation / ontology_basis surface_rust with atomic_claim subject 'plumbing fixture', state 'staining, corrosion, or buildup without leak'.

**Retrieval effects.** None: nothing is edited, so no embed_text, support_any, deny_any, name or description changes and no item's rank moves anywhere. This is load-bearing rather than incidental. The correct item already ranks first on this issue, so any retrieval edit could only move a ranking that is already right, and embed_text edits in particular cannot be verified offline (embed_text is the entire embedding input when set and there is no offline embedding store). Leaving retrieval untouched also protects the seven reviewed dated_interior_trim billings and rejections in the CAP-008 family, which a 'trim'-token or bathroom-scene narrowing would have disturbed.

**Economics fit.** Not applicable: no ops, and economics fields (cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope) are never natively authorable on any surface in any case. Recorded as context: outdated_bathroom_finishes carries work_item_code FIXTURES_UPDATE with cost_model room_allowance and pricing_status null; dated_interior_trim carries TRIM_REPLACE with cost_model null.

**Package route effects.** None, because nothing changes. The package facts are nevertheless the reason this mismatch is classified consequential and are recorded here for the 2d follow-up's priority: outdated_bathroom_finishes is package_driver for bathroom_modernization in the bathroom scene; dated_interior_trim is package_support for the same package; bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn, the item that did bill on this photo, is package_support for the different package bathroom_repair. Neither item carries a route_override, and billability.json gives route 'work' for both by work_default, though tier differs (work vs optional). Whether this property's bathroom_modernization package actually went unformed is recorded as an unresolved question.

**Product policy.** Not applicable: no ops and no policy trigger. The trades in play are plumbing, bathroom_fixtures_tile and trim_doors_windows; none is the product-quarantined electrical trade, so nothing forces the outcome on policy grounds. No item here carries a min_photo_evidence or route_override concern, so no policy gap is being masked either.

**Expected benefit.** No catalog benefit is claimed, because no catalog change is made - and that is the affirmative finding, not an absence of one. The benefit delivered is threefold and entirely non-catalog. (1) A precisely specified pass_2d_follow_ups entry with the diagnostic signal a selection fix could use (observation-trade versus selected-item trade_bucket, with a same-kind same-scene candidate of the observation's own trade ranked above). (2) A documented refusal: two plausible catalog levers on dated_interior_trim are shown to be either forbidden (compensating for a 2d selection failure), damaging to reviewed successes, or not natively authorable, so the next analyst need not re-litigate them. (3) The split itself, which gives CAP-008 sole ownership of dated_interior_trim and leaves CAP-006 a homogeneous within-family cluster, with no worklist item losing an owner.

**Regression risk.** Zero regression risk by construction: no file changes, no ops, no dry run needed, the guarded catalog artifacts are untouched and every reviewed billing and rejection in the family behaves exactly as it does today. The residual risk is the opposite one and worth naming: leaving the catalog alone leaves the cross-family 'trim' homograph in place, so a future bathroom photo whose plumbing-trim observation is classified modernization can be mis-selected the same way. That risk is accepted deliberately - it is a selection risk owned by 2d, the correct item ranks first when it occurs, and the reviewed evidence shows the condition's degradation reading still bills through bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn. It is carried in the follow-up entry and in the CAP-008 note rather than mitigated by a catalog edit at bar none.

**Post-catalog Pass 2d follow-ups.**

The selector took the millwork sense inside a bathroom scene whose candidate list was otherwise entirely bathroom-modernization items. The selector passed over the rank-1 candidate, which was kind_ok true and scene_ok true, for the rank-3 candidate, on an issue where the rank-1 item's own embed_text names 'dated faucet or hardware'. Terra then correctly rejected the substituted claim and the condition was excluded (reason_code verdict_unsupported), so a real dated-fixtures observation went unbilled. Consequence is not cosmetic: outdated_bathroom_finishes is severity 3, tier work, and the package_DRIVER for bathroom_modernization, whereas dated_interior_trim is severity 1, tier optional and only package_support. The catalog is deliberately unchanged; do not implement this as a catalog guardrail. Frozen attribution independently agrees (stage 2d, downstream, medium confidence). |

If no other condition on this property supplied a bathroom_modernization driver, the loss is economically material rather than cosmetic and the 2d follow-up deserves priority. The digest carries no realised package set, so I could not verify this and have recorded it as a question rather than a claim. That is a real cross-family ambiguity and it is the reason a millwork item was in this candidate list at all. I do not propose acting on it: the item belongs to CAP-008, the evidence here is one unit at bar none, the right item still ranked first, and both candidate levers are bad (deny_any is a global hard guardrail; scene_groups is not natively authorable on a reclassified carryover and would be a migration-system gap). If CAP-008 ends up editing that item's surfaces for its own reasons, this is a fact worth having on the table there., outdated_bathroom_finishes carries in_worklist=false in the divergence scan, so this cluster implicates a non-worklist item. If the lead requires every cluster to implicate a worklist item, the only alternative would be to implicate dated_interior_trim here, which would re-create the double-implication with CAP-008 that this split exists to remove. It is the same photo as the unit - redfin_11079485 photo_005.jpg, a sibling condition on the same image. ## Migration-system gaps

### CAP-013 — Broad 'unfinished wall' retrieval promise on an item whose claim requires exposed OSB

Status `migration_system_gap` · outcome `migration_system_gap` · confidence `medium` · items `unfinished_interior_wall_osb_exposed`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows an interior wall area that is unfinished, patchy, rough, or incomplete at the base or corner, while no OSB or wall sheathing is exposed, so the catalog claim's material commitment (exposed OSB) is not what is visible.

**Cluster.** subject: Interior wall surface at the base and at a closet return corner: the wall-to-floor edge where base trim belongs, and a patched stretch of wall face above a baseboard.; mechanism: Branch A (gold redfin_11000447 photo_006): no baseboard along the whole left wall run, with a raw, ragged, unpainted wall-to-floor edge and a loose board on the floor. Branch B (runtime redfin_10952874 photo_033): bare unpainted patch compound and scraped/torn paint from mid-height down to a chipped baseboard corner on a closet return wall. In both photos every wall surface is finished drywall/plaster and no OSB, plywood, or sheathing is exposed anywhere, including the dark closet interior.; kinds: defect; scenes: bedroom, living_areas; commitments: object, material. POOLED, no split, no reshape. I re-tested the structure step's pooling and confirmed it. One item-level mechanism holds both units: unfinished_interior_wall_osb_exposed's retrieval surface promises a generic unfinished/incomplete wall (name 'Unfinished Interior Wall (OSB Exposed)'; description 'indicates an unfinished or incomplete wall assembly'; embed_text 'Interior wall not finished'; support_any 'unfinished wall', 'exposed wall'; divergence scan promised_not_claimed = ['missing_incomplete','trim_casing']) and took rank 1 on BOTH bullets ('The trim is incomplete.' and 'An area near the closet corner is unfinished and patchy.'), after which its narrow atomic_claim ('interior wall: exposed OSB/sheathing on an unfinished assembly') caused Terra to correctly reject both. Splitting would leave two one-unit clusters at bar none and destroy the only two_independent_human bar in this family. The two branches do have different first-wrong-stages downstream of that shared attractor, and that is handled inside the judgment (branch B is routed to pass_2d_follow_ups and is explicitly excluded from the coverage-gap claim) rather than by splitting the cluster.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions subject, mechanism, severity, repair, route.

| unit | class | role | property | photos | adjudication | review row | Terra | human truth |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| gold:redfin_11000447:photo_006.jpg:g5 | gold_reference | support | redfin_11000447 | photo_006.jpg | supports_claim | CAP-013:gold:redfin_11000447:photo_006.jpg:g5 | — | miss_candidate |
| gold:redfin_11000447:photo_008.jpg:g10 | gold_reference | support | redfin_11000447 | photo_008.jpg | supports_claim | coverage:gold:redfin_11000447:photo_008.jpg:g10 | — | miss_candidate |
| runtime:canary:redfin_10952874:20260818_231243_cb71fce9:oc1_75f700ab7c3ae0fd | human_review | support | redfin_10952874 | photo_033.jpg | supports_claim | CAP-013:runtime:canary:redfin_10952874:20260818_231243_cb71fce9:oc1_75f700ab7c3ae0fd | unsupported | — |

**Transferred units.** gold:redfin_11000447:photo_008.jpg:g10 ← `coverage:gold:redfin_11000447:photo_008.jpg:g10`: Promoted by the lead from the coverage lane, where its own group triaged it to this cluster. Reviewed under its pinned coverage row: 'The stone-pattern floor meets bare green painted wall with no base or cove trim anywhere along the left wall or either face of the partition' (supports_claim). It is the same missing-base-trim concept the cluster tests, on the same property as the cluster's gold unit, so it corroborates without adding a second property.

**Evidence bar.** recomputed `two_independent_human` (claimed `two_independent_human`): 3 human/gold, 0 lead, 2 properties, 2 correlated, corroboration `none`, contextual `{}`; reviewed refutations 0 (lead 0), mixed signal `False`. Recounted by hand from the CAP-013 digest and cross-checked against digest/index.json. Two units, both role 'support', both adjudicated exactly 'supports_claim', zero refuted, zero unclear. Unit 1 gold:redfin_11000447:photo_006.jpg:g5 is evidence_class gold_reference with independent=true. Unit 2 runtime:canary:redfin_10952874:20260818_231243_cb71fce9:oc1_75f700ab7c3ae0fd is evidence_class human_review with independent=true. Both classes count as human under the ladder, giving human=2, so the ladder returns two_independent_human. leads=0: there are no model_judge units in this cluster at all, so nothing here rests on a factorized-verifier lead and the 'a lead never grounds a non-deferred outcome' rule is not engaged. Properties are distinct (redfin_11000447 and redfin_10952874), photos are distinct (photo_006.jpg and photo_033.jpg), and the two units come from different run ids and different evidence pathways (a gold-reference photo audit versus a runtime miss case), so there is no shared-method or shared-subject correlation to discount. The digest's own recomputation agrees exactly: bar_met_by 'two_independent_human', human_units 2, lead_units 0, independent_support 2, distinct_properties 2, correlated_support 0, reviewed_refutations 0, mixed_signal false. This is the cleanest bar in the trim-bath-kitchen family. The met bar is what makes the declared coverage gap eligible for a non-deferred outcome, and it also satisfies the 'at least one reviewed human/gold unit' requirement twice over. Correlation: Not required (2 distinct properties, correlated_support 0), supplied for transparency about what the bar does and does not carry. The two-unit bar establishes the POOLED cluster claim - that the OSB item's retrieval promise outruns its claim and lost a real condition on two independent properties. The coverage-gap sub-claim, which is what drives the migration_system_gap outcome, rests on branch A alone (gold:redfin_11000447), because branch B has a reachable correct item and is deliberately excluded from the gap. A reviewer should read the gap as one-property evidence of a structural hole that a 128-item catalog search and two independent reviewer notes corroborate, not as two-property evidence of the hole itself. Conflict: Not applicable and none required: mixed_signal is false. Both human/gold units adjudicate supports_claim; there is no human or gold refutation and no unclear verdict anywhere in this cluster (adjudication_counts = {supports_claim: 2}). The only 'rejections' in the record are Terra's, which both reviewers judged correct and which are the substance of the support units rather than a conflicting signal.

**Human inputs.**

| ref | source | ruling | effect |

| convention_1 | PHASE_C_BRIEF.md section 2 item 1 (Steven's ruling) | Strictest claim-bearing wording governs semantic fit; Terra sees only the atomic_claim while 2d and retrieval see name/description/embed_text/support_any, and a mismatch between those two surfaces is a real catalog defect where it matters which surface failed. | Fixed the diagnosis on the catalog's retrieval surface rather than on Terra. It is why both Terra rejections are treated as correct behaviour and why the item's promised_not_claimed divergence is recorded as a genuine defect even though I propose no op against it. |
| convention_2 | PHASE_C_BRIEF.md section 2 item 2 (Steven's ruling) | Judge at the characteristic issue level and never multiply units: several issues on one condition are one evidence unit. | Branch A's three trim-related bullets on photo_006 ('The trim is incomplete.', 'The wood header or trim area at the stair opening is exposed.', 'The ceiling edge or header area has a rough finish.') count as ONE unit, not three. This keeps the bar honest at two rather than inflating it to four. |
| convention_3 | PHASE_C_BRIEF.md section 2 item 3 (Steven's ruling) | Plain/basic/builder-grade presence is not billable by itself; a condition needs visible datedness, deterioration, INCOMPLETENESS, or another concrete modernization need. | Decisive for the gap being a real loss rather than a harmless one. Missing base trim with a raw wall-to-floor edge is incompleteness with a concrete remedy (install and finish base trim), so the condition the catalog cannot express is one Steven has ruled billable. |
| rc_3a19f759f2d9 | PHASE_C_BRIEF.md section 2 item 6, reconciled conflict case (deck weathering) | Where the right existing concept was reachable, treat the case as a 2d specificity question, not as missing coverage. | Governing precedent for excluding branch B from the coverage gap. damaged_drywall_or_cracks was reachable at rank 3 with kind_ok and scene_ok, so branch B becomes a pass_2d_follow_ups entry and contributes nothing to the gap claim. |
| scope_direction_20260903 | PHASE_C_BRIEF.md section 3 (2026-09-03 priority filter) | Never compensate for a 2d selection failure by broadening the catalog; Pass 2a is frozen; catalog-first means missing coverage and consequential semantic defects are the primary candidates. | Rejected outright the lever of widening the OSB item's atomic_claim to cover any unfinished or incomplete wall - it would compensate branch B's 2d failure and broaden a severity-3 FRAMING_DRYWALL_FINISH defect over cosmetic patchiness and missing trim. It also confirms that missing coverage is an in-scope primary finding rather than something to document and drop. |

**Diagnosis.** primary `missing_catalog_coverage`; contributing catalog_wording_or_specificity, retrieval_metadata_or_reachability, pass_2d_selection. The catalog owns this on two counts, and only the second is fixable within the authoring surface. (a) COVERAGE (branch A, the outcome-bearing count): a real, warranted, concretely repairable condition - base trim absent along an entire wall run with a raw wall-to-floor edge - has no defect-kind item in the catalog to land on. Steven's convention 3 names incompleteness explicitly as a qualifying concrete need, so this is a billable loss and not a harmless naming difference. Every stage upstream did its job: 2a captured it ('trim is minimal or incomplete'), 2b rendered it faithfully ('The trim is incomplete.'), 2c kept it and classified it as a defect (correct: missing_required_component), and 2d picked the best of six items none of which was right. When every stage behaves and the condition is still lost, the catalog is the owner. (b) WORDING (shared, both branches): unfinished_interior_wall_osb_exposed's retrieval and selection surfaces promise a generic unfinished/incomplete wall while its Terra-facing atomic_claim commits to a material (OSB/sheathing) and to an unfinished wall assembly. This is exactly the surface split named in convention 1, and the divergence scan records it as promised_not_claimed = ['missing_incomplete','trim_casing']. It is the mechanism that pulled both bullets to rank 1 and then guaranteed their rejection. I decline to propose the narrowing op for it and give the reasons under alternative_stage_analysis and unresolved_questions; that abstention is a verifiability judgment, not a finding that the divergence is acceptable.

| rejected owner | why |
|---|---|
| terra_verification | Terra was correct on both units and both reviewers say so. Branch A rationale: 'The visible interior walls are finished surfaces, with no exposed OSB or sheathing.' Branch B rationale: 'No exposed OSB or wall sheathing is visible.' The Phase B reviewer independently confirmed each against the photo ('the wall surface is finished painted drywall/plaster with no OSB or sheathing exposed'; 'no OSB or sheathing is exposed anywhere'). Terra rejected exactly what it was handed and would have been wrong to accept it. |
| upstream_pass_2a_observation | Out of scope by the 2026-09-03 direction, and factually clean here. Both attribution rationales quote the 2a prose as accurate: branch A's 2a said 'trim is minimal or incomplete'; branch B's 2a listed under 'Wall/baseboard repairs needed' the phrase 'an obvious unfinished/patchy area near the closet corner' and prescribed 'patching, caulk, and full repaint'. Neither 2a text mentions OSB, sheathing, or a wall assembly. There is no Pass 2a error to defer. |
| upstream_kind_or_pass_2c | 2c's defect classification is correct on both bullets under the decisions-file policy ('asserted failures stay defect'; a missing required component is a defect). The defect classification is consequence-bearing - it is what removed the degradation-kind baseboard_wear_scuffs from branch A's candidate pool - but it is not an error, and re-classifying missing trim as degradation to make an existing item reachable would be corrupting the ontology to paper over the coverage hole. |
| pass_2d_selection | Correct for branch B and rejected as PRIMARY for the cluster. Branch B is a genuine 2d selection failure (damaged_drywall_or_cracks was reachable at rank 3, kind_ok and scene_ok) and is recorded as a pass_2d_follow_ups entry with the catalog left unchanged, following Steven's rc_3a19f759f2d9 precedent. **Semantic before after.** No change proposed; the catalog is left exactly as it is. Stated for the record so the gap is actionable if the authoring surface is extended: the shape the evidence warrants is a DEFECT-kind item with subject 'interior base trim', state 'missing or incompletely installed base trim leaving a raw wall-to-floor edge', ontology_basis missing_required_component, trade_bucket trim_doors_windows, work_item_code TRIM_REPAIR, scene_groups covering interior scenes, sitting between baseboard_wear_scuffs (degradation, cosmetic wear on installed trim) and bare_or_missing_finish_flooring (defect, missing_required_component on the floor). It must be defect-kind or it stays unreachable from the bullets that describe it. In parallel, unfinished_interior_wall_osb_exposed's retrieval surface should stop advertising a generic unfinished/incomplete wall, but that op is deliberately not proposed here (see unresolved_questions).

**Retrieval effects.** None, since no op is proposed. The retrieval facts that would matter to any future op: unfinished_interior_wall_osb_exposed's embed_text is non-empty and is therefore the entire embedding input for the item, so any edit to it changes the item's rank on every bullet corpus-wide and cannot be verified offline (there is no offline embedding store). support_any is only a soft ranking hint and the annotator search index, and both resolutions in this cluster took the llm path with shortcut_reason null, so the support_any tokens 'unfinished wall' and 'exposed wall' are not the demonstrated attractor. Retrieval pools are kind-homogeneous and gated per bullet, which is why a defect-kind bullet can never reach the degradation-kind baseboard_wear_scuffs.

**Economics fit.** Not engaged - no op. The economics are nonetheless the wall that closes the gap road: cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role and estimate_scope are never authorable in the decisions file for either a carryover or a split successor, and split_successor_pricing policy 'inherit_parent_economics_v1' makes any successor inherit its parent's economics byte-identically. A missing-base-trim defect minted under baseboard_wear_scuffs would arrive priced as a severity-1 cosmetic wear item with no authorable path to install-and-finish economics.

**Package route effects.** None, since no op is proposed. Recorded as the measured cost of the status quo: the OSB item carries package_affinity null, while damaged_drywall_or_cracks is a package_driver for bedroom_repair and living_repair and baseboard_wear_scuffs is a package_support for bedroom, living, kitchen and bathroom modernization with repair_support_when_driven true. Both mis-resolutions therefore removed their observations from package formation as well as from the line items, and routed TRIM_REPAIR-class and DRYWALL_PATCH-class work onto FRAMING_DRYWALL_FINISH at severity 3.

**Product policy.** No product-quarantine interaction. The implicated item's trade_bucket is paint_drywall and the true homes are paint_drywall and trim_doors_windows; none is the quarantined electrical trade, so nothing here is forced to non_catalog_action on product-policy grounds. (visible_electrical_risks appears in both frozen candidate pools but is not implicated and did not take either resolution.)

**Expected benefit.** None claimed from this judgment, because it proposes no change. The benefit of the recorded gap, if the authoring surface is extended, is the recovery of interior base-trim incompleteness as a billable defect - currently a guaranteed loss on every property where it appears, because the bullets that describe it are routed into a claim (exposed OSB) that Terra will always and correctly reject. The measurable target is fewer lost real conditions; there is no hallucination-reduction claim, and no dollar claim is made.

**Regression risk.** None from this judgment. The risk this judgment AVOIDS is the substantive point: every candidate lever carried an unbounded or wrong-direction risk. Widening the OSB claim would broaden a severity-3 FRAMING_DRYWALL_FINISH defect over cosmetic patchiness and missing trim and would compensate a 2d selection failure. Widening baseboard_wear_scuffs's claim would not recover branch A (kind gate) and would push a severity-1 defaultHidden cosmetic item toward missing-component territory on another cluster's item. Narrowing the OSB item's embed_text would change its rank on every bullet in the corpus with zero reviewed uses and zero correct rejections available to detect the damage. With no reviewed control of any kind in this cluster, no proposal could have been shown safe.

**Gap description.** MIGRATION-SYSTEM GAP: the catalog needs a defect-kind item claiming missing or incompletely installed interior base trim, and the kind_v2_decisions authoring surface cannot create one. Three independent constraints close every road. (1) No standalone add or merge exists; a genuinely new item can only appear as an extra successor of a legitimate split entry. (2) No legitimate split parent exists. A split entry separates a legacy item whose ATOMIC CLAIM conflates two mechanisms with different evidence and remediation - the pattern of the damaged_soffit_or_porch_ceiling entry, which separated asserted_failure from visible_deterioration. Both candidate parents here have a single, already-atomic claim and merely over-broad retrieval text: baseboard_wear_scuffs claims wear only while its embed_text advertises 'Missing baseboards, detached trim, visible gaps at wall base, incomplete base trim installation', and unfinished_interior_wall_osb_exposed claims exposed OSB while its retrieval surface advertises a generic unfinished/incomplete wall. The remedy for an over-broad retrieval text is to narrow the retrieval text; manufacturing a split to mint a successor would be a standalone add wearing a split's clothes. (3) Even a manufactured split fails on its own terms: a split successor may not keep the parent's id, so baseboard_wear_scuffs would be deprecated with requires_re_resolution across the corpus while carrying five human-approved billings owned by CAP-001; and split_successor_pricing policy 'inherit_parent_economics_v1' with economics never authorable would deliver the new item priced as a severity-1 cosmetic TRIM_REPAIR wear condition, unable to bill an install. Separately, the kind gate rules out the cheap alternative of routing the concept to an existing item: 2d pools are kind-homogeneous per bullet, 'The trim is incomplete.' drew an all-defect pool, and the only interior trim items are degradation (baseboard_wear_scuffs) and modernization (dated_interior_trim), so no claim rewrite on an existing trim item can make it reachable from these bullets. What the gap needs from the migration system is either the ability to author a genuinely new item outside a split, or the ability to author economics for a new successor; either one alone would unblock this.

The op would be native (name/description/embed_text/support_any via overrides on an unchanged carryover) but I decline to propose it for three reasons that a live run could resolve: both observed resolutions took the llm path with shortcut_reason null so support_any is not the demonstrated attractor; embed_text is the entire embedding input with no offline store, so the intended rank change is unverifiable before a live run; and the item has zero reviewed uses, so no control can bound the corpus-wide blast radius. That op should be judged on CAP-001's evidence, and the lead must NOT credit it with recovering anything in CAP-013: branch A's bullet sat in a kind-homogeneous defect pool and baseboard_wear_scuffs is kind degradation, so it was unreachable regardless of what its claim says. Widening a claim does not change a kind., damaged_drywall_or_cracks also carries promised_not_claimed ['missing_incomplete'] - its embed_text advertises 'missing sections' while its claim is 'cracks, holes, or impact damage'. I am not declaring that a defect (holes arguably covers missing sections) but it is the same pattern on the item this cluster routes branch B toward, and the lead may want it noted alongside CAP-001's.

## Appendix A — no-change clusters

### CAP-002 — Damaged or rotted exterior siding and trim: mechanism confirmed, subject was an outbuilding

Status `no_change` · outcome `no_change` · confidence `high` · items `damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows exterior siding or trim with rot, warping, cracks or missing sections, and the catalog claim of damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim names a mechanism or subject that is not what is visible.

**Cluster.** subject: exterior wall cladding and trim on a detached metal storage shed (the dwelling itself is not implicated by this unit); mechanism: creased and dented corrugated panels, a missing door, a detached trim board on the slab, bent door-opening trim and rust streaking - i.e. warping and missing sections; kinds: defect; scenes: exterior; commitments: mechanism, subject. Narrowed in place from the provisional CAP-002, which pooled three physical subjects (painted siding, masonry, exterior door casing) under one abstract exterior-envelope claim. The pooled cluster's two_independent_human bar was carried by exactly two units - a stone-masonry material mismatch (redfin_80925528) and an exterior door casing routed to a siding item (redfin_10740044) - that share nothing but the exterior scene: different subject, different mechanism, different commitment type, different trade (masonry_exterior_structure vs exterior_siding_trim vs trim_doors_windows) and different candidate levers. Convention 2 forbids one such constituent rescuing another, so those two units and their items move out to CAP-018 (brick_weathered_or_discolored) and CAP-019 (exterior_siding_discoloration_fading, exterior_door_paint_failure). The split deliberately costs this cluster its bar; that bar was an artifact of pooling, not evidence. What remains is a single-item, single-subject cluster: damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim against outbuilding cladding damage.

**Reshape.** from CAP-002 (narrower): Same proposal id, narrowed claim. The provisional claim tested two commitment types (mechanism and material) across siding, trim and masonry; this claim tests only the siding/trim mechanism-and-subject commitment on the one unit that remains. Every moved unit keeps its original CAP-002 review_row_id, and its adjudication is unchanged. 5 provisional units = 1 (CAP-002) + 2 (CAP-018) + 2 (CAP-019); no unit is duplicated or dropped, and no worklist item loses a cluster.
