**Diagnosis.** primary `terra_verification`; contributing catalog_wording_or_specificity. The catalog does not own this unit and there is no catalog lever to pull. The claim under test is REFUTED by the only reviewed unit: damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim's atomic_claim ('exterior siding or trim - rot, warping, cracks, or missing sections') names exactly what the photo shows on the shed - bent and creased panels (warping), a missing door and a detached trim board (missing sections). Under convention 1, the claim-bearing wording governs semantic fit, and that wording is unqualified as to whether the cladding is on the dwelling or on an outbuilding, so the shed satisfies the subject term as written. The frozen v1 truth agrees (terra_claim_supported), the lineage is clean at every stage (2a prose stated 'damaged siding and trim'; 2b joined exact; 2c kept; 2d resolved rank 1 by support_phrase_hit; 2e kept; projection produced oc1_1650c027b1d144a2), and Terra then returned 'unsupported' on the rationale that the siding and trim 'do not show clear rot, warping, cracks, or missing sections' - a verdict the image contradicts. The only catalog observation worth recording is a surface divergence that this unit does not turn on: the item's embed_text advertises 'Rotted door bottom, water damage at door base, deteriorated door sill or frame, door frame rot' while its atomic_claim excludes doors (divergence_scan promised_not_claimed = ['door']). That is a genuine convention-1 surface mismatch - the retrieval surface over-promises what Terra will be asked to confirm - but it is one of three items in this family that promise door frame/casing/trim on their retrieval surfaces while none claims it, and the door question is owned and analysed in CAP-019. Listing it as a contributing cause here is documentation for the lead, not a lever: with bar none and a refuting unit, no edit to this item can be authored, and the one edit the unit might suggest (excising the door line from embed_text) is unverifiable offline because embed_text is the entire embedding input for the item.

It also would not have saved this case: the loss happened at Terra, which rejected the mechanism, not the subject. |
| pass_2d_selection | Nothing to follow up. The resolved item was rank 1 of 6 by support_phrase_hit with routing_reason exact_kind, kind_ok true and scene_ok true. The only nearby alternative, exposed_sheathing_or_missing_siding at rank 2, is a worse fit (no sheathing is exposed; the panels are deformed, not stripped). shed_exterior_paint_failure never entered the frozen list and is degradation-kind against a defect observation, so it was unavailable, not passed over. |
| upstream_pass_2a_observation | The 2a prose captured the condition directly under 'Severe deferred maintenance': 'missing/removed door, damaged siding and trim, deteriorated window'. It described the damage as 'damaged' rather than using the claim's vocabulary, which is a wording difference, not a lost or invented observation. This is not a Pass 2a error and must not be routed to out_of_scope_pass_2a. |
| upstream_kind_or_pass_2c | 2c classified the bullet defect, which matches the item's kind; the bullet survived (the 2c drops on this property were trees, fencing and metal posts). No kind or survival fault. |

**Semantic before after.** No operation is proposed, so no semantics change. damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim keeps atomic_claim {subject: 'exterior siding or trim', state: 'rot, warping, cracks, or missing sections', ontology_basis: 'asserted_failure'}, kind defect, severity 3, scope replace, trade exterior_siding_trim, change_type 'narrowed' with a single successor that keeps the parent id.

**Retrieval effects.** None. embed_text, name, description, support_any and deny_any are untouched, so no item's rank changes anywhere. (Any future edit to this item's embed_text - for example excising the door-rot line that the atomic_claim does not cover - would change its rank on every query and cannot be verified offline, since embed_text is the entire embedding input and there is no offline embedding store.)

**Economics fit.** Untouched and unauthorable. Economics fields (cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope) are never authorable in the decisions file, and this case reached none of them: Terra returned unsupported, accepted=false, disposition excluded, reason_code verdict_unsupported.

**Package route effects.** None. route_override stays null, package_affinity stays {exterior: {package_role: package_driver, package_type: exterior_repair}}, and no routing changes.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. Trade is exterior_siding_trim, not the product-quarantined electrical trade, so no non_catalog_action is forced on policy grounds; the no_change is reached on the evidence.

**Expected benefit.** None claimed - this is a deliberate no_change. The value delivered is negative: it records that a two_independent_human bar reported for the provisional cluster was an artifact of pooling three unrelated subjects, and that on the one unit that actually concerns damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim the item named the visible mechanism correctly and the loss belongs to Terra.

**Regression risk.** Zero by construction - no bytes change. The risk this outcome avoids is the opposite one: proposing an outbuilding carve-out or a dwelling-only narrowing off a single refuted unit at bar none, with no reviewed outbuilding success control anywhere in the family to test it against.

**Unresolved questions.** Steven's ruling owed: should damage to a detached outbuilding (shed, barn, garage) bill under the dwelling envelope item at all? The catalog answers 'yes for damage, separately for paint' only by accident - shed_exterior_paint_failure exists for finish while damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim silently absorbs outbuilding damage. A ruling either way is a prerequisite before anyone proposes an outbuilding-damage concept or a dwelling-only narrowing., If outbuilding damage is billable, is the economics right? damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim is package_driver for exterior_repair at severity 3, scope 'replace', work_item_code EXTERIOR_REPAIR, so a shed can seed a dwelling-scale exterior repair package. None of severity, scope, cost, work_item_code or package_affinity is authorable in the decisions file for this narrowed carryover, so if the answer is 'no' this is a policy/economics gap, not a catalog edit. Nothing was billed on this case (Terra rejected it), so the exposure is currently hypothetical., Terra-side, unowned by this program: does Terra apply an implicit dwelling-envelope framing that the atomic_claim does not state? Its rationale denied the mechanism outright rather than raising the subject, so this case is direct evidence of a mechanism-reading error and only suggestive evidence of a scope framing., Family-level, for the lead: three items here promise door frame / casing / trim on their retrieval surfaces while none of them claims it on the atomic_claim - damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim (['door']), exterior_siding_discoloration_fading (['trim_casing','wear_scuff']) and exterior_door_paint_failure (['trim_casing','moisture_rot','damage_break']). That is one overlapping-ontology finding, best recorded once at family level rather than three times across CAP-002 and CAP-019.

**Human disposition.** _blank_ (approved / approved_with_modification / rejected / deferred / reclassified_non_catalog)

### CAP-004 — Flooring material commitments: vinyl/linoleum vs plank, sheet, and tile

Status `no_change` · outcome `no_change` · confidence `high` · items `vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted`, `vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained`, `worn_or_stained_flooring`, `hard_flooring_broken_or_warped`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows flooring that is torn, lifting, open at seams, worn, stained, or dirty, and the flooring material visible does not match the material the catalog item commits to (vinyl/linoleum for the vinyl items; hard flooring for hard_flooring_broken_or_warped; the reviewer-named material of the green floor for worn_or_stained_flooring).

**Cluster.** subject: hard and resilient flooring; mechanism: torn, lifting, open-seam, worn, or dirty flooring whose material (vinyl/linoleum vs plank/sheet/tile/hard) differs from the material the resolved item commits to; kinds: defect, degradation; scenes: kitchen, bathroom, living_areas, bedroom; commitments: material, object. Unchanged from Phase A and re-affirmed by the family structure step: pooled on one commitment type (material) across flooring items. The wear-vs-damage-vs-style boundary on the same floors is CAP-005, so a unit may legitimately appear in both clusters and be adjudicated differently under the two claims (runtime:production:redfin_80916010:...:oc1_2c8aac3461bdb5b6 is refuted here on material and supports_claim there on wear/kind). No split was available (no new proposal ids were allocated to this family) and none is warranted: splitting a bar=none cluster cannot improve its bar, and merging with CAP-005 would let that cluster's two_independent_human bar carry material findings that have zero counted human support and three human refutations.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions subject, mechanism, scope, route.

**Diagnosis.** primary `terra_verification`; contributing upstream_pass_2a_observation, pass_2d_selection. The catalog does NOT own this cluster, and the Phase A hypothesis that the material commitments are wrong is overturned by the adjudications. Take the four human units one at a time. (1) rc_d60c090abd2c, redfin_11000447 photo_004: the reviewer read maroon SHEET VINYL with a worn-through traffic lane and a ragged lifted edge at the wall base - exactly the material and mechanism vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted commits to - and 2d actively overrode rank 1 (hard_flooring_broken_or_warped) to pick the vinyl item at rank 2. The catalog named it, retrieval retrieved it, 2d chose it; Terra then rejected it ('no clearly torn, worn-through, or lifting material is apparent'), which the reviewer refuted from the photo. Attribution stage=terra, downstream, high confidence. (2) rc_128caa6212b8, redfin_11000447 photo_003: 2d resolved to vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted and the human graded the CLAIM axis 'exact' against that item's wording; Terra rejected on a material-identity ground ('the visible flooring is hard plank material') that the reviewer says the photo cannot establish. Attribution stage=terra. Adjudicated unclear, so it contributes nothing to the bar. (3) rc_cf0c3ee00fcb, redfin_80925528 photos 042/044: the floor is intact green marbled tile, essentially clean; 2a welded a false wear/dirt mechanism onto a correct 'dated' reading. Attribution pass_2a, and Pass 2a is frozen - out of scope by the 2026-09-03 direction. Note the correctly-named half was NOT lost: on the same photo 2d resolved the 'is dated' bullet to older_flooring_style, so no coverage was missing. (4) rc_9c702893a6ce, redfin_80916010 photo_005: the floor is beige faux-tile SHEET VINYL, precisely what vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained's subject commits to, so the material commitment held and the CAP-004 claim is refuted; the failure is on the wear/kind axis and is CAP-005's, where the same unit is a counted support. Three of the four human units are attributed to Terra and one to Pass 2a; none to catalog wording. The only surviving material mismatch in the whole cluster is the model_judge context unit at redfin_125970550 photo_019, where the better-named item was reachable at rank 2 with kind_ok and scene_ok - a 2d selection question that the scope direction explicitly forbids compensating for by broadening the catalog, and a lead can never ground a non-deferred outcome in any case. Prefer the smallest lever: no_change plus documentation.

Two human units are Terra misses on correctly-material-matched claims: rc_d60c090abd2c ('The flooring is visible, but no clearly torn, worn-through, or lifting material is apparent' against a photo the reviewer describes as showing a worn-through traffic lane and a ragged lifted edge) and rc_128caa6212b8 ('The visible flooring is hard plank material, with no vinyl or linoleum tears, lifting, or open seams shown', which asserts a material identity the reviewer says the photo cannot settle and additionally denies the open seams 2a reported). Both carry attribution downstream/stage=terra at high confidence, both graded exact_and_warranted by the human. This is consistent with the already-closed error-attribution finding (docs/RESULT_error_attribution_20260831.md) that adjudicated miss cases are overwhelmingly downstream at Terra, so these two are instances of an owned finding rather than new work. |
| wording_creates_unsupported_commitments | Partly, and benignly on the evidence here. hard_flooring_broken_or_warped shows a real two-surface divergence under convention 1: its description says 'Hardwood/laminate/vinyl/tile' and its embed_text names chips/warping/cupping, while the atomic_claim Terra actually sees commits only to subject 'hard flooring' with state 'chips, warping, or broken pieces' (divergence_scan promised_not_claimed = laminate_wood_tile, resilient). The retrieval-facing surface therefore invites resilient sheet goods that the claim-bearing surface does not name, which is the plausible pull behind the one 2d misselection. But on the single case where it fired, Terra supported it ('broken and lifted sections in the foreground and along the perimeter') and the human agreed (v1_verdict terra_claim_supported); nothing failed, no billing was lost, and no counted human unit is attached. Document, do not repair. Symmetrically, vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted promises peel_flake in retrieval text that its claim does not name, with no observed consequence. |
| steals_neighbors_or_damages_successes | Not applicable - no ops are proposed. Assessed for the record, because the obvious tempting edit is to widen hard_flooring_broken_or_warped's atomic_claim subject to name resilient sheet goods (or, per Steven's unclear-row note, to collapse the material split into one generic 'hard flooring' subject). Either would collide head-on with the vinyl split it was deliberately separated from (parent scratched_or_damaged_flooring vs parent worn_or_stained_vinyl_linoleum), would move torn sheet-vinyl work from the replace/modernization route to the repair route, and would put five reviewed supports_claim controls at risk (rc_2ed75ba413ed, rc_7cbbf388a7c7, rc_6f59ce859fb7, rc_a5f2fa9cf45f, rc_d7eda0ba34df) plus the one correct rejection rc_6c4e3194c4c9, for zero counted human support. |
| alters_eligibility_package_economics | No change is proposed, so nothing moves. Recorded because it is what makes the one imperfect mapping consequential rather than equivalent: hard_flooring_broken_or_warped is scope=repair and routes bedroom/living as a package_driver into bedroom_repair / living_repair, while vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted is scope=replace and drives bedroom_modernization / living_modernization. Same trade_bucket (flooring), same work_item_code (FLOORING_REPLACE), same severity 1, both billable, no safety difference - but a different repair scope and a different package lane on a living_areas scene. Per the brief, dollar effects are context only. |

Unchanged under no_change; any future broadening of worn_or_stained_flooring, vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained, or the dated/worn boundary must demonstrate it stays rejected. |

**Semantic before after.** No change. Every implicated item keeps its current claim-bearing and retrieval-facing wording: vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted ('vinyl or linoleum flooring' / 'torn, worn through, or lifting material'), vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained ('vinyl or linoleum flooring' / 'staining, discoloration, or wear on intact material'), worn_or_stained_flooring ('flooring' / 'wear, staining, or discoloration without structural concern'), hard_flooring_broken_or_warped ('hard flooring' / 'chips, warping, or broken pieces'). The audit finding recorded against this wording is that it was CORRECT on both decidable material cases, and that the one documented divergence - hard_flooring_broken_or_warped promising laminate/wood/tile/resilient in description and embed_text while committing only to 'hard flooring' in the atomic_claim - produced a supported, human-agreed billing on its single firing.

**Retrieval effects.** None; no embed_text, name, description, support_any, deny_any or require_any is touched, so no item's rank moves anywhere. This is deliberate: embed_text is the entire embedding input when set and cannot be verified offline, and the cluster has zero counted human support that would justify spending that unverifiable risk. The frozen candidate lists already put the right item in the pool on every unit (reachability_scan: scene_ok true throughout; all rank=null entries are cross-kind gates from 2d's exact_kind filter, which no retrieval-surface edit can lift).

**Economics fit.** No economics field is touched - none is natively authorable in any case (cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope are never authorable for carryovers or split successors). All four implicated items keep tier=work, severity 1 and work_item_code FLOORING_REPLACE. Context only, per the brief: the two candidates in the one imperfect mapping differ in scope (repair vs replace), which is where any dollar effect would have shown up had a change been proposed.

**Package route effects.** None. hard_flooring_broken_or_warped stays a package_driver into bedroom_repair and living_repair with kitchen support; vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted and vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained stay drivers into bedroom_modernization and living_modernization with kitchen support; worn_or_stained_flooring stays support-only everywhere and defaultHidden. No route_override is proposed, and none of these items is on the quarantined electrical trade.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. All four items are trade_bucket flooring, which is not product-quarantined (the quarantine covers electrical only), so nothing here forces non_catalog_action on policy grounds. min_photo_evidence and other gate values live inside estimate and are policy gaps, not authorable surfaces; none is proposed.

**Expected benefit.** The benefit of no_change is negative-risk rather than positive-yield, and that is the correct trade here: it preserves five reviewed supports_claim controls and one reviewed correct rejection, it leaves the frozen Pass 2a negative control (rc_cf0c3ee00fcb, a clean floor 2a called worn and dirty) rejected, and it avoids re-opening a material split that the adjudicated evidence says is working. The cluster's real yield is diagnostic and is captured outside the catalog: two Terra misses on correctly-named, correctly-material-matched conditions, one frozen Pass 2a hallucination, and one 2d selection follow-up.

**Regression risk.** None from this outcome, since nothing changes. Stated for the successor who revisits this: the tempting edits are (a) widening hard_flooring_broken_or_warped's atomic_claim subject to name resilient sheet goods, or (b) Steven's suggested collapse of the material split into one generic 'hard flooring' subject. Both would make hard_flooring_broken_or_warped and the vinyl split compete for the same observations they were split apart to separate, would move torn sheet-vinyl work from a replace/modernization route to a repair route, and would loosen a claim whose only reviewed correct rejection (rc_6c4e3194c4c9, 'just paint and discolor, nothing is chipped') sits directly under it - all on zero counted human support and against three human refutations.

The better item was fully reachable: rank 2, kind_ok=true, scene_ok=true (reachability_scan). Nothing was lost - Terra supported the selected item and the human agreed (v1_verdict terra_claim_supported, disposition accepted_for_work) - so this is a precision and routing question, not a miss: hard_flooring_broken_or_warped is scope=repair and drives living_repair, while vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted is scope=replace and drives living_modernization. Likely mechanism for the pull, recorded not proven: the hard item's description names vinyl while its atomic_claim does not. Do NOT compensate for this by broadening the catalog. Steven's own v1.1 note on that row - 'we might want to change the catalog to "hard flooring" or something similar so that we dont get caught up on hardwood, LVP, linoleum' - is the single strongest argument for collapsing the material split, and it runs OPPOSITE to every adjudicated material finding in this cluster (on both decidable cases the material matched the item used). It cannot be acted on: unclear never becomes support, and it is one note on one row. Promotion recipe: re-review photo_003 at full resolution to settle the material, or add a second decidable vinyl-vs-laminate case where the material commitment demonstrably costs a billing; only then does a material-subject op have a counted human support behind it., Is Terra's material-identity reasoning a systematic failure mode on printed-grain resilient flooring? Two of four human units here died on it (rc_128caa6212b8 asserts 'hard plank material' about a floor the reviewer says could be LVP; rc_d60c090abd2c denies wear-through the reviewer sees), and a third case at the same family - the redfin_125970550 lead - shows Terra happily verifying SHEET goods against a 'hard flooring' claim. Terra is inconsistent about what 'hard' excludes. This needs a Terra-lane sample, not a catalog edit; it also sits inside the already-closed downstream-miss finding (docs/RESULT_error_attribution_20260831.md)., hard_flooring_broken_or_warped's two-surface divergence (description and embed_text promise Hardwood/laminate/vinyl/tile; the Terra-facing atomic_claim commits only to 'hard flooring'; divergence_scan promised_not_claimed = laminate_wood_tile, resilient) is documented as benign because on its one firing Terra supported and the human agreed. It would become actionable only with a case where the retrieval-facing vinyl promise pulled 2d to the hard item AND a human-supported billing was lost or mis-routed. The redfin_125970550 case is that shape but is lead-only and lost nothing., Both vinyl split successors have zero reviewed positive uses, zero agreements and zero correct rejections. Before any op touches vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted or vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained, the control base has to be built; today a change to either could not be regression-checked at all.

### CAP-005 — Flooring wear vs damage vs older style: kind and severity boundaries

Status `no_change` · outcome `no_change` · confidence `high` · items `hard_flooring_scratched_or_worn`, `older_flooring_style`, `worn_or_stained_flooring`, `vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows flooring whose visible condition (heavy scuffs/marks/soiling, discoloration, open seams or raised edges, cracked/missing pieces, patch sections) is materially different in kind or severity from what the resolved item claims (scratched/worn hard flooring; older flooring style; worn or stained flooring).

**Cluster.** subject: hard and resilient flooring; mechanism: scuffs, paint/adhesive marks, soiling, discoloration, open seams, raised edges, cracked or missing pieces, or patchwork on floors resolved to a wear (degradation) or older-style (modernization) item; kinds: degradation, modernization, defect; scenes: kitchen, bathroom, living_areas, bedroom; commitments: mechanism, severity, scope. Pooled on the wear/damage/style boundary that every flooring lead and both hard_flooring_scratched cases sit on; older_flooring_style enters only through model leads and is context, never support. Material commitments are CAP-004. Phase C keeps the pinned shape: the family structure step verified that splitting the two counted supports apart would leave two human=1 halves with no counted lead corroboration (every lead here is role=context), collapsing the only met bar in the flooring family to none, while both halves diagnose to the same non-catalog conclusion. The shared unit at redfin_80916010 legitimately sits in CAP-004 as well (refuted there on the material commitment, supports_claim here on the kind/severity commitment); removing it from this cluster would drop it to human=1 and collapse the bar.

At redfin_10952874 (rc_eef1daf73e2a) the reviewer found paint spills and splatters on otherwise intact laminate billed as a scratched or worn finish - human truth misnamed_but_warranted, attribution stage 2d. At redfin_126418713 (rc_d0197df43080) the reviewer found that hard_flooring_scratched_or_worn was exactly the right name for the faint smudge and hairline scratches visible, refuting the cluster claim - human truth exact_and_warranted, attribution stage terra. The refutation does not cancel the support because the two fail at different stages and about different behaviour: one is a selection error with the better item at rank 1, the other is a verification error on a correctly resolved item. The refutation's own property shows Terra failing in both directions on the same item - rc_d0197df43080 rejects a real condition while control rc_c135e76317be supports the same claim on newly refinished floors across three photos - so the refuting signal is evidence about Terra's consistency, not evidence that the catalog's wear wording is well calibrated. Net effect on the outcome: the mixed signal reinforces no_change, because the supporting half points at Pass 2d and the refuting half points at Terra, and neither points at an editable catalog surface.

The two positive-use controls (rc_5b633a3650f2 plain beige 12-inch ceramic tile, rc_d2d4a18410aa large-format beige tile) are concrete era tells and bill correctly today, while the hallucination card rc_7cceb5c878de (new plush carpet) is exactly what the convention excludes. No wording change is needed to implement the convention. |
| convention_3 | Steven's Phase C conventions (brief section 2, ruling 3) | Plain / basic / builder-grade presence is not billable by itself; a condition needs visible datedness, deterioration, incompleteness or another concrete modernization need. | Read against the six refuted leads on clean floors, this says the modernization lane is currently firing on the right cases and does not need loosening. It also means the redfin_80916010 floor is billable on its dated tell (printed faux-tile sheet vinyl in a dated kitchen), not on dinginess - which is why the fix would have to reach older_flooring_style, and why it cannot be reached from a degradation-only candidate pool. |
| v1_1_human_truth:rc_9c702893a6ce | frozen v1.1 human labels (redfin_80916010: misnamed_but_warranted, claim=misnamed, work=warranted) | Work is warranted on that floor but the billed name is wrong. | Establishes the redfin_80916010 unit as a real MISS (disposition excluded, reason_code verdict_unsupported), which is what makes this cluster catalog-first eligible and what forced the support_phrase_hit investigation that closed the deferral. |
| v1_1_human_truth:rc_d0197df43080 | frozen v1.1 human labels (redfin_126418713: exact_and_warranted, class dirB_recovery) | The billed claim was exact and the work warranted; Terra's rejection was wrong. | Creates the mixed signal and the mandatory conflict_note, and pins the refuting half of the cluster to Terra rather than to any catalog surface. |
| phase_b_adjudications | pinned Phase B photo review (read-only) | 9 supports_claim / 10 refuted across the 19 rows; the three role=support rows adjudicate supports_claim (redfin_10952874), refuted (redfin_126418713) and supports_claim (redfin_80916010). | Fixes the bar at two_independent_human with mixed_signal true. No adjudication is disputed or reinterpreted here; the one row where a second look was tempting (rc_63fac87a14e4, near-black photo) stays unclear and is carried as an unresolved question, not converted to support. |

**Diagnosis.** primary `pass_2d_selection`; contributing upstream_kind_or_pass_2c, terra_verification, retrieval_metadata_or_reachability. Catalog ownership was tested to the point of a runnable counterfactual and REJECTED. The one candidate catalog lever with a counted human support behind it was vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained's support_any, because the redfin_80916010 miss is recorded as resolution_path=lexical_shortcut / shortcut_reason=support_phrase_hit. The family structure step deferred that lever because the digest never names which phrase matched. This analysis resolved it: tools/scene_classifier_passes.py::_resolve_candidate_via_lexical_shortcut tests only candidates[0]'s support_any using tools/pipeline_common.py::term_matches, and running that real matcher over the real observation ('Vinyl flooring is worn-looking and visually dated.') against the item's shipped support_any returns exactly one hit - the bare material token 'vinyl'. 'worn vinyl' and 'stained vinyl' do not match, and 'worn' is not a token of this item at all, so the case's own attribution rationale ('latched onto the token "worn"') is factually wrong about the mechanism. That answer points AWAY from the catalog rather than toward it, for three independent reasons. (1) Prevalence: bare subject nouns in support_any are the catalog's own authoring convention, not this item's error - 125 of 128 items carry at least one condition-free support token (appliance, cabinet, window, gutter, outlet, deck, front door, ...), and the sibling successor vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted carries the identical five material tokens. Pruning one successor would make it inconsistent with 124 other items and would leave the same hazard everywhere else. (2) Contract: tools/catalog_embeddings.py documents and implements support_any as 'soft support / ranking hint only (not enforced as gate)', and retrieval score is pure cosine over the item's embed text, so support_any changes no rank anywhere. The author wrote a legal ranking hint; 2d's shortcut unilaterally promotes that hint into a sufficient rule for asserting the item's atomic_claim ('staining, discoloration, or wear on intact material') without ever consulting the LLM. The defect is in the consumer, not in the field. (3) divergence_scan agrees: vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained records promised_not_claimed = [] - its material tokens match its own subject, so there is no two-surface divergence to repair. Under convention 1 the surface that failed here is 2d-side mechanics, not claim-bearing wording. The other flooring items are equally clean on the claim surface: hard_flooring_scratched_or_worn's support_any (scratch, scuff, worn floor, dull finish, worn finish) carries no stain or style token, so the catalog did not invite the redfin_10952874 error either.

| rejected owner | why |
|---|---|
| catalog_wording_or_specificity | Neither counted support could have been rescued by a wording change. At redfin_10952874 the better-fitting item was already rank 1 on both issues and 2d selected rank 3 by LLM. At redfin_80916010 the right item (older_flooring_style) was kind_ok=false and absent from the pool before 2d ran, so its claim text - 'clean but' included - was never consulted; that is what overturns the 'clean but' hypothesis. The one tested wording-adjacent lever (support_any pruning) is rejected under catalog_ownership_argument. |
| missing_catalog_coverage | Both needed concepts exist as catalog items with the right scene groups (see coverage_gap.rationale). Nothing observed here is unnameable in catalog 3.2, so no split parent and no new successor is warranted. |
| overlapping_or_ambiguous_ontology | The dated/worn boundary is a deliberate design, not an ambiguity: older_flooring_style is modernization with 'clean but visually outdated'; worn_or_stained_flooring and hard_flooring_scratched_or_worn are degradation with 'without structural concern' / 'on intact flooring'. The reachability scan shows the boundary is actually decided upstream by Pass 2a's word choice - 'dated' alone yields an all-modernization pool with older_flooring_style at rank 1, while adding 'worn' or 'scuffed' flips the pool to degradation and drops it entirely - and is then enforced by the exact_kind filter. Collapsing the ontology would be a very large lever aimed at an upstream cause, and it would remove the anti-double-bill guard analysed under alters_eligibility_package_economics. |
| upstream_pass_2a_observation | Not the owner of either counted support. At redfin_10952874 the 2a prose named the paint and staining correctly on both photos; at redfin_80916010 the prose carried the modernization meaning ('worn-looking and visually dated', with an LVP/tile replacement recommendation), so 2a did not misread either photo. 2a IS the owner of lead 9ec347a23f3ed720 (missing and broken tiles at redfin_125970550: both observations name only staining, so hard_flooring_broken_or_warped was ungated but rank=null and 2d could not select a condition never described) and of the flooring negative control rc_cf0c3ee00fcb at redfin_80925528. Both are out_of_scope_pass_2a, both are lead-only or control rows, and neither can ground a change. |
| downstream_tier_projection_routing_package_costing | No counted unit fails after Terra. rc_eef1daf73e2a was accepted_for_work with reason_code route_work; rc_9c702893a6ce and rc_d0197df43080 both died with reason_code verdict_unsupported, i.e. at Terra or before it. There is no post-Terra routing or disposition loss in this cluster. |
| product_policy_or_quarantine | All four implicated items are trade_bucket 'flooring'. The electrical product quarantine does not apply, so nothing forces non_catalog_action. |

**Alternative-stage analysis.**

| question | answer |
|---|---|
| existing_item_exists | Yes, for both counted supports. redfin_10952874 needed worn_or_stained_flooring (or floor_dirty_or_heavily_soiled) and worn_or_stained_flooring was rank 1 on both issues, kind_ok and scene_ok true. redfin_80916010 needed older_flooring_style, which exists with kitchen in its scene_groups. No item needs to be added or split, and no successor id is required. |
| upstream_kind_wrong | TRUE for redfin_80916010. The compound bullet 'Vinyl flooring is worn-looking and visually dated.' carried the degradation kind and 2d ran under routing_reason=exact_kind, so all seven candidates were degradation items and older_flooring_style is recorded kind_ok=false (reachability_scan, issue 14a0ed440898b159). No catalog edit can insert a modernization item into a degradation-only pool, which is why the whole 'reword older_flooring_style' family of fixes is inert here. Whether the label was assigned at 2c or chosen at 2d is not exposed by the frozen record and is carried as an unresolved question. FALSE for the other two counted units, where the degradation lane was the right lane. |
| scene_or_retrieval_metadata_cause | No. scene_ok is true for every hypothesised better item on every counted issue - kitchen and bedroom are in all four implicated items' scene_groups - so no scene_groups change is implicated (and on carryovers scene_groups is not natively authorable anyway). Retrieval ranking is not the cause either: tools/catalog_embeddings.py indexes items on their embed text and ranks by cosine similarity alone, with support_any documented and implemented as a non-gating hint, so no support_any, deny_any or require_any authoring change could have moved a candidate rank. The only metadata that changed an outcome is the kind label, handled above. |
| retrieved_but_2d_selected_other | TRUE for redfin_10952874 (rc_eef1daf73e2a). worn_or_stained_flooring was rank 1 on BOTH issues, kind_ok and scene_ok true, resolution_path=llm, candidate_count 7, and 2d resolved to hard_flooring_scratched_or_worn at rank 3 - including on the photo_029 bullet ('discoloration, patchy areas, and scuffs'), the least scuff-forward wording of the two - while 2d itself assigned worn_or_stained_flooring to a sibling bullet on the same photo. FALSE for redfin_80916010: the better item was not in the pool at all, and the in-pool second choice (worn_or_stained_flooring at rank 2) over-claims wear exactly as the selected item does, so no re-ranking rescues that case. |
| terra_adjudicated_wrong_detail | TRUE, and it owns the counted refutation. At rc_d0197df43080 (redfin_126418713) 2a, 2b, 2c and 2d all handled a correctly named condition and Terra rejected it as 'generally intact without clear scratches, scuffs, or worn finish', which the reviewer's read of photo_044 contradicts (a dark smudge and hairline scratches in raking light on intact glossy oak). The same item at the same property over-fires in control rc_c135e76317be, where Terra SUPPORTED the claim on newly refinished high-gloss oak in three photos. Terra also over-supported at rc_eef1daf73e2a, confirming the underlying work while describing stains the billed claim does not cover. Under-firing and over-firing on one item at one property is a Terra consistency problem, not a claim-wording problem, and it is not fixable from the catalog. |
| wording_creates_unsupported_commitments | Tested and answered NO for every counted unit. The claim-bearing surface behaved correctly in all three: Terra was shown exactly what the catalog asserts, and its rejection at redfin_80916010 and its acceptance at redfin_10952874 are both faithful readings of the claim text it was handed. The one mechanism that could have made a catalog field carry an unsupported commitment - 2d's support_phrase_hit shortcut asserting vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained's deterioration claim off the bare token 'vinyl' - is a resolver behaviour applied to a catalog-wide authoring convention (125 of 128 items carry condition-free support tokens), and divergence_scan records promised_not_claimed=[] for that item. The single genuine two-surface divergence in this cluster is worn_or_stained_flooring, whose support_any carries the style tokens 'dated', 'aged' and 'outdated' on a claim that asserts only wear, staining or discoloration (divergence_scan promised_not_claimed=['dated']). It is natively editable via overrides on a reclassified carryover, but no counted human unit traces a failure to it and every unit where it could have mattered resolved by LLM rather than by shortcut, so it stays a documented observation for a future retrieval-metadata lane. |
| steals_neighbors_or_damages_successes | No op is proposed, so nothing is disturbed. For the record, the op that was built and rejected (dropping the five bare material tokens from vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained's support_any) was simulated against the shipped resolver: it does stop the shortcut on the failing observation, but it also stops it for the whole legitimate class - 'The vinyl flooring is stained and discolored.', 'Linoleum floor is worn through near the sink.' and 'Sheet vinyl is stained.' all stop shortcutting and fall through to the LLM path (they are not lost, since the item's embedding rank is untouched, but their resolution behaviour changes and cannot be verified offline). That is a broad unmeasured behavioural change on an item with zero reviewed positive uses, zero correct rejections and zero hallucination cards - no control surface at all on which damage could be detected. That asymmetry is a second, independent reason not to propose it. |
| alters_eligibility_package_economics | No change is proposed, so no economics move. The economics answer this cluster owes, stated affirmatively: KEEP 'clean but' in older_flooring_style's atomic_claim. It is the only textual guard that stops a floor that is both dated and dirty from billing on both lanes, and both lanes are already simultaneously live - redfin_80925528 emitted a degradation bullet ('worn and dirty' -> worn_or_stained_flooring, accepted, and the human called it a 2a hallucination) and four modernization bullets ('the green floor is dated' -> older_flooring_style at rank 1) on the same floor. The lanes are not economically symmetric: older_flooring_style is severity 2, scope replace and package_DRIVER for bedroom_modernization and living_modernization, while worn_or_stained_flooring is severity 1, package_SUPPORT only and defaultHidden true. Removing 'clean but' would therefore not merely add a hidden second line - it could promote a whole-room modernization package that the wear item cannot trigger on its own, with zero counted human support asking for it and two hallucination controls (rc_7cceb5c878de, rc_c135e76317be) sitting on exactly that surface. One honest caveat that strengthens rather than weakens the call: six leads on floors the reviewer described as dirty, soiled or heavily worn (redfin_11000447 x2, redfin_125779232 x2, redfin_125970550, redfin_80990371) carry stored_verdict=supported on older_flooring_style anyway, so the guard is under-enforced at runtime today. Deleting the words would legitimise that leakage rather than fix it, and the enforcement gap belongs to Terra. |

**Semantic before after.** No change proposed; the catalog's semantics stay exactly as authored. Recorded for the lead, the op that was built and rejected would have been: legacy_id worn_or_stained_vinyl_linoleum, path /successors/1/support_any (index 1 is vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained; index 0 is vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted), op set, after ['worn vinyl', 'stained vinyl', 'discolored flooring'] - dropping the five bare material tokens linoleum / vinyl / vinyl sheet / sheet flooring / resilient flooring - with the required companion sets on /atomicity_rationale and /expected_effects/routing. It is natively expressible (support_any is set directly on a split successor) and it does fix the failing case in simulation, but it is NOT proposed for the reasons in diagnosis.catalog_ownership_argument and alternative_stage_analysis.steals_neighbors_or_damages_successes. No dry run was executed and no expected_item_changes are claimed.

**Retrieval effects.** None, because nothing is changed. The retrieval fact that decided the analysis: tools/catalog_embeddings.py builds the index from each item's embed text and ranks with cosine similarity only, and build_guardrails_from_catalog documents support_any as 'soft support / ranking hint only (not enforced as gate)'. So no support_any edit anywhere in this cluster could have changed a candidate rank, and the ranks that actually mattered - worn_or_stained_flooring at rank 1 on both redfin_10952874 issues, older_flooring_style absent by kind at redfin_80916010 - are untouchable from the catalog's retrieval-hint surface.

**Economics fit.** Unchanged. The affirmative economics answer this cluster owes: KEEP 'clean but' in older_flooring_style. It is the only wording separating the modernization lane (severity 2, scope replace, package_driver for bedroom_modernization and living_modernization) from the degradation lane (worn_or_stained_flooring: severity 1, package_support, defaultHidden true), both lanes already fire on the same floor at redfin_80925528, and removing it would systematise double-billing on dated-and-dirty floors while allowing a whole-room modernization package to be promoted off a floor whose only defect is dirt.

**Package route effects.** None. No package_affinity, package_role, route_override, trade_bucket, tier or scope field is touched - and on a carryover none of those is natively authorable in any case. The package facts that argue for no_change are recorded under alternative_stage_analysis.alters_eligibility_package_economics, and the package divergence inside the rc_eef1daf73e2a mapping (bedroom_repair driver versus bedroom_modernization support) is recorded on the pass_2d_follow_up where it belongs.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. All four implicated items are trade_bucket 'flooring'; none is product-quarantined, so nothing forces non_catalog_action and no product lane is affected.

**Expected benefit.** None claimed, because no change is made. The value of the analysis is a closed negative result: the single question the family structure step said could flip this cluster to a native proposal - which support_any phrase fired the support_phrase_hit at redfin_80916010 - has been executed against the shipped resolver and answered, and it answered against the catalog. The lead can close that deferral rather than carry it, the two pass_2d_follow_ups now name verified mechanisms instead of hypotheses, and the 'clean but' economics question is answered in the affirmative.

**Regression risk.** None arising from this finding, since nothing changes. The regression risk that was measured belongs to the rejected op: it suppresses the support_phrase_hit shortcut for the entire vinyl/linoleum observation class (verified on 'The vinyl flooring is stained and discolored.', 'Linoleum floor is worn through near the sink.' and 'Sheet vinyl is stained.', all of which fall through to the LLM path), on an item that has no reviewed control family at all, while leaving the sibling successor vinyl_linoleum_torn_or_lifted carrying the identical five material tokens.

The photo_029 bullet leads with discoloration and patchy areas - the least scuff-forward wording of the two - and still routed to the scratch/wear item, and 2d itself assigned worn_or_stained_flooring to a sibling bullet on the same photo, so this is a selection failure and not an artifact of 2a's phrasing. The catalog did not invite it: hard_flooring_scratched_or_worn's support_any is scratch / scuff / worn floor / dull finish / worn finish, with no stain or style token, and under the leading-boundary matcher none of those fires on the photo_025 observation, which is consistent with the recorded llm path. Operationally consequential, not equivalent, on mechanism (abrasion versus paint contamination), scope (repair versus replace) and packaging (hard_flooring_scratched_or_worn is bedroom package_driver into bedroom_repair; worn_or_stained_flooring is bedroom package_support into bedroom_modernization). Catalog unchanged. |
| rc_9c702893a6ce | vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained | older_flooring_style | — | flooring_dated_style_vs_wear | redfin_80916010 photo_005, issue 14a0ed440898b159. Two defects in one case, both non-catalog. (a) Reachability: older_flooring_style was not in the pool and could not have been - routing_reason=exact_kind on a degradation bullet, all seven candidates degradation, older_flooring_style kind_ok=false (reachability_scan). better_rank is null for that reason, and the best in-pool alternative (worn_or_stained_flooring at rank 2) over-claims wear exactly as the selected item does, so no re-ranking rescues this. (b) Mechanism, now verified rather than assumed: 2d took resolution_path=lexical_shortcut with shortcut_reason=support_phrase_hit, and re-running the shipped matcher (tools/scene_classifier_passes.py::_resolve_candidate_via_lexical_shortcut with tools/pipeline_common.py::term_matches) over the real observation against the item's shipped support_any returns exactly one hit: the bare material token 'vinyl'. Not 'worn vinyl', not 'stained vinyl'; 'worn' is not a token of this item at all, so the case's attribution rationale is wrong about which token fired. The shortcut therefore skipped the LLM entirely and asserted 'staining, discoloration, or wear on intact material' on the strength of a material noun; Terra correctly rejected that claim and a real dated-flooring replacement went unbilled. The actionable owner is the shortcut rule - support_any is documented as a non-gating ranking hint and 125 of 128 items author bare subject nouns into it - not this item's tokens. Suggested 2d-lane fix, for the deferred Pass 2d work and explicitly NOT proposed as a catalog change: require a condition-bearing term match (or subject-plus-condition co-occurrence) before the shortcut may bypass the LLM. |

**Unresolved questions.** Whether the degradation kind on the compound bullet 'Vinyl flooring is worn-looking and visually dated.' (redfin_80916010, issue 14a0ed440898b159) was assigned at 2c or chosen at 2d. The frozen record exposes catalog kind only alongside the 2d-resolved item; routing_reason=exact_kind with all seven candidates degradation points to a real upstream kind filter, but that is inference. It decides whether the follow-up is owned by 2c or by 2d; it does not change the outcome, because neither owner is the catalog., worn_or_stained_flooring's 'dated' / 'aged' / 'outdated' support_any tokens on a claim asserting only wear (divergence_scan promised_not_claimed=['dated']) are a genuine two-surface divergence and are natively editable via overrides on a reclassified carryover, but no counted human unit traces a failure to them. Promotion recipe: find a case where worn_or_stained_flooring is candidates[0] and the observation matches only a style token - i.e. resolution_path=lexical_shortcut with shortcut_reason=support_phrase_hit and no wear word in the bullet. No such case exists in the frozen evidence; every unit where those tokens could have mattered resolved by LLM., Catalog-wide question raised but not answered by this cluster, for the Pass 2d lane: 2d's lexical shortcut treats any support_any hit on candidates[0] as sufficient to assert that item's atomic_claim without LLM adjudication, while catalog_embeddings documents support_any as a non-gating ranking hint and 125 of 128 items author bare subject nouns into it. That contract mismatch is a resolver-design question; a per-item catalog fix would be arbitrary and would leave the hazard on the other 124 items. Suggested shape (not proposed here): require the shortcut to match a condition-bearing term, or a subject term co-occurring with a condition term, before it may bypass the LLM., rc_63fac87a14e4 (older_flooring_style at redfin_81000709 photo_024) stays unclear because the photo is near black, which leaves older_flooring_style with no usable correct-rejection control. Promotion recipe: re-expose or re-shoot that photo, or add a second correct-rejection card on that item before any future proposal touches the modernization lane.

**Human disposition.** _blank_ (approved / approved_with_modification / rejected / deferred / reclassified_non_catalog)

### CAP-006 — Bathroom fixture, surround, and vanity conditions resolved to a sibling item naming a different component or mechanism

Status `no_change` · outcome `no_change` · confidence `high` · items `bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn`, `tub_surround_or_shower_pan_damage`, `vanity_worn_finish`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows a bathroom component (tub/shower surround or seams, bath fixtures, vanity cabinet body or toe-kick) whose visible deterioration or damage differs in component or mechanism from what the resolved catalog item claims.

**Cluster.** subject: bathroom tub/shower surround and seams, bath fixtures (tub, shower pan, toilet), and vanity cabinet body and toe-kick; mechanism: discolored, stained, worn, chipped, broken or detached bathroom components resolved to a catalog item whose named component (fixtures vs floor/wall finish) or mechanism (worn finish on an intact unit vs a broken or detached component) is not the one visible; kinds: degradation, defect; scenes: bathroom; commitments: object, mechanism. Pooled on the bathroom-component boundary within one trade bucket: after the reshape every unit is a bathroom_fixtures_tile component resolved to a sibling bathroom_fixtures_tile item, held together by one commitment type (the resolved item names a different component or a different mechanism than the photo shows). The provisional cluster additionally carried rc_eccd12ababc5 (redfin_11079485, 'The shower trim is old'), a plumbing-fixture observation resolved to dated_interior_trim in trade bucket trim_doors_windows; that is wrong-subject-across-families rather than wrong-component-within-family, so it moves to CAP-021 and takes dated_interior_trim with it (CAP-008 becomes that item's sole owner, so no worklist item loses a cluster). Never multiplied units: rc_6285e9a79e45 aggregates four observations into one condition and is counted once (convention 2).

**Reshape.** from CAP-006 (narrower): The claim is narrowed from the provisional CAP-006 claim by dropping the 'shower valve/trim' branch: rc_eccd12ababc5 leaves for CAP-021 and dated_interior_trim leaves with it. All remaining units keep their original CAP-006 review rows and their reviewer verdicts are unchanged; the narrower claim is entailed by the pinned one, so every retained adjudication still answers the claim it was reviewed against. The recomputed bar survives the narrowing (two counted human supports on two distinct properties).

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions subject, mechanism, severity, route.

Two human units on two distinct properties gives two_independent_human. The third support unit, rc_ace7463d6837 (redfin_125970550), is human but adjudicated refuted, so it does not count toward the bar and instead supplies the cluster's one reviewed refutation with photos available. The four model_judge units (redfin_10806500, redfin_10952874, redfin_11185681, redfin_125970550/oc1_562dd1da5b0d9252) are context and count for nothing; no conclusion here rests on them. The provisional digest recomputed three human supports on three properties because it still contained rc_eccd12ababc5; after that unit moves to CAP-021 the bar is unchanged at two_independent_human. Recomputed counts for the tool to match: counted_support_units 2, human 2, leads_counted 0, distinct_properties 2, reviewed_refutations 1, mixed_signal true. Correlation: Not required (two distinct properties, redfin_125779232 and redfin_11000447, with no shared photo, condition or run). Recorded for completeness: the only same-property pairing anywhere in the cluster is the refuted human unit and one model lead on redfin_125970550 photo_017, and neither of those counts toward the bar, so no correlated evidence inflates the count. Conflict: Mandatory and supplied: this cluster has mixed human signal. Two human support units were adjudicated supports_claim and one human support unit, rc_ace7463d6837, was adjudicated refuted. The conflict is real but structured, not noise. The refutation is confined to tub_surround_or_shower_pan_damage: on redfin_125970550 photo_017 the reviewer found the resolved item names the visible damage exactly ('the tiled shower enclosure is broken open at the valve... which is exactly the cracked/chipped/damaged wet-area enclosure the resolved item claims'), so on that unit there is no component or mechanism mismatch at all - the failure is Terra's rejection of a correct claim. The two supporting units concern different items (bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn, vanity_worn_finish) and a different failure, so the refutation does not contradict them; it constrains the cluster's reach. I treat it as decisive against any proposal touching tub_surround_or_shower_pan_damage - the reviewed evidence on that item says the catalog is right and the verifier was wrong - and it reinforces the no_change outcome rather than undermining the bar. The adjudication itself is untouched: refuted stays refuted.

**Diagnosis.** primary `pass_2d_selection`; contributing upstream_kind_or_pass_2c, terra_verification, catalog_wording_or_specificity. The catalog does not own this cluster, and I tested that conclusion rather than assuming it. (1) The divergence scan shows NO retrieval-vs-claim divergence for two of the three implicated items (bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn and vanity_worn_finish have empty promised_not_claimed and empty claimed_not_promised) and only 'moisture_rot' for tub_surround_or_shower_pan_damage. Unlike CAP-008 and CAP-013, there is no surface here that promises one thing to retrieval and commits to another at Terra. (2) The one genuinely catalog-owned surface I found is that bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn carries the bare adjectives 'stained' and 'worn' plus the location nouns 'tub$' and 'shower' in support_any, and that its embed_text advertises 'perimeter grime at shower pan or tub edges'. Three of the four bullets in rc_6285e9a79e45 resolved by resolution_path lexical_shortcut / shortcut_reason support_phrase_hit. That looks like a catalog lever until you read the code: tools/scene_classifier_passes.py builds the shortcut only for the ALREADY top-ranked candidate (a min-score and min-margin gate at lines 1101-1109 runs before _candidate_support_terms is consulted), so support_any did not select the item, it only skipped the LLM call for an item retrieval had already put at rank 1. The fourth bullet in the same case ('The toilet seat or toilet appears worn.') took the llm path with the same top-8 and chose bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn anyway. Deleting the generic tokens would therefore have handed these bullets to the same LLM that independently picked the same item; it would not recover the case it is built on, it cannot be verified offline (support_any is also a soft ranking hint and the annotator search index, and there is no offline embedding store), and it puts three human-verified positive uses of the item at risk (rc_699d79f6ee07, rc_d5cf0019df00, rc_ff9596f21e23, all exact/warranted and all phrased in exactly those words). Rejected as not the smallest lever and not a fix. (3) The second catalog-flavoured observation is vanity_worn_finish's atomic_claim qualifier 'on intact vanity', which redfin_11000447 photo_008 falsifies (base/toe-kick missing, raw framing exposed) while Terra supported it anyway. Removing that qualifier would broaden vanity_worn_finish across the boundary the 3.x split deliberately drew against its sibling vanity_damaged_or_water_stained - and that sibling already fired correctly on the same photo, so the broadening would buy nothing and would make an over-claim easier. Rejected. (4) What remains is a 2d selection failure with the better item reachable (rc_6285e9a79e45), a kind-gate that cost nothing because the exact item fired on the sibling bullet (rc_e9d27cf6e9aa), and a plain Terra error on a correctly named item (rc_ace7463d6837). Per the 2026-09-03 scope direction, a 2d selection failure is never compensated by editing the catalog; it becomes a pass_2d_follow_ups entry with the catalog unchanged.

| rejected owner | why |
|---|---|
| upstream_pass_2a_observation | Pass 2a named every component correctly and at the right strength in all three human units: at redfin_125779232 it assigned heavy staining to the floor and only hedged 'somewhat worn/discolored' for the surround; at redfin_11000447 it emitted both the general wear and the open toe-kick; at redfin_125970550 it named the damaged valve/trim and the questionable pan. No 2a error to attribute, and 2a is frozen and out of scope regardless. |
| missing_catalog_coverage | All 128 items were searched. Every mis-billed subject here has a claiming item and in each case that item was actually used somewhere in the same frozen run (vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained, damaged_drywall_or_cracks, wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents, outdated_bathroom_finishes, vanity_damaged_or_water_stained). Coverage is present; selection is what failed. |
| retrieval_metadata_or_reachability | Scene was bathroom on every unit and scene_ok is true for every hypothesised better item in the reachability scan. Retrieval put the better items inside the frozen top-8 for the two mis-selected bullets (vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained rank 4 on the floor bullet, bathroom_paint_peeling_or_worn rank 2 and wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents rank 3 on the wall bullet), so retrieval delivered the right candidates and 2d passed over them. |
| downstream_tier_projection_routing_package_costing | Checked directly in the frozen artifacts and the downstream behaved correctly, indeed it absorbed both misnamings. At redfin_125779232 the misnamed condition's FIXTURES_UPDATE work item (wk1_0a432a2287bfbd24) was suppressed as dedup_collision into wk1_c8ce56fe056d7138, which outdated_bathroom_finishes already drove at 1092-10924. At redfin_11000447 both vanity conditions merged into one active VANITY_REPLACE work item (wk1_1b9a56a0ba5affbe, 380-4563) with both per-condition items suppressed as dedup_collision. Dollar effects are context only, but this rules the downstream out as an owner. |

Rejected as the owner for this cluster because the split demonstrably worked on this very photo: the defect-phrased sibling bullet reached vanity_damaged_or_water_stained, Terra supported it, it was accepted for work, and it deduped with the misnamed condition into a single VANITY_REPLACE work item. The ontology cost a display name, not a condition. Recorded as an unresolved question for the program rather than a defect of this cluster. |

| upstream_kind_wrong | Partly, on one unit, and it cost nothing. For rc_e9d27cf6e9aa the 2c kind of the billed bullet was degradation, so the reachability scan shows vanity_damaged_or_water_stained (defect) with kind_ok false and rank null - genuinely unreachable for that bullet. But 2b had split one compound 2a sentence into two bullets and the defect-phrased sibling reached the exact item on the same photo in the same run, so the correct condition was billed anyway. The frozen attribution assigns this case to stage 2d; the reachability scan shows the exact item was not in that bullet's candidate set, so on the evidence the kind partition, not 2d, is the constraint - a correction to the frozen attribution that changes the stage story but not the outcome, since the correct billing happened regardless. For rc_6285e9a79e45 and rc_ace7463d6837 the kinds (degradation, defect) match the resolved items exactly (routing_reason exact_kind on every issue). |

All three implicated items carry scene_groups ['bathroom'] and no require_any that could have excluded these observations. Retrieval is exonerated positively rather than by default: it placed the better items inside the frozen top-8 on the two bullets where a better item existed and was of the right kind. |

Three of the four took the lexical_shortcut path; the fourth took the llm path and still chose the same item. In rc_ace7463d6837 2d chose correctly at rank 1. In rc_e9d27cf6e9aa the better item was not in the candidate list at all. |
| terra_adjudicated_wrong_detail | Yes, twice, in opposite directions. rc_ace7463d6837 is a pure Terra error: the human truth is exact/warranted, tub_surround_or_shower_pan_damage ranked 1 and names the visible damage precisely, yet Terra returned unsupported ('no clear crack, chip, or enclosure damage is visible') against a photo showing a tile wall broken open at the valve and a chipped, eroded pan and curb, and the condition was excluded with reason_code verdict_unsupported - a lost real condition owned entirely by Terra. In the other direction Terra's supporting rationale on rc_6285e9a79e45 ('The tub surround and tub base show visible discoloration and worn-looking seams') overstates what the reviewer found on the surround, so Terra ratified rather than caught the 2d misnaming; and on rc_e9d27cf6e9aa Terra supported a claim whose 'on intact vanity' qualifier its own rationale ('dark deterioration along its exposed side edge') contradicts. |
| wording_creates_unsupported_commitments | Yes, one real instance, and it is confined. vanity_worn_finish's atomic_claim commits to 'worn or faded finish on intact vanity'; at redfin_11000447 photo_008 the vanity is not intact (base/toe-kick missing, raw framing exposed), so the billed claim asserted an intactness the photo contradicts. That is a genuine unsupported commitment under convention 1, but it is not a catalog defect worth repairing here: the qualifier is exactly what separates this successor from its sibling vanity_damaged_or_water_stained, which fired correctly on the same photo. bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn's 'undamaged' qualifier is the mirror-image boundary against bath_fixtures_chipped_or_damaged and behaved correctly on all three of its reviewed positive uses. Neither item shows any promised_not_claimed or claimed_not_promised divergence in the scan. |
| steals_neighbors_or_damages_successes | Every lever considered would. Narrowing bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn's support_any or embed_text puts three human-verified positive uses at risk (rc_699d79f6ee07 'discolored cultured-marble tops with worn chrome faucets, a soiled shower pan and a dingy blue toilet', rc_d5cf0019df00 'the tub floor is brown-stained... all on undamaged fixtures', rc_ff9596f21e23 'blue-green staining across its basin... a dulled, worn surface'), and because support_any also feeds ranking and the annotator index the damage cannot be measured offline. Adding deny_any for floor/wall words would fire on the legitimate 'tub floor is brown-stained' wording in rc_d5cf0019df00. Adding require_any fixture nouns would not have blocked the two bad bullets, which both contain the word 'tub' as a location. Loosening vanity_worn_finish's intactness qualifier would steal from its own sibling, which is already doing the job. This is the decisive reason the cluster stays at no_change despite a met bar. |

Both items are package_driver/package_support in bathroom_repair or bathroom_modernization and no package route would move. The harm in this cluster is a wrongly named billed condition (a naming-level false positive on the fixtures subject) plus one lost real condition at Terra, not dollars. |

**Semantic before after.** No catalog change is proposed, so no item's semantics change. Recorded for the reconciliation: bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn keeps atomic_claim {subject: 'bath fixtures', state: 'heavy staining or worn finish, undamaged', ontology_basis: visible_deterioration}; vanity_worn_finish keeps 'worn or faded finish on intact vanity'; tub_surround_or_shower_pan_damage keeps 'cracked, chipped, or damaged wet-area enclosure'. All three are left exactly as they are because the reviewed evidence says each names its own subject correctly and the failures happened at selection and verification.

**Retrieval effects.** None, by construction. No embed_text, name, description, support_any, deny_any or require_any is touched, so no item's rank changes anywhere. This is deliberate: bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn's rank-1 dominance across bathroom wear bullets is real but unmeasurable offline (no embedding store), and every candidate edit to its retrieval surface risked three human-verified positive uses without recovering the case that motivated it.

**Economics fit.** Unchanged. All three items keep bills=true, route work, tier work, trade bathroom_fixtures_tile; work_item_codes stay FIXTURES_UPDATE, VANITY_REPLACE and TUB_SHOWER_REPAIR; no economics field is authorable in any case.

**Package route effects.** None. bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn stays package_support in bathroom_repair, vanity_worn_finish and tub_surround_or_shower_pan_damage stay package_driver in bathroom_repair, and no route_override is proposed. Observed in the frozen runs and reported as context only: today's two misnamed conditions both deduped into work items that a correctly named condition already drove, so package economics were already unaffected.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. No implicated item sits in the product-quarantined electrical trade; all three are bathroom_fixtures_tile and all three bill under work_default.

**Expected benefit.** None claimed from the catalog, which is the point of this outcome. The benefit of the judgment is negative-space: it removes three tempting but wrong levers from the program's candidate list with a mechanical reason for each (support_any cannot select an item it did not already rank first - tools/scene_classifier_passes.py lines 1101-1109; loosening vanity_worn_finish's intactness qualifier steals from a sibling that already fired on the same photo; there is no negative control on any of these items against which a broadening could be proved safe), and it routes the two real defects to their owners: two pass_2d follow-ups and one Terra verdict error (rc_ace7463d6837), which is the only lost real condition in the cluster.

**Regression risk.** Zero by construction - no file changes, no item diff, no order change. The risk this outcome accepts is the opposite one: if the lead later concludes that bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn's breadth is worth attacking, that work must be done in the retrieval lane with an online harness and the three positive-use controls as its regression set, not as a decisions-file edit justified by this cluster.

**Unresolved questions.** Retrieval-lane question deferred out of the catalog: bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn is rank 1 for almost any bathroom wear bullet in the corpus - it took rank 1 on all four bullets of rc_6285e9a79e45 and on every issue of all four model leads, including bullets about ceilings, caulk, grout, shower glass and a curb. That is an embedding-rank concentration, not a claim defect, and there is no offline embedding store to test a fix against. Whether 2d should ever bind more than one bullet to one condition is a pipeline-policy question outside this cluster's scope; it is the mechanism that made this misnaming possible and it will recur independent of any catalog wording.

Splitting would leave two one-unit clusters, each stripped of the other's reviewed refutation, and would change no outcome: neither half is catalog-owned. A materially more exact item, deck_surface_weathering, was in the frozen candidate list at rank 4 with kind and scene both open; per convention 2 and the 2026-09-03 scope direction that makes it a Pass 2d specificity question, and the standing instruction is never to compensate for a 2d selection failure by broadening the catalog. Widening patio_or_porch_surface_wear toward wood decking would in fact collide with deck_surface_weathering, which already owns that subject, and would make the known-false 'uneven'/'no porch here' 2a observations easier rather than harder to bill.

**Retrieval effects.** None. No embed_text, name, description, support_any, deny_any or require_any is edited, so no item's rank moves anywhere in the corpus. This is deliberate: embed_text is the entire embedding input for an item and there is no offline embedding store, so any retrieval edit here would be unverifiable, while the reviewed evidence gives no reason to make one - the item already retrieved at rank 1 on both units.

**Economics fit.** Not applicable - no operation is proposed, and economics fields (cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope) are never natively authorable in the decisions file. Context only: patio_or_porch_surface_wear prices as SURFACE_RESURFACE and deck_surface_weathering as DECK_REFINISH, both severity 1 cosmetic work-tier, so the 2d specificity difference moves the work item code but not the tier or the route.

**Package route effects.** None. route_override stays null and package_affinity stays {exterior: {package_role: package_support, package_type: exterior_repair}}. Even the follow-up's preferred item carries the identical package role and type, so nothing in packaging or routing is at stake in this cluster.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. Trade bucket is exterior_siding_trim, not the product-quarantined electrical trade, so no non_catalog_action is forced on policy grounds; the outcome is no_change on the merits.

**Expected benefit.** The benefit is documentary and preventive rather than mechanical: this cluster's two 'misnamed_billed' human labels are shown NOT to be catalog defects, which removes patio_or_porch_surface_wear from the catalog-change candidate list and protects three reviewed concrete-slab positive uses from a widening that would have been justified only by a Pass 2a hallucination and a Pass 2d specificity slip. The one carried-forward artifact is a single pass_2d_follow_ups entry for the 2d lane, plus an out_of_scope_pass_2a note for rc_3deb5aaef0f0.

**Regression risk.** None from this outcome - nothing is edited, so no billing, retrieval, routing or costing behaviour changes and no control can move. The residual risk is the risk of the alternative that is being declined: broadening this item's subject or claim toward wood decking would (a) overlap deck_surface_weathering, (b) make 'The hardscape is neglected, uneven-looking' and 'Porch and entry details appear aging' (on a photo with no porch) easier to bill, and (c) do so with zero correct-rejection or hallucination controls on this item available to detect the damage.

### CAP-015 — Kitchen cabinet damage called intact by Terra

Status `no_change` · outcome `no_change` · confidence `high` · items `cabinets_damaged_or_water_stained`, `cabinets_worn_finish`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows kitchen cabinet doors, drawers, or lower components that are visibly damaged, misaligned, worn, or chipped, while clearly broken components or water staining (the mechanism the defect claim names) are not evident.

**Cluster.** subject: kitchen cabinet doors, drawers, lower components; mechanism: damaged, misaligned, worn, or chipped cabinet components without clearly broken parts or water staining; kinds: defect, degradation; scenes: kitchen; commitments: mechanism, severity. POOLED, no split, no reshape. Two units, one kitchen-cabinet subject, one mechanism boundary (broken/water-stained defect vs worn-finish degradation). Splitting is structurally pointless here: the redfin_125779232 unit is the only human unit and it is adjudicated refuted, and the redfin_80990371 unit is a model_judge lead, so a split would produce one refutation-only cluster and one lead-only cluster, and a lead-only cluster can take no non-deferred outcome at all. Keeping them pooled is also the honest object under test, because the claim as written is a single mechanism-boundary question about the same two split siblings. Both hypothesised unsupported commitment types (mechanism, severity) were tested against the reviewed evidence and neither survived: on the one reviewed human unit the mechanism the defect item names WAS evident, and severity was never in dispute (both siblings are severity 2, tier work, scope replace, work_item_code CABINET_REPLACE).

Unit redfin_80990371 (model_judge, independent true) is adjudicated supports_claim, so it is the single counted unit and it is a lead. First, refutation-backed: reviewed_refutations = 1 (the redfin_125779232 human unit, role support, adjudicated refuted, photos status 'available' with photo_002.jpg present on disk), which satisfies the first disjunct and admits outcomes {no_change, non_catalog_action, migration_system_gap}; no_change is the one chosen. Second, the 'any non-deferred outcome needs >=1 reviewed human/gold unit' rule is met by that same reviewed human refutation. ops is empty, as required for a non-native outcome, and no structural_exception is claimed. Conflict: mixed_signal is false by the definition that governs it: mixed signal requires both a human/gold supports_claim and a human/gold refuted among the support units, and there is no human/gold supports_claim unit here at all. The record does nevertheless contain a directional disagreement worth naming, because it is the reason this cluster reads as a Terra finding rather than a catalog finding: the single human unit refutes the cluster claim (the named mechanism was evident) while the single model_judge lead supports it (a worn-finish case with nothing broken). Those are not in conflict about the world - they are two different properties showing two different mechanisms, and each was routed to the correct sibling. The lead's support is what the cluster claim was built to test, and it cannot carry a non-deferred outcome on its own.

**Diagnosis.** primary `terra_verification`; contributing —. The catalog does not own this cluster, and the evidence says so at every surface the catalog controls. (1) Terra is shown only the atomic_claim. Here that claim is {subject: 'cabinets', state: 'broken components or water staining', ontology_basis: asserted_failure}. In photo_002.jpg of redfin_125779232 an entire drawer front is missing beside the sink, leaving an open cavity into the cabinet box, with black gouges and worn-through finish on the neighbouring door fronts. A missing/detached drawer front IS a broken cabinet component, so the atomic_claim was satisfied on its own terms and Terra's rationale ('The cabinet doors and drawers shown appear intact, with no clear broken components or water staining visible') is contradicted by the image. No rewording of a claim that already covers the facts would have changed the verdict; the failure is the verification of a satisfied claim. The frozen attribution agrees independently: stage terra, attribution downstream, confidence high, with an explicit rung-by-rung check that 2a, 2b, 2c, 2d, 2e and projection all carried the condition intact. (2) The 2d-facing surfaces also behaved: name 'Damaged or Water-Stained Cabinets', description 'Cabinets with broken or damaged doors/drawers, water staining, or swollen panels', support_any ['cabinet','drawer','damaged','water stain','broken','swollen'] pulled the bullet 'Some cabinet doors and drawers appear damaged.' to rank 1 by support_phrase_hit with routing_reason exact_kind. The divergence scan finds only one promised-not-claimed token on either item ('door'), which is the description saying 'doors/drawers' where the claim says 'components' - the same objects under a generic word, a harmless naming difference, not a semantic defect. Under the 2026-09-03 catalog-first scope direction that is exactly the case that gets no_change plus documentation. (3) The only remaining catalog-flavoured observation is that cabinets_worn_finish's 'worn or faded finish on intact cabinets' qualifier is strained by the chipped bottom rail and damaged end-panel base in redfin_80990371 photo_023. That observation exists only inside a model_judge lead, which never grounds a non-deferred outcome, and the two required correct_rejection controls on that item were both adjudicated refuted, i.e. the reviewer confirmed those rejections were right, so the item is discriminating correctly in the reviewed record. Even if the boundary were moved, both siblings share trade_bucket kitchen_cabinets_counters, scene kitchen, severity 2, tier work, scope replace, work_item_code CABINET_REPLACE and a kitchen package_driver / kitchen_modernization affinity, so the change would be operationally inert while blurring a split that was made deliberately.
