### CAP-017 — Textured geometric wallcovering as dated wallpaper (hallucination-lane unit)

Status `no_change` · outcome `no_change` · confidence `medium` · items `dated_wallpaper_present`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows a visually busy textured or geometric wallcovering behind the vanity that a buyer would read as dated wallpaper; if it is paint, tile, or a non-wallpaper finish, the catalog claim's object commitment (wallpaper) is unsupported.

**Cluster.** subject: bathroom wallcovering behind vanity; mechanism: busy textured geometric wallcovering that Terra called dated wallpaper and the v1 label called a hallucination; the reviewer identified the object as a beige stick-mosaic tile accent panel; kinds: modernization; scenes: bathroom; commitments: object, scope. Unchanged singleton, kept exactly as reviewed. The claim_under_test is verbatim from the provisional cluster because the reviewer adjudicated against those exact words; re-wording it under a non-reshaped cluster would silently re-scope a pinned adjudication. No merge was available: the only other dated_style modernization cluster in the walls-ceilings family is CAP-003 (interior paint palette), which has a different physical subject (wall paint colour vs a bathroom wall tile panel), a different commitment type (scope vs object) and a different item, so pooling would have contaminated a clean four-human palette question. No split: one unit. No coverage unit was promoted into it and no unit left it, so dated_wallpaper_present remains implicated here and nowhere else.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions subject, repair, scope, route.

**Diagnosis.** primary `pass_2d_selection`; contributing terra_verification, retrieval_metadata_or_reachability. The catalog is not the thing that failed, and this cluster is the clean case for saying so. (1) The item's claim surface is precise: atomic_claim subject 'wallpaper', state 'dated or visually busy wallpaper', ontology_basis dated_style. It commits to an object, and that object commitment is exactly what the photo refutes - which is the item behaving correctly, not incoherently. (2) The item's two runtime surfaces agree: divergence_scan reports dated_wallpaper_present with promised_not_claimed [] and claimed_not_promised [], i.e. zero divergence between the retrieval text (name/description/embed_text/support_any, all of which say wallpaper and only wallpaper) and the atomic_claim Terra sees. This is the opposite of the CAP-014 / CAP-016 pattern in this family, where retrieval promises more than the claim tests. (3) The space is already partitioned three ways by subject and scene (dated_wallpaper_present / dated_bathroom_wallpaper / vintage_tile_pattern_style / outdated_bathroom_finishes), so there is no ambiguity for the ontology to resolve. (4) The false material word 'wallpaper' is not in the observation and not in Terra's rationale - it is introduced solely by the act of selecting this item. The only catalog-shaped lever available would be to widen the subject from 'wallpaper' to 'wallcovering', and that would be strictly harmful: it would manufacture overlap with dated_wood_paneling, vintage_tile_pattern_style and outdated_bathroom_finishes, and it would convert this very hallucination into a supported billing. The scope direction is explicit that a 2d selection failure is never compensated by broadening the catalog. So: no_change, plus one pass_2d_follow_ups entry.

|---|---|

The prior note said Pass 2a called a tile mosaic strip a 'wallcovering' and that Pass 2a is frozen, which would have forced deferred_insufficient_evidence with out_of_scope_pass_2a. The frozen attribution is an orchestrator revision (revised=true, reviewer claude_orchestrator, stage 2d, downstream, medium confidence) made after adversarial verification, and it records that case rc_45b9d2f6923c carries exactly ONE issue, fe4392d2f287d083, whose observation is 'Wallcovering is dated.' - material-neutral and true, since the panel genuinely is a wall covering in the generic sense and genuinely reads dated. The material-specific 2a sentences ('Wallpaper behind the vanity has an older style.', issue 325a0a38baf3b043; 'Wallpaper behind the vanity has a bamboo or raised pattern.') belong to a DIFFERENT issue that is not in this case's issue_ids, and the most material-specific of them was dropped at 2c. So no false material claim reaches this chain from 2a. Filing this as out_of_scope_pass_2a would attribute the error to a sentence that is not in the case. |
| catalog_wording_or_specificity | The claim is already at the right specificity and already names the object it means. Nothing about 'wallpaper / dated or visually busy wallpaper' is vague, incoherent, or broader than its retrieval text (divergence_scan: both directions empty). Making it narrower would change nothing here (it is already narrower than the truth); making it broader is the harmful move argued above. There is also no reviewed base for either edit: 0 positive uses and 0 correct rejections. |
| missing_catalog_coverage | vintage_tile_pattern_style ('bathroom tile', 'vintage or dated pattern, intact') and outdated_bathroom_finishes ('bathroom finishes', 'dated tile, vanity, or fixtures') both exist, are kind-compatible (modernization) and scene-compatible (bathroom). The reachability scan confirms kind_ok and scene_ok for both. Coverage exists; selection failed. |

The hard guardrails even enforce the boundary - dated_bathroom_wallpaper's require_any demands a wallpaper token and vintage_tile_pattern_style's require_any demands a tile token, so on this material-neutral observation both correctly stayed out of the candidate list. That is a clean ontology being applied to an under-specified input, not an ambiguous one. |

The defect is which item was handed to them. Note in passing that the selected item carries no bathroom entry in package_affinity (only bedroom, kitchen, living), so a bathroom hit bills a standalone WALLPAPER_REMOVE_PAINT and joins no package - a consequence of the selection, and in any case package_affinity is never natively authorable. |

| question | answer |
|---|---|
| existing_item_exists | Yes, two of them. outdated_bathroom_finishes was IN the frozen top-8 at rank 6 with kind_ok true and scene_ok true. vintage_tile_pattern_style is the most exact concept for the true object (subject 'bathroom tile', state 'vintage or dated pattern, intact', TILE_UPDATE) and is kind- and scene-compatible, but rank null - it was not retrieved. dated_bathroom_wallpaper also exists for genuine bathroom wallpaper and correctly stayed out. |
| upstream_kind_wrong | No. Pass 2c assigned kind modernization; the resolved item's kind is modernization and routing_reason is exact_kind; and the true reading (a dated-style tile accent) is also a modernization, not a defect or degradation. The kind gate admitted every plausible alternative here - reachability_scan shows kind_ok true for dated_wallpaper_present, outdated_bathroom_finishes and vintage_tile_pattern_style alike. Unlike CAP-001 in this family, no kind gate excluded the better item. |
| scene_or_retrieval_metadata_cause | Partly, and it explains the absence of the single most exact item, but it is not actionable. vintage_tile_pattern_style carries require_any ['tile','tiles','tiled'], a HARD guardrail; the observation 'Wallcovering is dated.' contains no tile token, so the one item whose subject matches the truth was filtered out before ranking. dated_bathroom_wallpaper's require_any (wallpaper tokens) removed it the same way. Both guardrails behaved as designed on a deliberately material-neutral input - they are the reason the catalog did not double-bill, not a defect. Two further facts close the lever: require_any is NOT natively authorable on a carryover entry (vintage_tile_pattern_style is change_type reclassified with a single successor), so relaxing it would be a migration-system gap rather than a proposal; and it would be the wrong fix anyway, since loosening a tile guardrail to catch tile-free wording is precisely how neighbours start stealing each other's hits. Scene metadata is not implicated: the scene was bathroom and every relevant item lists bathroom. |
| retrieved_but_2d_selected_other | Yes - this is the decisive finding. outdated_bathroom_finishes sat at rank 6 of the frozen top-8 with kind and scene both open, and 2d (resolution_path llm) selected dated_wallpaper_present at rank 5. The observation it was resolving, 'Wallcovering is dated.', names no material; the selected item's claim_text ('wallpaper dated or visually busy wallpaper') supplies the material word 'wallpaper' that the photo then refutes. Right item reachable, another selected: one pass_2d_follow_ups entry, catalog unchanged. |
| terra_adjudicated_wrong_detail | Yes, in the specific sense that Terra never tested the object commitment it was given. Terra returned 'supported' with the rationale 'A visually busy textured geometric wallcovering is visible behind the vanity.' That sentence is true and material-neutral - it describes the panel accurately and never asserts wallpaper - yet the atomic_claim Terra was verifying has subject 'wallpaper'. Terra confirmed the state limb ('dated or visually busy') and silently passed the subject limb. This is a contributing cause, not the primary one: had 2d resolved to outdated_bathroom_finishes or vintage_tile_pattern_style, the same rationale would have been an honest confirmation. Recorded as a Terra-calibration observation (verify the claim's subject, not only its state), adjacent to the CAP-003 calibration finding in this family but resting on a single unit. |

Old/patterned wallpaper. Remove and repaint walls.', support_any all wallpaper terms) and the Terra surface (atomic_claim subject 'wallpaper') say the same thing. Under convention 1 there is no surface mismatch here to own. Every one of those surfaces in fact WARNED 2d off this observation. |
| steals_neighbors_or_damages_successes | Not applicable - ops is empty, so nothing can be stolen or damaged. Recorded because it is the reason ops is empty: the only catalog edit anyone would reach for (widening the subject from 'wallpaper' to 'wallcovering' or 'wall finish') would overlap dated_wood_paneling (subject 'wall paneling', same five scene groups), vintage_tile_pattern_style and outdated_bathroom_finishes, and would flip this very unit from a caught hallucination into a supported billing. That is the textbook broadening-to-cover-a-2d-miss the scope direction forbids. |
| alters_eligibility_package_economics | Not applicable - ops is empty, so no eligibility, tier, route or package field changes. For context only, and not as a reason for the outcome: the mis-selection has a real economic shape, because dated_wallpaper_present has no bathroom key in package_affinity (bedroom, kitchen, living only) and prices a standalone WALLPAPER_REMOVE_PAINT at severity 2 / trade interior_finishes, whereas outdated_bathroom_finishes is the bathroom package_driver at severity 3 with cost_model room_allowance and trade bathroom_fixtures_tile. Per the scope direction dollar effects are context only; this judgment rests on the false object commitment, not on the amount. |

**Semantic before after.** No change proposed. dated_wallpaper_present keeps atomic_claim subject 'wallpaper', state 'dated or visually busy wallpaper', ontology_basis dated_style, kind modernization, severity 2, trade_bucket interior_finishes, tier work, scope repair, scene_groups [kitchen, bathroom, bedroom, living_areas, utility], route_override null, work_item_code WALLPAPER_REMOVE_PAINT, deny_any ['peeling paint','mold$'], require_any null, migration entry change_type reclassified with the single successor dated_wallpaper_present, and its clean divergence pair (promised_not_claimed [], claimed_not_promised []). Its neighbours dated_bathroom_wallpaper, vintage_tile_pattern_style and outdated_bathroom_finishes are likewise untouched, so the subject-based boundary between generic wallpaper, bathroom wallpaper, bathroom tile pattern and bathroom finishes is preserved exactly as it stands.

**Retrieval effects.** None. No embed_text, name, description, support_any, deny_any or require_any is touched on any item, so no item's rank changes anywhere. This is also the only verifiable option: embed_text is the entire embedding input when set, there is no offline embedding store, and dated_wallpaper_present's embed_text ('Dated wallpaper present. Old/patterned wallpaper. Remove and repaint walls.') is the one surface that already correctly warns 2d away from a tile panel. The two hard guardrails that shaped this candidate list - vintage_tile_pattern_style's require_any ['tile','tiles','tiled'] and dated_bathroom_wallpaper's require_any on wallpaper tokens - are left exactly as they are; both behaved correctly here, and neither is natively authorable on a carryover entry in any case.

**Economics fit.** Not applicable - no ops, and economics fields (cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope) are never natively authorable in any event. Recorded for context only: the mis-selection billed WALLPAPER_REMOVE_PAINT at severity 2 under trade interior_finishes and was accepted for work (disposition accepted_for_work, reason_code route_work), whereas the reachable alternative outdated_bathroom_finishes is severity 3, trade bathroom_fixtures_tile, cost_model room_allowance. Per the scope direction, dollar effects are context only; this judgment rests on the false object commitment, not on the amount, and no dollar delta is claimed because the condition's billability was never ruled on.

**Package route effects.** None. No route_override is proposed (its only legal value is no_action, and nothing here argues the condition is non-actionable), no trade_bucket, tier or scope changes, and no package_affinity changes. Context worth recording for the 2d follow-up rather than for the catalog: dated_wallpaper_present's package_affinity has bedroom, kitchen and living keys but NO bathroom key, so a bathroom hit on this item produces a standalone work item that joins no bathroom_modernization package, while outdated_bathroom_finishes is that package's driver. The routing asymmetry is therefore a further operational consequence of choosing rank 5 over rank 6, and it is one more reason not to widen this item's subject to cover bathroom wall finishes: doing so would route bathroom finish work through an item that cannot package it.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. The implicated item's trade_bucket is interior_finishes, not the product-quarantined electrical trade, so no quarantine rule is engaged and nothing forces non_catalog_action. (dated_electrical_outlets_switches sat at rank 1 in this candidate list, but it was not selected, is not implicated, and is not part of this cluster.) The no_change outcome is chosen on the evidence and the diagnosis, not by policy.

**Expected benefit.** Documentary, and deliberately routed out of the catalog. Four things are recorded and nothing is edited: (1) one pass_2d_follow_ups entry naming a reachable, kind- and scene-compatible better item at rank 6 with a concrete selection rule - prefer the subject-general item when the observation names no material; (2) a negative result that closes the catalog lever for this item with evidence rather than by omission, namely that dated_wallpaper_present is one of the 42 of 128 items with zero divergence in both directions, so the CAP-014 / CAP-016 retrieval-promises-more-than-the-claim pattern does NOT apply here; (3) a Terra-calibration observation that the state limb was verified while the subject limb was not, adjacent to the CAP-003 calibration finding; (4) an explicit correction of the prior hypothesis, so no later reader re-files this as a frozen Pass 2a error. The net effect on the pipeline is fewer hallucinations only if the 2d follow-up is worked; the catalog contribution is to have proved that broadening it would produce MORE.

**Regression risk.** Zero from this judgment, since ops is empty. The risk this judgment avoids is the substantive point, and it is unusually sharp here. The tempting edit - widen the atomic_claim subject from 'wallpaper' to 'wallcovering' or 'wall finish' so the claim matches what Terra actually saw - would flip this very unit from a caught false billing into a supported one, because Terra's rationale ('a visually busy textured geometric wallcovering') would then verify the claim exactly. It would also overlap dated_wood_paneling (subject 'wall paneling', identical five scene groups), vintage_tile_pattern_style and outdated_bathroom_finishes, i.e. steal from three neighbours at once. And with 0 positive uses and 0 correct rejections for this item across the whole review, that edit would ship with no control able to detect the damage: a regression_controls list would be vacuously complete. Broadening to cover a 2d miss, with no control base, on one v1-only unit, is the exact failure mode the scope direction forbids.

**Post-catalog Pass 2d follow-ups.**

| case | selected | better item | rank | family | note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rc_45b9d2f6923c | dated_wallpaper_present | outdated_bathroom_finishes | 6 | bathroom_dated_finish_subject | Confusion family, stated in full: bathroom dated-finish subject confusion: a material-neutral 'wallcovering/finish is dated' observation resolved to a material-specific item that supplies an object word the observation never contained Issue fe4392d2f287d083, observation 'Wallcovering is dated.', scene bathroom, kind modernization, resolution_path llm, routing_reason exact_kind. Frozen top-8: dated_electrical_outlets_switches 1, dated_wood_paneling 2, vanity_countertop_dated 3, dated_window_treatment_valance 4, dated_wallpaper_present 5, outdated_bathroom_finishes 6, older_flooring_style 7, dated_bathroom_flooring_style 8. 2d took rank 5 over rank 6 although reachability_scan shows outdated_bathroom_finishes with kind_ok true and scene_ok true. The selection rule to examine: when the observation names no material (wallcovering, finish, surface), 2d should prefer the subject-general item (outdated_bathroom_finishes, subject 'bathroom finishes') over any item whose atomic_claim subject asserts a specific material (wallpaper, paneling, tile), because Terra will be asked to verify that material word and the observation gives no basis for it. Note for whoever works this: the most exact item for the true object, vintage_tile_pattern_style (subject 'bathroom tile'), was NOT in the candidate list at all - its require_any ['tile','tiles','tiled'] hard-gated it against a tile-free observation - so 2d could not have chosen it and this follow-up asks only for the rank-6 item it could have chosen. Single occurrence, one property, one photo; treat as a hypothesis to test against other neutral-observation cases, not as a measured rate. |

**Unresolved questions.** Is the condition billable at all under convention 3/4, and if so under which item? The reviewer's note says 'I believe it is a backsplash not wallpaper. But it does look dated', but human_truth.basis is v1_only with work and class_v1_1 null, so no warranted/absent ruling exists for this case. I opened photo_019.jpg once for this question: the accent panel is a cream/beige stacked stick-mosaic tile, floor-to-ceiling on the mirror wall, and the room also shows a four-bulb bell-shade Hollywood bar light, a beige cultured-marble vanity top and an espresso vanity with visibly chipped drawer fronts. To my eye those are late-2000s builder tells that would plausibly support outdated_bathroom_finishes ('dated tile, vanity, or fixtures'), while the tile panel alone under vintage_tile_pattern_style ('vintage or dated pattern, INTACT') is weaker because a stick mosaic is not era-specific. I record this as disagreement-shaped uncertainty only - it changes no adjudication and no outcome, since either way dated_wallpaper_present is the wrong item., If the follow-up is worked, is outdated_bathroom_finishes actually the better item, or is it merely the best REACHABLE one? vintage_tile_pattern_style is the more exact subject match but was hard-gated out by require_any ['tile','tiles','tiled'] on a material-neutral observation. Deciding this needs a 2d-side judgment about whether a neutral observation should be allowed to reach a material-specific item at all; my follow-up entry names outdated_bathroom_finishes because that is the item 2d could actually have chosen., Does this item have any correct behaviour anywhere? Promotion recipe for anyone who later wants to touch dated_wallpaper_present: human-review the corpus cases where it was resolved and Terra supported it, and specifically look for genuine wallpaper hits in bathroom scenes (where dated_bathroom_wallpaper should have won instead) to test whether the generic item is quietly out-competing the scene-specific one. Two supports_claim verdicts on distinct properties would lift a future cluster to two_independent_human; without that, and without a single correct rejection, no edit to this item is defensible., Is the Terra subject-limb observation general? Terra confirmed 'dated or visually busy' while never testing 'wallpaper'. One unit cannot establish that Terra systematically verifies the state limb and not the subject limb of an atomic_claim, but it is the same shape as the CAP-003 finding (Terra rejecting on the 'highly personalized' limb without testing 'dated') and the two together would be worth a Terra-calibration probe.

**Human disposition.** _blank_ (approved / approved_with_modification / rejected / deferred / reclassified_non_catalog)

## Appendix B — deferred and provisional clusters

### CAP-010 — Fence weathering with leaning sections (lead only)

Status `deferred_insufficient_evidence` · outcome `deferred_insufficient_evidence` · confidence `medium` · items `fence_weathered` · deferral `lead_only`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows a fence that is not merely weathered but has leaning, detached, or failing sections, which the catalog claim (fence weathered) does not name.

**Cluster.** subject: wood fence; mechanism: weathered fence with leaning or failing sections resolved to a weathering item; kinds: degradation; scenes: exterior; commitments: mechanism, severity. Unchanged from the provisional cluster: a singleton carried by one factorized lead, kept separate from porch and patio surfaces (CAP-009) because the physical subject (fence) and the mechanism under test (structural lean versus surface aging) both differ, and separate from the siding/masonry/casing clusters (CAP-002 / CAP-018 / CAP-019) which sit in different trades. There is no other unit anywhere in the frozen review that touches either fence item, so there is nothing to merge with. The two constituent issues (photo_017 'The wood fence appears weathered', photo_016 'The rear and right wood fencing appears aged') stay ONE unit per convention 2 - several issues on one condition are one evidence unit.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions mechanism, severity, repair.

**Human inputs.**

| ref | source | ruling | effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| Convention 1 (PHASE_C_BRIEF section 2) | Steven's ruling | Strictest claim-bearing wording governs semantic fit, and Terra is shown only the atomic_claim while 2d and retrieval see name/description/embed_text/support_any. | Fixes 'weathered or faded boards, structurally sound' as the governing surface for this cluster, which is why the mismatch is classified operationally_consequential rather than a naming quibble - and equally why the clause must not be softened, since it is the only surface that can reject a leaning fence. |
| Convention 2 (PHASE_C_BRIEF section 2) | Steven's ruling | Judge multi-photo conditions at the characteristic issue level and never multiply units. | The two fence issues on photo_016 and photo_017 stay a single evidence unit; the cluster is a genuine singleton and cannot be inflated to two units to manufacture a bar. |
| Scope direction 2026-09-03 (PHASE_C_BRIEF section 3) | Steven's direction | Catalog-first; Pass 2a is frozen and Pass-2a-owned findings are deferred, not fixed by catalog changes; never compensate for a 2d selection failure by broadening the catalog; prefer the smallest lever. | Drives the diagnosis to upstream_pass_2a_observation (the observations never reported the lean) and rules out every catalog lever, independently of the evidence bar. |

**Diagnosis.** primary `upstream_pass_2a_observation`; contributing retrieval_metadata_or_reachability, terra_verification. On the evidence available the catalog does NOT own this, and that conclusion is robust in both directions of the unresolved visual question. The two frozen Pass 2a observations for this condition are 'The wood fence appears weathered.' (photo_017) and 'The rear and right wood fencing appears aged.' (photo_016). Neither mentions leaning, a bowed rail or a displaced picket. Everything downstream then behaved correctly ON THAT INPUT: 2c classified degradation (correct for 'appears weathered'/'appears aged'); retrieval returned fence_weathered at rank 1 in both lists on routing_reason exact_kind; 2d resolved to it (issue 7902b3460b936c65 by lexical_shortcut on support_phrase_hit, issue 9365abaf671415fe by llm). The leaning is a fact about the photo that the factorized lead reports and that Pass 2a never reported. So EITHER the lean is real, in which case the lost structural aspect originates at Pass 2a - which is frozen and out of scope by the 2026-09-03 direction - OR the lean is not there, in which case there is no finding at all. Neither branch yields a catalog lever, and the deferral is forced independently by the evidence bar. The one catalog-shaped observation worth recording is a guardrail working as designed rather than a defect: fence_weathered's atomic_claim embeds a POSITIVE structural commitment ('weathered or faded boards, structurally sound') inside a degradation item, and per convention 1 that clause is the only surface Terra sees. A leaning-but-weathered fence should therefore be REJECTED by Terra on that clause, which is precisely how the split is meant to protect itself. The item's divergence entry (promised_not_claimed = ['stain_discolor'], claimed_not_promised = []) is not implicated by this unit. Do NOT propose dropping or softening 'structurally sound': it is the load-bearing discriminator of the split, removing it would let fence_weathered absorb its own defect sibling's subject matter, and with zero controls on either fence item such a change would be completely untestable.

Unanswerable from the frozen evidence: this unit has no case record, so only the lead's stored_verdict 'supported' is visible. Corroboration counts as independent only when the lead shares neither (property, photo) nor (property, condition_id) with a human unit; a human unit created on this very photo and condition would share both, making the corroboration 'method' and leaving the bar at none. What that review WOULD buy is real but smaller: one human/gold unit, which makes the cluster refutation-backed and unlocks no_change / non_catalog_action / migration_system_gap - i.e. it converts a forced deferral into a documented closure. To reach a bar the cheapest path is instead ONE human unit on a DIFFERENT property where a leaning or failing fence was billed to fence_weathered: the redfin_10952874 lead would then corroborate it independently (it shares no property with it), giving one_human_plus_independent_corroboration. Two such human units on two properties would give two_independent_human with no correlation_note needed. Note also that the frozen gold coverage corpus cannot supply this - all 63 coverage rows sit on just two properties (redfin_10806500, redfin_11000447) and none concerns a fence., Should a fence that is simultaneously weathered and leaning produce one condition on the defect sibling or two conditions across both? ### CAP-014 — Wall damage beyond scuffs: staining, cracks, missing material, patches, graffiti

Status `deferred_insufficient_evidence` · outcome `deferred_insufficient_evidence` · confidence `high` · items `wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents`, `damaged_drywall_or_cracks` · deferral `lead_only`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows wall damage that is more than scuffs, marks, or dents (water staining, cracks, missing material, unpainted patches, torn paneling, graffiti, cutouts), and the resolved catalog item commits to minor scuffs/marks/dents.

**Cluster.** subject: interior walls and ceilings, masonry walls; mechanism: water staining, cracks, missing wall material, unpainted patches, torn paneling, graffiti, cutouts resolved to a scuffs/marks/dents item; kinds: degradation, defect; scenes: living_areas, bedroom, utility, bathroom; commitments: mechanism, severity. Kept exactly as reviewed: no unit moved in or out, no split, no merge, no coverage row promoted, claim_under_test verbatim. The 14 units share one physical subject (interior wall surface), one commitment boundary (minor-use marks vs a heavier mechanism) and one resolved item family (wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents, with damaged_drywall_or_cracks on the single unit where 2d chose the heavier item). Splitting the staining units from the crack/missing-material units was considered and rejected: it would produce two lead-only fragments, neither of which could ever exceed bar none, and both fragments turn on the same 2d preference for wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents. Per convention 2 the multi-issue units are single units (redfin_11077450 photo_020 carries graffiti + patch/roller marks as one condition; redfin_166147710 oc1_da54345f spans photo_038 and photo_042 as one condition).

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions mechanism, severity, scope, repair.

**Evidence bar.** recomputed `none` (claimed `none`): 0 human/gold, 10 lead, 0 properties, 0 correlated, corroboration `none`, contextual `{"refuted":3,"unclear":1}`; reviewed refutations 1 (lead 2), mixed signal `False`. Recounted by hand from the 14 unit adjudications and it matches index.json (bar none, human 0, lead 10, props 0, refutations 1, mixed_signal false). Counted units are role=support with adjudication exactly supports_claim: there are 10, and every one of them has evidence_class model_judge (the factorized verifier lead). Human/gold counted units = 0, so distinct properties among counted human units = 0 and there is no ladder rung to climb: not two_independent_human, not one_human_plus_independent_corroboration, and structural_exception is not claimed (it would require at least one counted human/gold unit, which does not exist, and would in any case be the wrong instrument here). The cluster's only human/gold unit is gold:redfin_10806500:photo_005.jpg:g4, adjudicated REFUTED with photos available, which makes reviewed_refutations = 1 and makes the cluster refutation-backed - so no_change would also have been legal. I chose deferred_insufficient_evidence over that fallback because the underlying pattern is live and specific rather than closed: the 2d preference for wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents is visible in the frozen ranks independently of the lead verdicts, and a documented deferral with a concrete promotion recipe preserves it, whereas no_change would assert the question is settled on evidence that never tested it. The remaining 3 units are lead refuted (2) and lead unclear (1); refuted and unclear are facts and are never re-read as support. Per the brief a lead never satisfies the bar and never grounds a non-deferred outcome, so no proposal, no coverage-gap declaration and no non-catalog action can rest on these 10 supports.

**Diagnosis.** primary `pass_2d_selection`; contributing catalog_wording_or_specificity, retrieval_metadata_or_reachability, upstream_pass_2a_observation. The catalog's share of this cluster is real but secondary, and it is not large enough to act on today. What the catalog owns: wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents shows a genuine two-surface split (divergence_scan: promised_not_claimed = [damage_break, stain_discolor]). The surfaces 2d and retrieval see promise damage and staining - description 'Walls show visible scuffs, stains, dents, or minor damage indicating heavy use or deferred maintenance', embed_text 'Wall scuffs, marks, dents, or localized staining ... smudges, dents, dirty areas, or minor staining', support_any containing 'stain' and 'stained' - while the only surface Terra sees, the atomic_claim, is the narrow 'scuffs, marks, or minor dents from use'. The item's own deny_any (['mold$','mildew','water stain']) proves the catalog author already intended water staining to be out of scope, but the guardrail is lexical and every paraphrase in this cluster ('heavy staining', 'stained or discolored', 'discoloration') passed straight through it. So the catalog does help 2d read the scuffs item as the stain item. What the catalog does NOT own: the selection itself. On all 13 issues belonging to the 10 lead-supported units, peeling_or_discolored_paint sat inside the frozen top-8 (rank 1 once, rank 2 ten times, rank 5 once, rank 6 once) with kind_ok and scene_ok true, and 2d took wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents anyway; on two of those issues it took wall_scuffs from a LOWER rank than the better wall/paint item (redfin_125970550 issue b03567bd7ca1e07b, wall_scuffs rank 2 over peeling rank 1; redfin_166147710 issue 8ce15be3647f21b6, wall_scuffs rank 3 over bathroom_paint_peeling_or_worn rank 1 and bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn rank 2). That lower-rank preference is a frozen structural fact that does not depend on any lead verdict being correct, which is why pass_2d_selection is the primary cause. Under the 2026-09-03 scope direction that is a pass_2d_follow_ups entry and explicitly not a licence to broaden the catalog. The smallest catalog lever that would ever be worth trying is therefore NOT the atomic_claim but the 2d-facing description override on wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents (remove 'stains ... or minor damage'): because embed_text is non-empty it is the entire embedding input, so name and description do not move rankings, meaning a description edit changes only what the 2d selection prompt reads and leaves Terra's claim and all retrieval ranks untouched. It stays deferred because the human evidence points the other way: 6 of the 7 reviewed controls are positive uses of wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents, all human truth claim=exact/work=warranted, and two of them are exactly the stain/patch language such an edit would remove (rc_9e57092c89dd, a required control, 'tan-brown streaky stains and marks down to the baseboard'; rc_a2c0f92f2c30, 'grey smudges, dark streaks, white smears and patched areas'). Humans have therefore endorsed billing stain-like wall marks under this item at the low end, and the single gold/human unit in the cluster refutes the claim outright by showing the item used correctly when only minor marks exist.

| rejected owner | why |
|---|---|
| terra_verification | Terra failed on exactly one unit here and it is the unit that refutes the cluster claim, not one that supports it. On gold:redfin_10806500:photo_005.jpg:g4 the frozen attribution is downstream/terra (medium confidence): 2d resolved correctly to wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents at rank 1, Terra returned unsupported by scoping its rationale to the marks 'near the windows', which really are decals, and missed the small marks and fastener holes on the right wall. The reviewer confirms both halves. That is a Terra localisation miss on a correctly named minor-marks condition - it argues the catalog item was right, not that it under-describes. For the 10 lead-supported units Terra's stored verdict is 'supported', so no Terra failure is even alleged. |
| upstream_pass_2a_observation | Recorded as a contributing cause, not the owner, and Pass 2a is frozen in any case. On at least three units 2a's own text understates what the reviewer saw and therefore weakens the retrieval query: redfin_80925528 photo_005 'The walls have marks and uneven surfaces' for a crack plus missing plaster; redfin_11185681 photo_027 'Walls have scuffs, small marks, and inconsistent paint' for paint chipped to substrate; redfin_80925528 photo_044 'The wall finish behind the sink appears patched' for paint peeled away to bare plaster. But 2a understatement cannot explain the pattern, because the units where 2a was accurate landed on the same item: 'The left wall has graffiti', 'Wall surfaces have heavy staining and deteriorated wallboard or paneling' and 'Ceiling and upper walls have heavy staining and discoloration' all resolved to wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents. Per the scope direction, any finding that were genuinely 2a-owned would be deferred out_of_scope_pass_2a; this one is not. |
| upstream_kind_or_pass_2c | Ruled out on frozen data. reachability_scan reports kind_ok true and scene_ok true for every hypothesised better item (damaged_drywall_or_cracks, peeling_or_discolored_paint, water_stain_ceiling, visible_mold_or_mildew) on every issue in the cluster. Nothing was gated out by 2c kind or by scene_groups, so the better items were selectable throughout. |
| downstream_tier_projection_routing_package_costing | Downstream behaved consistently with the item it was handed. wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents is severity 1, scope cosmetic, tier work, defaultHidden true, DRYWALL_PATCH, package_support in all four rooms; that projection is correct FOR that item. The under-billing and under-display that the leads point at is entirely inherited from the item choice at 2d, so downstream is a consequence, not a cause. |
| product_policy_or_quarantine | Not applicable. Both implicated items sit in trade_bucket paint_drywall; neither is in the quarantined electrical trade and neither carries a route_override or a pricing_status flag, so nothing here is forced to non_catalog_action by product policy. |

**Alternative-stage analysis.**

| question | answer |
|---|---|
| existing_item_exists | Yes, for every mechanism except graffiti. peeling_or_discolored_paint is the intended home for wall staining and paint loss (embed_text explicitly names 'Lower-wall moisture staining, tide marks, and discoloration near baseboards or base of walls'); damaged_drywall_or_cracks covers cracks, holes, patches and 'deteriorated wallboard'; water_stain_ceiling covers ceiling moisture staining (require_any ['ceiling'], severity 3, MOISTURE_REMEDIATION); bathroom_paint_peeling_or_worn covers bathroom wall/ceiling paint; interior_wall_stripped_to_studs and unfinished_interior_wall_osb_exposed cover missing wall material. The string 'graffiti' appears in none of the 128 items; its repair (prime and repaint) is nevertheless carried by peeling_or_discolored_paint's PAINT_INTERIOR. |
| upstream_kind_wrong | No. Every unit's 2c kind (degradation or defect) left the better items open: reachability_scan shows kind_ok true and scene_ok true for damaged_drywall_or_cracks, peeling_or_discolored_paint, water_stain_ceiling and visible_mold_or_mildew on all 20 issues in the cluster. This cluster has no gating story of the kind found in CAP-001, where paint_refresh_recommended was kind-gated out on every unit. |
| scene_or_retrieval_metadata_cause | Partly, and only for one item. damaged_drywall_or_cracks never entered the frozen top-8 on any of the 15 wall-surface issues that resolved to wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents, although its kind and scene were open and its support_any literally contains 'patch', 'uneven', 'crack' and 'hole' - the exact words in observations such as 'The walls have marks and uneven surfaces', 'The central wall has multiple visible patch areas' and 'A wall patch is visible near the outlet'. Because embed_text is non-empty it is the whole embedding input and support_any is only a soft hint plus the annotator search index, so this is a retrieval-reachability question about damaged_drywall_or_cracks' embed_text. It cannot be verified offline: there is no offline embedding store, and editing embed_text would move that item's rank on every query in the corpus. Scene metadata is not implicated: both implicated items carry all five interior scene groups. |
| retrieved_but_2d_selected_other | Yes, and this is the dominant stage. On all 13 issues belonging to the 10 lead-supported units a better wall/paint item was inside the frozen top-8 and 2d chose wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents instead (peeling_or_discolored_paint rank 1 once, rank 2 ten times, rank 5 once, rank 6 once; bathroom_paint_peeling_or_worn rank 1 on the bathroom issue). On two of the thirteen 2d reached DOWN the list for the scuffs item - issue b03567bd7ca1e07b took rank 2 over peeling at rank 1, issue 8ce15be3647f21b6 took rank 3 over bathroom_paint_peeling_or_worn at rank 1 - which shows an active preference for the scuffs item rather than a rank-1 default. Recorded as pass_2d_follow_ups; per the scope direction the catalog stays unchanged. Minor recount correction to the family structure step, which reported '12 of 13 ... rank 1 twice, rank 2 nine times': recounting the 13 supported-unit issues gives 13 of 13 in the top-8 with rank 1 once, rank 2 ten times, rank 5 once, rank 6 once (the second rank-1 case, issue f66edce9e86fb7d7, belongs to the unclear unit and should not be counted among the supported issues). The conclusion is unchanged and slightly strengthened. |
| terra_adjudicated_wrong_detail | Once, on the refuting gold unit only. gold:redfin_10806500:photo_005.jpg:g4 - Terra returned unsupported on a correctly resolved wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents condition (oc1_32bf8869e625ef1f) because it answered only for the wall 'near the windows', where the shapes really are decorative decals, and never looked at the right wall where the gold's small marks and fastener holes are; frozen attribution downstream/terra, medium confidence, with the 2a localiser named as the contributing factor. All 10 lead-supported units carry a stored Terra verdict of 'supported', so no Terra failure is alleged on the supporting side of this cluster. |
| wording_creates_unsupported_commitments | Yes on both implicated items, and this is the one genuinely catalog-owned fact in the cluster. wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents: divergence_scan promised_not_claimed = [damage_break, stain_discolor]; the retrieval/2d surfaces promise stains and 'minor damage' while the atomic claim Terra is shown says only 'scuffs, marks, or minor dents from use', and the item's own deny_any (['mold$','mildew','water stain']) shows the exclusion was intended but is lexical, so paraphrases such as 'heavy staining' and 'stained or discolored' pass through. damaged_drywall_or_cracks: promised_not_claimed = [missing_incomplete]; its embed promises 'missing sections, or deteriorated wallboard' while its claim subject is 'drywall' and its state is 'cracks, holes, or impact damage' - which is exactly the mismatch the reviewer flagged on the refuted unit redfin_166147710 oc1_5a69289015183dbc (the material is painted paneling, not drywall). Both are real defects under convention 1, and neither has a single human/gold supports_claim unit behind it today. |
| steals_neighbors_or_damages_successes | Both directions have live reviewed controls against them, which is the strongest single reason not to act now. Broadening the atomic claim to cover staining/damage would steal from peeling_or_discolored_paint, damaged_drywall_or_cracks and water_stain_ceiling - and damaged_drywall_or_cracks has its own reviewed success (rc_8c3e76880064, redfin_81000709 photo_028, truth exact/warranted) - while also making it easier to bill a condition the gold unit shows should be minor. Narrowing the retrieval or 2d-facing wording to push staining away would put the reviewed successes of wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents at risk: 6 positive-use controls, all truth exact/warranted, including the required rc_9e57092c89dd whose reviewer text is 'tan-brown streaky stains and marks down to the baseboard ... they are visible marks though they read as staining rather than scuffs' and rc_a2c0f92f2c30 'walls covered in grey smudges, dark streaks, white smears and patched areas'. Humans have thus endorsed stain-like and patch-like wall marks under this item at the low end. There is also no hallucination or correct-rejection control on either item (0 and 0 in per_item_counts), so no broadening could be shown safe even if the bar were met. |
| alters_eligibility_package_economics | Yes, materially, which is why the mismatch is classed operationally_consequential rather than equivalent. wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents is severity 1, scope cosmetic, defaultHidden true, work_item_code DRYWALL_PATCH, and package_support (with repair_support_when_driven true) in bathroom, bedroom, kitchen and living. The items the leads say should have been chosen sit in different economic positions: damaged_drywall_or_cracks is severity 2, scope repair, defaultHidden false, and a package_driver for bedroom_repair and living_repair; water_stain_ceiling is severity 3, MOISTURE_REMEDIATION, and a driver for bedroom_repair and living_repair; peeling_or_discolored_paint is PAINT_INTERIOR rather than DRYWALL_PATCH and is not defaultHidden. So a wrong choice here changes severity, display, work item, and whether a repair package has a driver at all - and under the catalog 3.2 contextual-repair rule it also changes whether wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents itself becomes billable support inside a driven repair package. That cuts both ways: the same sensitivity means an under-evidenced wording change would move real money and real package eligibility, so deferral is the conservative call. |

The reviewer's own words - 'tan-brown streaky stains and marks down to the baseboard ... they are visible marks though they read as staining rather than scuffs' - make this the single control most likely to flip if the stain language is removed from the item's 2d-facing surfaces. **Semantic before after.** No change proposed. Recorded for the promotion path only: wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents keeps atomic_claim state 'scuffs, marks, or minor dents from use' (subject 'walls', ontology_basis dent_without_functional_impact), description 'Walls show visible scuffs, stains, dents, or minor damage indicating heavy use or deferred maintenance.', embed_text 'Wall scuffs, marks, dents, or localized staining. Visible wall wear such as scuffs, smudges, dents, dirty areas, or minor staining. Patch, prep, and repaint as needed.' and deny_any ['mold$','mildew','water stain']; damaged_drywall_or_cracks keeps atomic_claim state 'cracks, holes, or impact damage' (subject 'drywall') and embed_text naming 'missing sections, or deteriorated wallboard'. The candidate future edit, if the promotion recipe returns two human supports, is a description-only override on wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents removing 'stains' and 'or minor damage' so the 2d selection prompt stops reading it as the stain item; that is native (legacy_id wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents, a reclassified carryover with a single successor, /overrides/description) and it is embedding-neutral because embed_text is non-empty and is therefore the entire embedding input. It is NOT proposed now.

**Retrieval effects.** None - nothing is changed. For the record: no edit contemplated here would move any embedding rank, because embed_text is left untouched and name/description do not feed the embedding while embed_text is set. The separate question of why damaged_drywall_or_cracks never enters the top-8 for crack/patch observations would require an embed_text edit, which cannot be verified offline (there is no offline embedding store) and would change that item's rank on every query in the corpus; that is exactly why it is left as an unresolved question rather than an op.

**Economics fit.** Unchanged. wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents stays severity 1, scope cosmetic, tier work, defaultHidden true, work_item_code DRYWALL_PATCH; damaged_drywall_or_cracks stays severity 2, scope repair, defaultHidden false, work_item_code DRYWALL_PATCH. No economics field is authorable in the decisions file in any case (cost, estimate, work_item_code, cost_model, package_affinity, package_role, estimate_scope are never native), so any change in this area would be a migration-system gap, not a proposal.

**Package route effects.** None - nothing is changed. Context for the promotion path: wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents is package_support with repair_support_when_driven true in bathroom, bedroom, kitchen and living; damaged_drywall_or_cracks is a package_driver for bedroom_repair and living_repair and support in kitchen/bathroom modernization; water_stain_ceiling is a driver for bedroom_repair and living_repair. Moving wall-staining observations between these items therefore changes whether a repair package acquires a driver, and under the catalog 3.2 contextual-repair rule it changes whether wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents itself becomes billable support inside a driven repair package. Neither item carries a route_override, so no routing change is contemplated and no_action is not in play.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. Both items are trade_bucket paint_drywall; neither is in the product-quarantined electrical trade, neither has a pricing_status flag or a cost_model, so nothing here is forced to non_catalog_action by product policy.

**Expected benefit.** None claimed, because nothing is proposed. The value delivered by this cluster is the record: 12 pass_2d_follow_ups entries (10 2d-selection cases, of which 8 are the scuffs-vs-paint family, plus 2 flagged retrieval-reachability) naming one dominant confusion family (2d prefers wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents over the better wall/paint item, twice reaching down the ranked list to do so), one documented catalog surface-split on wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents with the smallest viable lever identified (description override, embedding-neutral), one retrieval-reachability question on damaged_drywall_or_cracks, and a concrete four-photo promotion recipe. If the recipe returned two human supports on distinct properties the expected benefit would be fewer real wall-damage conditions billed at severity 1 cosmetic and hidden by default - but even then the likely landing is a 2d or retrieval action rather than a claim rewrite.

**Regression risk.** The risk of acting today is concrete and one-sided, which is why the outcome is deferred. Narrowing the wall_scuffs surfaces risks 6 reviewed positive uses, all truth exact/warranted, two of which are explicitly stain-flavoured (rc_9e57092c89dd, required; rc_a2c0f92f2c30). Broadening the atomic claim risks stealing from peeling_or_discolored_paint, damaged_drywall_or_cracks (which has its own reviewed success, rc_8c3e76880064) and water_stain_ceiling, and would make it easier to bill the very case the gold unit shows should stay minor - with zero hallucination or correct-rejection controls on either item to prove the broadening safe. The risk of deferring is that 10 lead-flagged real conditions keep being billed at severity 1 cosmetic and defaultHidden; that is the cost accepted here, and it is bounded because the pass_2d_follow_ups list preserves every case.

**Gap description.** No coverage gap is declared. Two naming gaps are recorded for the record and neither is actionable: graffiti/vandalism appears in none of the 128 items (nearest operational home peeling_or_discolored_paint, PAINT_INTERIOR, prime-and-repaint), and torn or water-damaged wall paneling has no item whose atomic claim names it ('wallboard' occurs only inside damaged_drywall_or_cracks' embed_text, whose claim subject is 'drywall'; dated_wood_paneling is a modernization/style item). Each rests on a single model-judge lead, each is operationally carried by an existing work item, and neither implicated item has a legitimate split parent through which a new successor could be authored, so promoting either would force migration_system_gap on evidence that cannot ground it.

### CAP-016 — Swirled plaster ceiling texture resolved to popcorn/acoustic texture (lead only)

Status `deferred_insufficient_evidence` · outcome `deferred_insufficient_evidence` · confidence `medium` · items `popcorn_or_acoustic_ceiling_texture` · deferral `lead_only`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows a heavy swirled or stippled plaster ceiling texture that is not popcorn/acoustic texture, and the catalog claim commits to popcorn or acoustic texture.

**Cluster.** subject: ceiling texture; mechanism: heavy hand-troweled swirl/fan/stomp plaster texture applied directly to a solid ceiling, with a medallion-style central swirl; not sprayed popcorn or acoustic texture, and not a suspended tile grid; kinds: modernization; scenes: living_areas, bedroom; commitments: object. UNCHANGED SINGLETON. No reshape, no merge, no split, no unit moved, no coverage row promoted; the claim_under_test is kept verbatim because the reviewer adjudicated against those exact words and re-scoping them would silently re-scope a pinned adjudication. I tested and rejected the one merge that was available. Pooling into CAP-003 (the other dated_style modernization cluster in walls-ceilings, and the only one in the family carrying four human supports) fails convention 2's pooling test on three counts: different physical subject (ceiling texture vs wall paint colour), different unsupported commitment type (object vs scope), different implicated item (popcorn_or_acoustic_ceiling_texture vs paint_refresh_recommended). Pooling would also be self-serving rather than analytic - it would import a four-human bar into an object-commitment question those four humans never looked at, which is exactly the artifact-bar failure mode CAP-018/CAP-019 were split apart to avoid. Splitting the unit is equally pointless: convention 2 forbids multiplying units, and the seven issues here (five photos, one property, one condition oc1_e6be634a3ab8e0f6) are one condition and therefore one evidence unit. That leaves one model_judge lead, which by the brief can ground nothing.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions mechanism.

| Convention 1 (brief section 2): strictest claim-bearing wording governs semantic fit; Terra is shown only the atomic_claim, while name/description/embed_text/support_any are what 2d and retrieval see, and it matters which surface failed. | Steven's ruling, brief section 2 | Test the two surfaces separately before assigning ownership. | Structuring. The Terra-facing surface is the one that commits to 'popcorn/stipple'. That is why primary_cause is catalog_wording_or_specificity rather than retrieval_metadata_or_reachability, and why the only lever ever worth considering is the atomic_claim rather than embed_text. |

**Diagnosis.** primary `catalog_wording_or_specificity`; contributing retrieval_metadata_or_reachability, missing_catalog_coverage. Stated as the hypothesis this deferral preserves, NOT as an established finding - no human or gold unit exists here, so nothing below is grounded. The hypothesis is the same retrieval-vs-claim split named in convention 1. Terra sees only the atomic_claim, which commits to the object 'dated popcorn/stipple texture'. 2d and retrieval see embed_text, which widens the item to 'Continuous stippled, sprayed acoustic, or heavy textured finish applied directly to a solid ceiling surface, without removable tiles or a visible grid', plus a support_any that contains the bare token 'ceiling texture'. Those two surfaces disagree about one thing precisely: whether a heavy applied texture that is NOT sprayed popcorn is inside the item. Retrieval says yes and did so consistently - the item held rank 1 on 6 of the 7 issues and rank 2 on the seventh, and two of the seven resolved by lexical_shortcut on a support_phrase_hit, meaning the generic phrase 'ceiling texture' alone routes any dated-ceiling-texture observation to this item without the LLM being consulted. The claim says no. Catalog ownership follows because the divergence lives entirely inside one item's own fields and no other stage had a lever: 2a described the finish accurately, 2c's kind was right, scene gating never bit, and 2d had no better-named item to choose because none exists. Note the counter-consideration honestly: divergence_scan reports promised_not_claimed = [] for this item, i.e. the automated scan does NOT flag the split I am describing, because 'heavy textured finish' is lexically close enough to 'stipple texture' that the concept extractor treats them as one. If the divergence is real it is a semantic one the scan cannot see, which is precisely why it needs a human verdict rather than a proposal.

**Semantic before after.** No change. popcorn_or_acoustic_ceiling_texture keeps atomic_claim {subject: 'ceiling texture', state: 'dated popcorn/stipple texture', ontology_basis: 'dated_style'} and keeps every retrieval surface verbatim, including embed_text ('Continuous stippled, sprayed acoustic, or heavy textured finish applied directly to a solid ceiling surface, without removable tiles or a visible grid'), support_any and the ten-entry deny_any. The before/after that a promoted version of this cluster would write is recorded here as a hypothesis only, so the lead can see what is being deferred: state would move from 'dated popcorn/stipple texture' toward a formulation covering any dated heavy texture applied directly to a solid ceiling surface, which must retain the tiles/grid discriminator. Nothing authorises writing it today.

**Retrieval effects.** None. No embed_text, support_any, deny_any, name or description is touched, so no item's rank changes anywhere and the existing rank-1-on-six-of-seven behaviour is unchanged. Recorded because it raises the cost of the alternative lever: embed_text is the entire embedding input when non-empty and cannot be verified offline, so any future proposal here should confine itself to the atomic_claim, which is Terra-facing only and leaves retrieval untouched.

**Economics fit.** Not applicable - no change, and economics fields are never authorable for this entry in any case. Context only: severity 2, tier work, scope repair, work_item_code CEILING_TEXTURE_UPDATE, pricing_status null, cost_model null. A swirl ceiling and a popcorn ceiling would price through the same work item, which is part of why this reads as a naming question rather than a coverage question.

**Package route effects.** None. The item keeps package_affinity package_support in bathroom_modernization, bedroom_modernization, kitchen_modernization and living_modernization, and route_override stays null. It is a support in every package, never a driver, so even a promoted widening could add support conditions to already-driven packages but could not create eligibility or change routing.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. trade_bucket is paint_drywall, which is not product-quarantined; the electrical product quarantine does not reach this cluster and nothing here forces non_catalog_action on policy grounds.

**Expected benefit.** None claimed - no change is proposed. The value of this judgment is negative-space and procedural: it keeps a genuinely coherent catalog hypothesis (a real object-commitment mismatch on the Terra-facing surface, with 2a, 2c, scene gating and 2d all exonerated) on the worklist with a corrected promotion recipe and a confirmed-native lever, instead of either spending a proposal on one model_judge lead or discarding the finding as noise.

**Regression risk.** None from this judgment, since nothing changes. The risk this judgment avoids is concrete and named: widening the atomic_claim toward 'heavy applied texture' on one lead's evidence would attack the very clause that produced this cluster's only correct rejection (rc_62d0dc3b9df8, a suspended acoustic-tile ceiling that 2d already routed to this item and that only Terra's narrow claim read rejected), on an item with zero hallucination controls and one correct-rejection control - i.e. a broadening that could not be proved safe.

**Unresolved questions.** The substantive question the deferral holds open, in the form a reviewer can answer: is a heavy hand-troweled swirl/fan/stomp plaster ceiling inside or outside 'dated popcorn/stipple texture'? The two surfaces disagree - embed_text says inside ('heavy textured finish applied directly to a solid ceiling surface'), the atomic_claim says outside. A reviewer who answers 'inside' makes this a no_change plus documentation; 'outside, and billable' makes it a one-op claim widening; 'outside, and not billable' makes it a correct rejection the catalog is currently getting right by accident., Prior to that, a convention question only Steven can settle: convention 4 names popcorn ceilings as a qualifying concrete tell of datedness. Is a decorative troweled swirl/medallion plaster finish also an era-specific tell, or is it a deliberate decorative finish that convention 3 would treat as mere presence and therefore not billable by itself? Nothing in the frozen record answers this, and it decides whether the condition should be billed at all - which is upstream of what it should be called., CORRECTION TO THE PROMOTION RECIPE CARRIED IN FROM THE STRUCTURE STEP, which the lead must weigh. That recipe says one property alone 'caps at one_human_plus_independent_corroboration'. It does not - it caps at 'none'. Corroboration counts as independent only when the lead shares neither (property, photo) nor (property, condition_id) with the human unit. A human verdict on redfin_126418713 photo_027/photo_031 would share BOTH with lead:redfin_126418713:oc1_e6be634a3ab8e0f6 (that condition spans all five photos), so the corroboration would be 'method' and the bar would stay none. What that single review WOULD buy is real but smaller: one reviewed human/gold unit, which makes the cluster refutation-backed via the second arm and unlocks no_change, non_catalog_action and migration_system_gap - enough to close the cluster as documented, not enough to author an op. Reaching a met bar needs a SECOND property with troweled swirl texture: two human verdicts on two properties gives two_independent_human with no correlation_note owed; one human here plus an independent lead elsewhere gives one_human_plus_independent_corroboration., Expressibility is confirmed and is NOT a blocker, so it should not be re-derived later. I ran the real in-memory dry run as a read-only probe (nothing written, no op proposed): setting /successors/0/atomic_claim on legacy_id popcorn_or_acoustic_ceiling_texture, together with /atomicity_rationale and /expected_effects/routing, classifies native ('carryover kind/atomic_claim' plus 'manifest metadata'), ok true, error null, metadata_problems empty, order_ok true, and item_diff exactly {"popcorn_or_acoustic_ceiling_texture": ["atomic_claim"]}. The entry is a reclassified carryover with a single successor at index 0, matching the four shapes the lead already verified. This is a fact about the migration surface only and is not evidence that any widening is warranted., A retrieval-side observation I could not resolve and did not act on: two of the seven issues resolved by lexical_shortcut on the support_any token 'ceiling texture', bypassing the LLM entirely. That token is generic enough to route any dated-ceiling-texture bullet to this item regardless of finish type. Whether that shortcut should be narrowed is a separate question from the claim question, it is natively expressible via overrides on support_any, and it has no evidence behind it here - flagging it so it is not lost, not proposing it.

### CAP-018 — Masonry weathering on stone billed under a brickwork material commitment

Status `deferred_insufficient_evidence` · outcome `deferred_insufficient_evidence` · confidence `high` · items `brick_weathered_or_discolored` · deferral `insufficient_evidence`

**Claim under test.** The photo shows heavily stained, moss-covered or worn-jointed masonry that is STONE rather than brick, billed under brick_weathered_or_discolored, whose atomic_claim commits to 'brickwork ... with intact mortar'.

**Cluster.** subject: exterior masonry other than siding or trim: stone facades and stone chimneys, a stone barbecue grill, a parged/painted masonry porch wall, and one genuine brick chimney; mechanism: heavy dark staining, biological growth and moss, spalling, and visibly worn or patched/repointed joints on masonry whose material is mostly stone rather than brick; kinds: degradation; scenes: exterior; commitments: material, mechanism. SPLIT OUT of the provisional CAP-002, which pooled three physical subjects (painted siding, exterior trim/casing, and masonry) under one abstract 'exterior envelope' claim. CAP-002's recomputed two_independent_human bar was carried by exactly two units that share only the scene: this masonry material mismatch and an exterior door casing routed to a siding item. Convention 2 forbids letting a differently named subject on another constituent condition rescue the claim, so those two cannot ground one another and the split deliberately COSTS the bar. That is the point of the split, not a side effect: the bar was an artifact of pooling. CAP-018 keeps exactly the masonry question. Subject (masonry vs siding/trim), trade (masonry_exterior_structure vs exterior_siding_trim), commitment type under test (material and the intact-mortar qualifier vs a fading/chalking mechanism) and the candidate lever (the brick_weathering_or_mortar_deterioration split entry vs an exterior_door_paint_failure claim widening) all differ from CAP-019's. The refuted brick unit rc_5b3b3cb9e726 moves in with them rather than staying in a siding cluster because it is the in-cluster demonstration that this exact item works when the material really IS brick - it belongs beside the widening question it constrains. Within the surviving support unit, all seven issues across six photos are kept as ONE evidence unit per convention 2 (they are one projected condition, oc1_fd62a1d71743be6b, which Terra verified once); the true branch (the genuine brick chimney in photo_023) does not rescue the five stone issues, so the reviewer's supports_claim adjudication stands as recorded.

**Reshape.** from CAP-002 (narrower): Strictly narrower than CAP-002's claim. CAP-002 asked whether exterior siding, trim OR masonry was billed under a claim naming a mechanism (fading/chalking; rot/warping/cracks) or a material (brick) that is not visible. CAP-018 keeps only the masonry arm and only the material/qualifier commitment: stone (or parged) masonry billed under brick_weathered_or_discolored's 'brickwork ... with intact mortar'. Both units carry their original CAP-002 packet rows and their reviewer text is quoted verbatim in transfer_note. No adjudication is changed, no unit is duplicated, and CAP-002's remaining unit plus CAP-019's two units account for the other three of the five provisional units.

**Mismatch class.** `operationally_consequential`; differing dimensions subject, mechanism, billability.

Among the support units one human unit supports the claim (rc_5063066c1a57: the billed masonry is mostly stone, and the material commitment is not what is visible) and one human unit refutes it (rc_5b3b3cb9e726: the facade is genuine red brick with modest soiling and continuous mortar, so both the material and the mechanism the item names ARE what is visible). The two are not in conflict about the world. They are two different properties with two different materials, and together they say something sharper than either alone: brick_weathered_or_discolored is correct on brick and wrong on stone, which is precisely a subject-scope finding rather than a mechanism finding. The refuted unit is also a warning about Terra rather than about the catalog - there Terra wrongly called a soiled brick facade uniform, and cited the claim's own definitional qualifier ('with intact mortar') as a reason to reject, which no rewording of the subject would fix. The disagreement is compounded by a coupling that must be disclosed: this cluster's retained correct-rejection control rc_dff4c3209df1 sits on redfin_10740044 photo_001, the SAME property and SAME photo as CAP-019's counted support unit rc_7b23faa0bd8f, so CAP-018 and CAP-019 are not evidentially independent on that property. Net effect on this judgment: the supporting side is one property; the constraining side is one human refutation plus one human-endorsed correct rejection that the candidate lever would flip. That balance is why the outcome is a deferral with a recipe, not a proposal.

| Convention 1 (brief section 2): strictest claim-bearing wording governs semantic fit; Terra sees only the atomic_claim while name/description/embed_text/support_any are what 2d and retrieval see, and it matters which surface failed. | Steven's ruling, brief section 2 | Test the two surfaces separately before assigning ownership. | Decisive. The 2d-facing surfaces (trade masonry_exterior_structure, embed 'Clean or seal masonry') pulled masonry observations in and 2d selected correctly; the Terra-facing atomic_claim then narrowed the subject to 'brickwork' and rejected them. Same item, one surface failed, and it is the catalog's. This is what makes primary_cause catalog_wording_or_specificity rather than terra_verification or pass_2d_selection. |
| Convention 2 (brief section 2): judge disjunctions and multi-photo conditions at the characteristic issue level; a true branch does not rescue a differently named mechanism; never multiply units. | Steven's ruling, brief section 2 | Several issues on one condition are one evidence unit. | Two effects. (a) rc_5063066c1a57's seven issues across six photos stay ONE unit, so the counted human total is 1, not 7, and the bar is none. (b) The single genuine brick issue (photo_023) does not rescue the five stone issues, so the reviewer's supports_claim adjudication is right on its own terms. The same convention is what forbids CAP-002's two supporting units from grounding one another, which is why this cluster exists and why the split deliberately costs the pooled bar. |
| Convention 3 (brief section 2): plain, basic or builder-grade presence is not billable by itself; a condition needs visible datedness, deterioration or incompleteness. | Steven's ruling, brief section 2 | Presence alone does not bill; visible deterioration must be established. | Turns the widening from a clean win into an open question. The control rc_dff4c3209df1 shows visible staining and grime on stone, but on my own reading the stone there is sound and lightly soiled. Under convention 3 that may well not be billable at all, in which case the material widening would import a non-billable presence case. This is the ruling the promotion recipe asks for. |

The reviewer refuted that gold row with 'reads as sound, light-grey concrete with only light surface dirt ... not visibly weathered, spalled or stained'. rc_dff4c3209df1 is the same shape on stone rather than concrete, which is why I forecast flips_to_supported as a HARM rather than as a recovered miss. |

The judgment is a deferral with an executable recipe rather than a proposal at bar none. |

There is no lead and no gold unit in this cluster to corroborate with, so no corroboration path exists on the frozen evidence; only a new human review can move the bar. |

**Diagnosis.** primary `catalog_wording_or_specificity`; contributing overlapping_or_ambiguous_ontology. The defect is real, it is catalog-owned, and it lives on exactly one surface. Convention 1 makes the two surfaces the thing to test. (1) TERRA-FACING SURFACE. Terra sees only the atomic_claim, which is {subject: 'brickwork', state: 'weathering or discoloration with intact mortar', ontology_basis: visible_deterioration}. Terra's verdict on rc_5063066c1a57 is 'unsupported' with the rationale 'The visible masonry is heavily stained and weathered, but it is stone rather than brick and some joints appear deteriorated.' That rationale concedes the substance of the condition and rejects on the two commitments the claim adds: the material, and the intact-mortar qualifier. Terra applied the claim it was given, faithfully and accurately - the reviewer's own reading agrees that most of the masonry is stone and that several photos show mortar that is not intact. This is the signature of a claim that is mis-scoped, not of a verifier that failed. (2) RETRIEVAL/2d-FACING SURFACE. Everything the catalog shows 2d points the other way: trade_bucket masonry_exterior_structure, name 'Weathered or Discolored Brick', description 'Brickwork with weathering, staining, or discoloration; mortar intact', and an embed_text that ends 'Clean or seal masonry.' The divergence scan records exactly this as promised_not_claimed = ['masonry']. So the item advertises masonry cleaning and sealing to the stage that selects it, then binds the verifier to brickwork. Retrieval and 2d behaved accordingly: brick_weathered_or_discolored ranked 1 on four of the seven issues and 2 on two more, and on three issues 2d actively chose it over a higher-ranked non-masonry candidate (over exterior_siding_discoloration_fading at rank 1 on both explicit stone observations, and over exterior_door_paint_failure at rank 1 on the parged-wall observation). 2d moved toward the better subject every time. It had nowhere better to go, because this item is the catalog's only masonry-degradation concept. (3) SCALE OF THE MISFIT, refined. The prior hypothesis said five stone issues plus one genuine brick chimney. Counting the seven issues by photo, it is five stone (photo_002 grill, photo_014 chimney, photo_022 grill, photo_034 chimney and stone walls, photo_035 stone facade), ONE genuine brick (photo_023, 'The tall brick chimney shows weathering'), and one PARGED/painted masonry wall below the porch (photo_023, 'Exposed painted or parged masonry below the porch is stained and uneven'), whose substrate is concealed by the coating and is therefore neither brick nor stone on the face of it. That third material matters: a brick-or-stone widening would still not name it. (4) WHY THE SMALLEST LEVER IS UNDER-DETERMINED. Terra gave two grounds. A subject widening to brick-or-stone answers only the first. The second ground - 'some joints appear deteriorated' - is a failure of the claim's own 'with intact mortar' qualifier, and relaxing that qualifier collides head-on with the split that created these two successors, whose stated atomicity_rationale is that the parent 'conflated failing mortar joints ... with brick weathering/discoloration'. The sibling that names deteriorated joints, mortar_joints_deteriorated, was structurally unreachable here: rank null on all seven candidate lists and kind-gated (kind defect against a degradation observation, kind_ok false in the reachability scan). So on this unit the material widening alone might not even recover the case it was designed to recover, and the fuller fix would blur a deliberate boundary. That is a second, independent reason this cluster is not ready to author an op, over and above the bar.

|---|---|

I rejected it as the primary owner because the catalog's own organisation already puts this condition inside brick_weathered_or_discolored - masonry trade, 'Clean or seal masonry' embed, sole masonry-degradation concept - so the concept is present and one surface is narrow, which is a wording defect under convention 1. Preferring the wording reading also picks the smaller lever: widening a claim on an existing split successor rather than authoring a new successor. Recorded as a live alternative for the promoter, and note that if it is ever taken the legitimate split parent (brick_weathering_or_mortar_deterioration) is available, so the path is not blocked. |
| pass_2d_selection | 2d did not fail; it over-performed. On five of seven issues it took the rank-1 or rank-2 masonry item, and on three issues it passed OVER a higher-ranked candidate to reach the masonry item (exterior_siding_discoloration_fading at rank 1 on both stone-wall observations, exterior_door_paint_failure at rank 1 on the parged-wall observation). There is no materially more exact reachable item it declined: the only other masonry item is kind-gated. pass_2d_follow_ups is therefore empty for this cluster, and the scope direction's warning against compensating for a 2d failure by broadening the catalog does not bite here. |
| terra_verification | Terra was accurate about the photos and faithful to the claim: it conceded the weathering, then rejected on material and on deteriorated joints, both of which the reviewer independently confirms are what the images show. Contrast the unit that stayed in CAP-002 and the sibling brick unit rc_5b3b3cb9e726 in this cluster, where Terra's rationale ('generally uniform ... no clear weathering') is contradicted by the image - that IS a Terra error, and it is the reason rc_5b3b3cb9e726 is adjudicated refuted. Two different Terra behaviours on one item; only the second is a verifier fault, and it is not the one this cluster is about. |

The frozen attribution rationale confirms 2a carried the condition on four of six photos explicitly. 2a is frozen and, on this cluster, also right; the word 'stone' survives all the way to 2d and is lost only when the claim is applied. |

That is an overlapping-ontology observation shared with CAP-019, and the lead may prefer to record it once at family level. |

The gate that kind creates - defect-kind mortar_joints_deteriorated unreachable from a degradation observation - is the split working as designed and is correct on its own terms. It is nevertheless the mechanism by which the deteriorated-joints half of this property's masonry condition has no reachable home, which is why it appears in contributing causes as an ontology observation rather than as a kind error. |

For context only, the item bills when supported (billability: bills true, route work, tier work, defaultHidden false). |

**Alternative-stage analysis.**

| existing_item_exists | Partly, and that partial fit is the finding. brick_weathered_or_discolored exists, is the catalog's ONLY non-pool masonry-degradation concept, sits in trade masonry_exterior_structure, prices through MASONRY_REPAIR at severity 1, tier work, scope repair, and its embed_text ends 'Clean or seal masonry' - it is where this condition belongs. What does not exist is any item whose claim names stone: a full scan of all 128 items returns no stone-facade concept, and 'stone' appears only in two paving items and the interior dated_fireplace_surround. mortar_joints_deteriorated exists for the deteriorated-joints half but is kind defect. So the right home exists, with the wrong claim, and the second-best home is kind-gated. |
| upstream_kind_wrong | No. 2c assigned degradation to observations about staining, weathering and discoloration, which is correct, and all seven issues routed with routing_reason exact_kind. That gate is the split boundary behaving as designed. |

Retrieval delivered the masonry item at rank 1 on four issues, rank 2 on two, and rank 6 on the parged-wall issue. One documented-not-actioned observation: exterior_siding_discoloration_fading's embed_text promises 'Lower wall staining near grade, darkened exterior siding near foundation', which is why it took rank 1 on both explicit stone-wall observations - a retrieval surface reaching into masonry territory. Editing embed_text is unverifiable offline (it is the entire embedding input when set), so this is a documentation item, not a lever. |

There is no materially more exact reachable item that was passed over, so there is nothing here for pass_2d_follow_ups. This is the opposite of CAP-019, where a rank-2 kind-compatible and scene-compatible item was passed over for a rank-5 one. |
