On rc_5063066c1a57 Terra's rationale ('heavily stained and weathered, but it is stone rather than brick and some joints appear deteriorated') is confirmed by the reviewer and by my own reading of photo_014, so Terra applied a mis-scoped claim correctly. On rc_5b3b3cb9e726 Terra's rationale ('generally uniform, with mortar joints intact and no clear weathering or discoloration') IS contradicted by the image per the reviewer, and it also cites the claim's own definitional qualifier ('with intact mortar') as a ground for rejection - that unit is a Terra error and the reviewer refuted the cluster claim on it accordingly. Both facts are recorded; only the first bears on the catalog. |
| wording_creates_unsupported_commitments | Yes - this is the whole finding, and there are two commitments, not one. (a) MATERIAL: the atomic_claim subject is 'brickwork' while the item's name, trade, description-adjacent embed ('Clean or seal masonry') and role as the catalog's sole masonry-degradation concept all point at masonry generally. divergence_scan records promised_not_claimed = ['masonry'] for exactly this. (b) QUALIFIER: the claim's state is 'weathering or discoloration with intact mortar'. On this property several photos show mortar that is not intact (worn joints on the sandstone bungalow; visibly patched/repointed mortar at the photo_035 stone entry), so the qualifier fails independently of the material. Terra cited both. Any repair of this claim has to decide what to do about the qualifier, and the qualifier is the split boundary against mortar_joints_deteriorated - which is why the smallest lever is not obviously sufficient and the next lever up is not obviously safe. |

The candidate widening (subject 'brickwork' -> brick or stone masonry) would flip the required correct-rejection control rc_dff4c3209df1 (redfin_10740044, photo_001 and photo_009, human v1 truth terra_claim_unsupported, reviewer adjudication refuted, i.e. the reviewer AGREED the rejection was right). The reviewer rejected it SOLELY on material: 'The facade is rusticated stone block, not brick; dark staining and grime on the stone near the basement window and along the foundation base are visible, but the item's material commitment (brickwork) is not what is there.' Remove the material commitment and the remaining facts - visible staining and grime on stone - satisfy the widened claim. I opened photo_001 to see how bad that flip would be: the facade is clean, light buff rusticated limestone with continuous joints and only mild soiling under the sill and near grade. It is a different severity class from redfin_80925528's dark-streaked, moss-grown, spalled sandstone. So the flip would be a genuinely bad one: it would bill a facade that is not visibly weathered, which is exactly what convention 3 and the gold ruling on gold:redfin_10806500:photo_001.jpg:g1 (weathering not established, not billable) forbid. Any future widening must therefore carry a visible-weathering threshold, not just a wider material, and must demonstrate rc_dff4c3209df1 still rejects under it. For the promoter: a subject widening would change no economic field - they are never authorable anyway - but it would enlarge the billed population, applying brick_weathered_or_discolored's severity 1 / tier work / scope repair / MASONRY_REPAIR / package_support in exterior_repair (pricing_status inherited_from_split_parent) to stone facades. Trade and repair are a reasonable fit (cleaning or sealing masonry is the same work on stone as on brick), which is why the mismatch is subject-and-billability rather than route or trade. Billability is the dimension that actually moves: brick_weathered_or_discolored bills (bills true, route work), so widening the subject converts a class of currently-unbilled stone conditions into billed ones, and rc_dff4c3209df1 shows that class includes at least one the humans said should not bill. |

**Regression controls.**

| rc_dff4c3209df1 | correct_rejection | brick_weathered_or_discolored | refuted | flips_to_supported | FORECAST UNDER THE CANDIDATE WIDENING THAT IS NOT BEING PROPOSED. ops is empty, so nothing changes today and this card is in fact untouched. The forecast is the point of the entry: this required correct_rejection on brick_weathered_or_discolored (redfin_10740044, photo_001 and photo_009; human v1 truth terra_claim_unsupported; reviewer adjudication refuted, i.e. the rejection was right) was rejected SOLELY on material - 'The facade is rusticated stone block, not brick; dark staining and grime on the stone ... are visible, but the item's material commitment (brickwork) is not what is there.' Remove the material commitment and the remaining recorded facts satisfy the widened claim. I opened photo_001: sound, light buff rusticated limestone, continuous joints, mild soiling under the sill and near grade - so the flip would bill a facade that is not visibly weathered. This single forecast, independently of the bar, is why no op is written. Any future proposal must show this card still_rejected under its exact wording. |
| rc_35a00646adaf | positive_use | brick_weathered_or_discolored | supports_claim | still_supported | Required positive_use on brick_weathered_or_discolored (redfin_126224899 photo_010; Terra supported; human truth exact/warranted; reviewer adjudication supports_claim: 'The yellow brick wall is heavily soiled and darkened across its lower courses beside the door while the mortar joints remain intact'). Unaffected today because nothing changes, and unaffected under any candidate widening either, since widening a subject from brick to brick-or-stone never removes brick. Listed so a future promotion inherits it as a mandatory control and so the record shows which side of the ledger is safe. |

**Semantic before after.** No change is proposed and ops is empty, so there is no before/after. Recorded for the promoter, as the shape a future op would take and NOT as a proposal: brick_weathered_or_discolored is successor index 1 of the split entry brick_weathering_or_mortar_deterioration (index 0 is mortar_joints_deteriorated), and its current atomic_claim is {subject: 'brickwork', state: 'weathering or discoloration with intact mortar', ontology_basis: 'visible_deterioration'} with name 'Weathered or Discolored Brick'. The candidate change widens the subject to cover stone (and, per unresolved question 3, arguably parged masonry) and renames accordingly, and must also refresh /atomicity_rationale and /expected_effects/routing. It does not resolve the 'with intact mortar' half of Terra's objection, which is a separate and larger decision because that qualifier is the boundary against the defect sibling.

**Retrieval effects.** None, since nothing changes. For the promoter: a claim-only widening would leave retrieval untouched, because embed_text, support_any, deny_any and require_any are unchanged and embed_text is the entire embedding input when set - so ranks would not move anywhere and the change would be verifiable offline. A name change reaches the 2d selection prompt but not the embeddings. This is one genuine attraction of the candidate lever: it is the surface that failed, and it is also the surface that can be changed without unverifiable retrieval side effects.

**Economics fit.** Not applicable - no change. Context: brick_weathered_or_discolored is severity 1, tier work, scope repair, work_item_code MASONRY_REPAIR, package_affinity exterior -> exterior_repair as package_support, pricing_status inherited_from_split_parent, bills true on route work. Cleaning or sealing stone is the same trade and roughly the same work as cleaning or sealing brick, so a subject widening would not strain the pricing; the cost of the widening is billing MORE conditions, not mispricing the ones it bills.

**Package route effects.** None, since nothing changes. A future subject widening would not touch package_affinity, package_role or route_override (none is set, and route_override's only legal value is no_action anyway), so package membership and routing would be unchanged in kind - only the number of exterior_repair support conditions would grow.

**Product policy.** Not applicable. Trade is masonry_exterior_structure, which is not product-quarantined; the electrical quarantine does not reach this cluster.

**Expected benefit.** None claimed today - that is the honest position at bar none. The benefit of THIS judgment is that it isolates a real, catalog-owned defect from the pooled CAP-002 claim that was hiding it, names the exact surface at fault (the atomic_claim subject on split successor index 1), proves that no other stage owns it, and hands forward a recipe that a future session can execute in four steps. The benefit a future proposal would claim, if the recipe is satisfied: stone and parged masonry weathering, currently the catalog's only home for which is an item that rejects it at Terra on material, would become billable through the concept that already advertises 'Clean or seal masonry'.

**Regression risk.** The risk of the change NOT made, stated so it is not lost. (1) rc_dff4c3209df1 flips: a required correct rejection the reviewer endorsed, rejected solely on material, would become supported under a bare subject widening - and the photo shows sound, lightly soiled limestone, so the flip would create a billing on a facade that is not visibly weathered. (2) Zero hallucination controls exist on this item, so no proposal here can DEMONSTRATE that a broadening does not make a false observation easier to bill; the safety case would be vacuous. (3) Relaxing the 'with intact mortar' qualifier, which is what Terra's second ground would actually require, blurs the deliberate split against mortar_joints_deteriorated and would let degradation-kind masonry observations absorb joint failures that the defect successor exists to carry. (4) The one thing NOT at risk: rc_35a00646adaf, the brick-on-brick positive use, survives any subject widening untouched.

**Unresolved questions.** BILLABILITY, and this is the question the promotion turns on. Is staining, grime or biological growth on stone masonry near grade billable at all under convention 3 (plain/basic presence is not billable by itself; a condition needs visible datedness, deterioration or incompleteness)? The reconciled gold ruling on gold:redfin_10806500:photo_001.jpg:g1 says the analogous hardscape case is NOT billable - the reviewer refuted it with 'reads as sound, light-grey concrete with only light surface dirt on the treads; it is not visibly weathered, spalled or stained', i.e. weathering was not established. rc_dff4c3209df1 is the same shape one material over. If Steven rules that ordinary soiling on sound stone is not billable, then the correct outcome for CAP-018 is no_change plus documentation, because the widening would recover the redfin_80925528 case only by also billing the redfin_10740044 case. If he rules that heavy staining, moss and worn joints ARE billable but ordinary soiling is not, then the lever is not a bare subject widening but a subject widening carrying a visible-weathering threshold, and rc_dff4c3209df1 must be shown to still reject under it., SUFFICIENCY OF THE SMALLEST LEVER. Terra rejected rc_5063066c1a57 on TWO grounds, material and 'some joints appear deteriorated'. Widening the subject to brick-or-stone answers only the first, and the reviewer confirms several photos show mortar that is not intact, so the widened claim might still fail on this very unit. Relaxing the 'with intact mortar' qualifier would answer the second but collides with the split's own atomicity_rationale (the parent 'conflated failing mortar joints ... with brick weathering/discoloration') and would steal from mortar_joints_deteriorated, which is itself unreachable from degradation-kind observations (rank null, kind_ok false on all seven issues). Someone has to decide whether a stone-masonry weathering condition with worn joints belongs to the degradation successor, to the defect successor, or to neither, before an op is written., PARGED AND PAINTED MASONRY IS A THIRD MATERIAL. Counting the support unit's seven issues by photo gives five stone, one genuine brick (photo_023 chimney) and one parged/painted masonry wall below the porch (photo_023, 'Exposed painted or parged masonry below the porch is stained and uneven'), whose substrate is concealed. A brick-or-stone widening still would not name that one. If the intent is 'the catalog's masonry-weathering concept', the subject should probably read as masonry generally rather than as an enumeration of two materials - which is a slightly larger wording change and needs the same evidence., A PROMOTION I CONSIDERED AND REJECTED. The nearest thing to a second independent stone unit in the frozen record is the coverage row gold:redfin_11000447:photo_001.jpg:g6 ('Cornice and upper trim show weathering and paint wear', adjudicated supports_claim, covering_item null). I did not promote it, and the lead should know why: nothing there was billed under brick_weathered_or_discolored, the subject is cornice and upper trim rather than a masonry field, and the reviewer's own text says the decisive fact cannot be established - 'how much of that is paint wear versus soiled stone facing cannot be separated'. Promoting it would have manufactured a two_independent_human bar out of a row that does not test this claim. The other stone/masonry coverage rows are equally unusable here (photo_007 g6 is peeling paint on interior basement masonry; photo_008 g8 and g10 are a stone-PATTERN resilient floor)., PROMOTION RECIPE, stated so it can be executed. (1) One further independent human-reviewed unit on a DIFFERENT property where stone or parged masonry weathering was lost at Terra specifically to the brickwork commitment - that takes the bar to two_independent_human on two properties, and the correlation_note would then not be needed. (2) Steven's ruling on question 1 above. (3) A decision on question 2 (the intact-mortar qualifier). (4) A demonstrated expected_after for rc_dff4c3209df1 under the exact proposed wording; if it still says flips_to_supported, the proposal should not be written. Expressibility is already settled and is not part of the recipe: the lead's dry run confirms that setting /successors/1/atomic_claim and /successors/1/name on the brick_weathering_or_mortar_deterioration split entry (index 1 is brick_weathered_or_discolored; index 0 is mortar_joints_deteriorated), together with /atomicity_rationale and /expected_effects/routing, classifies native and runs clean. Expressibility is not warrant., MY OWN PHOTO READING vs THE REVIEWER: no disagreement to record. I opened two photos and both readings match the frozen verdicts (see photos_opened). Nothing in this judgment changes, or asks to change, any adjudication.

**Human disposition.** _blank_ (approved / approved_with_modification / rejected / deferred / reclassified_non_catalog)

The concept is nevertheless REACHED today: on this very photo, Pass 2d resolved the observation 'The porch guard and ironwork feel dated.' to curb_appeal_upgrade (resolution_path llm, routing_reason exact_kind). So the porch ironwork is covered - and deliberately unbilled, because curb_appeal_upgrade carries route_override no_action. The gold row's security-bar framing never entered the pipeline at all (gold attribution = downstream, stage 2c), so even a perfect catalog would not have surfaced this wording. Product question flagged: whether removing security bars/grilles is billable scope is a product/policy decision, not a catalog defect - and if the answer is ever 'yes, bill it', note that route_override is NOT natively authorable on a carryover entry (curb_appeal_upgrade is change_type 'reclassified', not 'split'), so that change would be a migration-system gap, not a native decisions proposal. |
| gold:redfin_10806500:photo_001.jpg:g4 | Corrugated downspout extension terminates at grade close to the basement window and foundation | miss_candidate | downstream, 2c | supports_claim | covered_by_existing_item | clogged_or_damaged_gutters | The downspout concept is covered by clogged_or_damaged_gutters on the SUBJECT surface (subject 'gutters and downspouts', work_item_code GUTTER_REPAIR, remedy = extend/redirect the discharge) and its description explicitly promises 'dumping water near foundation'. But its atomic_claim - the only text Terra sees - lists the state as 'clogged, sagging, or disconnected drainage'. A corrugated extension that is intact, connected and simply terminates at grade beside the basement window is none of those three, so Terra would very likely reject it. This is a claim-vs-retrieval divergence inside an existing item, not a missing item, and atomic_claim IS natively settable on a carryover entry - so it is a cheap wording follow-up if the lead wants it. standing_water_or_poor_grading does not cover it either: its claim commits to 'standing water or negative grading at the structure', and neither pooling nor grading is asserted or visible. Separately, the miss on this photo is NOT catalog-owned: gold attribution is downstream, stage 2c, confidence high - the observation never survived to a bullet, so no catalog wording would have recovered it. |
| gold:redfin_10806500:photo_001.jpg:g6 | Storm/screen door is dated | miss_candidate | downstream, 2b | supports_claim | covered_by_existing_item | dated_entry_or_patio_door | Clean coverage, and it bills. dated_entry_or_patio_door names storm doors on every surface that matters: description ('Dated front, entry, storm, sliding glass, or patio door'), embed_text ('Front, storm, sliding glass, double, or patio door unit appears aged, worn, builder-grade, or stylistically dated'), support_any contains 'storm door'. The reviewer established datedness, not mere presence ('white aluminium storm/security door with a decorative scrollwork insert, a dated style'), so convention 3 is satisfied - this is not plain/basic presence. Under the Phase C rules a refuted adjudication can never become support, so this row grounds nothing: no coverage gap, no cluster attachment, no covering-item claim. Counterfactual only, for the lead's record: if a window HAD been inside the enclosure, the catalog has no item for a window opening within a wet area - dated_or_older_windows is dated-style/aging units (kind modernization, tier optional), tub_surround_or_shower_pan_damage is a cracked/chipped enclosure, missing_or_damaged_caulk_at_tub_or_shower has require_any [caulk, sealant, silicone, grout line], and bathroom_paint_moisture_damage needs bubbling/discoloration/efflorescence. That hypothetical hole is NOT evidenced by this row and must not be promoted from it. Two things I record WITHOUT proposing anything. (2) UNRESOLVED QUESTION, explicitly not a re-adjudication: the reviewer's 'whitish streaks along the right wall' are consistent with efflorescence, which would belong to garage_or_basement_damage (defect, foundation_structure, utility, bills, FOUNDATION_REPAIR) - a different route, severity and diagnostic obligation, i.e. operationally consequential if mis-selected. The reviewer and the gold note both treat the streaking as descriptive rather than an established defect, so I do not upgrade it; I flag it so the lead can decide whether a moisture question is owed. |

Same retrieval-vs-claim divergence as photo_005 g3: visible_electrical_risks promises bare-bulb language in embed_text that its atomic claim excludes (divergence_scan promised_not_claimed = missing_incomplete). 2d resolved no lighting observation on this photo; the only electrical-adjacent observations ('Electrical installation practices appear older-looking.', 'Mechanical systems appear aged.') resolved to None. |
| gold:redfin_10806500:photo_007.jpg:g9 | Open wood basement stairs are worn | out_of_catalog | —, — | supports_claim | not_billable_observation | — | Steven's entry-steps ruling applied with the staining nuance kept intact. On gold:redfin_10806500:photo_001.jpg:g1 (the same property's concrete entry steps) the ruling was: the algae staining is real, weathering is NOT established, therefore not billable - staining and soiling are not by themselves established deterioration. The same nuance governs here: the reviewer records 'white-painted treads ... dirty and worn', i.e. tread soiling and paint wear on a utility stair in an unfinished basement. 'Open' stair construction (no risers) is the ordinary form of a basement stair - plain presence under convention 3, not incompleteness. No rot, no structural movement, no broken or soft tread, no loose or missing railing and no trip hazard was adjudicated. 2a itself framed the stairs as CONTINGENT future scope, not a present defect: 'If converting the basement, stair geometry, handrails, fire separation ... could become meaningful costs' - which is the unfinished_basement_present finishing opportunity, deliberately routed no_action, not present work. The pipeline agrees by omission: 'Basement stairs and access are basic.' reached 2d and resolved to null, and 'basic' is exactly the plain-presence framing convention 3 excludes. Explicitly NOT claimed, and I did not open the photo to look for it: a genuinely missing or loose handrail would be a different, billable condition under missing_or_damaged_handrails; the frozen record does not establish one and inventing it would exceed the gold finding. |
| gold:redfin_11000447:photo_001.jpg:g2 | Public sidewalk in front has visible cracking | out_of_catalog | —, — | supports_claim | not_billable_observation | — | The cracked slabs are the public sidewalk in the right-of-way in front of the stoop, not the seller's property, so no seller-billable work exists regardless of catalog coverage. Worth recording as a guardrail observation, not a coverage gap: paving_cracked_or_settled (degradation, landscaping_drains, tier work, route_override null, defaultHidden true, bills true) and paving_heaved_or_trip_hazard both commit to 'paved surfaces' with support_any including 'walkway' and NO ownership guardrail - empty deny_any, null require_any - so nothing in the catalog stops 2d from billing public right-of-way paving when an observation mentions a cracked sidewalk. That is a hallucination-adjacent exposure (billing someone else's asset), and the natural lever is a deny_any hint on the paving successors (both ARE split successors of driveway_or_walkway_cracking, so overrides are natively authorable there). I raise it as an observation for the lead; I am not proposing it, since no adjudicated unit shows the pipeline actually doing it. |

Same finding as before: no item names security bars/grilles anywhere in the 128-item catalog, and the concept lands in curb_appeal_upgrade's facade-accents claim, which is route_override no_action and therefore never bills. Two honest caveats for the lead. (a) There is a reachable BILLING alternative for this particular row that the sister row used: the subject here is a door unit ('white metal security storm door with a scroll-and-bar grille over its full height'), and dated_entry_or_patio_door explicitly covers storm doors and bills. I did not route it there because the reviewer established only the presence and prominence of the grille, not datedness, and convention 3 forbids billing presence alone; asserting datedness here would be adding a commitment the adjudication does not carry. The lead should note the resulting inconsistency across the two properties: 10806500's storm door bills (adjudicated dated), 11000447's does not (adjudicated only as a security-bar door). (b) Pass 2d resolved NO door item on this photo at all - its three resolutions were dated_or_older_windows, an unresolved railing observation, and patio_or_porch_surface_wear - so this row was never a live billing candidate. |

The only rust/corrosion item in the catalog, metal_carport_rust_corrosion, is HARD-gated by require_any ['carport','undercarport'] and can never fire here. damaged_or_rotted_siding_or_trim commits to 'exterior siding or trim' with rot/warping/cracks/missing sections - a rusted mesh grate is neither the subject nor the mechanism. boarded_up_entry_or_window is a different concept (plywood over an opening). So the exterior-metal deterioration concept has no home. Same underlying gap as gold:redfin_10806500:photo_001.jpg:g7 (worn-paint stair rail) - one concept, two mechanisms (rust/deformation and paint failure), two properties. |

That is not the ordinary appearance of a finished room, so convention 3 does not excuse it: the condition is an exposed/incomplete mechanical installation in finished space and the remedy is real work (build a chase or soffit, or reroute the run). Nothing in the catalog can express it - zero items mention duct, hvac, furnace, mechanical, boiler or water heater, and there is no hvac/mechanical trade_bucket. This RESOLVES the gold agent's stated caveat ('if v5 has no HVAC/exposed-duct item this would be out_of_catalog instead') in favour of out_of_catalog. Crucially, the catalog is NOT the operative lever on this case: the 2b bullet 'Room has a large exposed galvanized HVAC duct.' appears verbatim in p2c_dropped, so the observation died at the 2c bullet filter and would never have reached 2d even if an item existed; the frozen attribution agrees (downstream, stage 2c, confidence high). It is therefore in scope (not a frozen-Pass-2a problem - 2a called it 'the biggest functional/design issue') but the fix is upstream plus a policy decision, not a catalog wording change. |

This is exactly CAP-013's subject: no atomic_claim in the catalog reaches missing or incomplete base trim, while baseboard_wear_scuffs' embed_text promises it. Bound into CAP-013 as a support unit on its own pinned coverage row. |
| gold:redfin_11000447:photo_008.jpg:g2 | Open unfinished cutout/hole in the wall above (rough-edged opening) | miss_candidate | pass_2a, — | supports_claim | covered_by_existing_item | damaged_drywall_or_cracks | PRODUCT-QUARANTINE CHECK RUN EXPLICITLY, AND IT DOES NOT FIRE. Pass 2a called this 'An open rectangular electrical cutout is high on the partition wall' and 2d resolved that bullet to visible_electrical_risks, whose trade_bucket is electrical - the one product_quarantined trade - so this row looked like a rule-2 case. PHOTO OPENED: redfin_11000447/photo_008.jpg (same open as row g10), to answer 'does the cutout expose electrical components, or is it a plain rough-edged wall opening?'. It is a small rectangular rough-edged opening high on the partition above the vanity showing brown backing material, with NO conductors, no device box, no cover-plate remnant and no scorch marks. visible_electrical_risks' claim ('electrical components \| exposed wiring, scorch marks, or unsafe components') is therefore not what is visible; the word 'electrical' came from a 2a guess about the opening's purpose, not from anything in the image. The nearest covering concept is a rough-edged hole in a wall, and damaged_drywall_or_cracks claims exactly that ('drywall \| cracks, holes, or impact damage'), scene-eligible in bathroom. FLAGGED PLAINLY: if the lead reads triage rule 2 as driven by the frozen 2d resolution's trade metadata rather than by the nearest covering concept, this row flips to product_quarantined - I read it as the latter, both because the rule says 'nearest covering concept' and because LEAD_VERIFIED_FACTS section 6 makes billability the precondition and warns against inflating the apparent cost of the electrical quarantine with rows that are not really electrical. |
| gold:redfin_11000447:photo_008.jpg:g3 | Two crudely fitted, screw-fastened access panels are visible behind and beside the vanity | out_of_catalog | —, — | supports_claim | covered_by_existing_item | damaged_drywall_or_cracks | Not an electrical row despite the electrical-sounding neighbours on this photo. I opened redfin_11000447/photo_008.jpg to answer one question: is the access-panel wall a finished wall (a patch-and-finish defect) or an unfinished/stripped assembly (a different item)? Answer: the green partition wall beside the vanity is finished and painted, with a large square screw-fastened panel cut into it, screw heads and gapped, crudely cut edges visible, plus a separate small rectangular cutout higher on the same wall. So the scope is DRYWALL_PATCH on a finished wall - not exposed OSB (unfinished_interior_wall_osb_exposed) and not stripped-to-studs (interior_wall_stripped_to_studs). IMPORTANT caveat on 'covered': damaged_drywall_or_cracks covers this on its RETRIEVAL surface only. Its description says 'poor repairs' and its embed_text says 'rough patches, missing sections', but its atomic_claim - the only text Terra sees - is 'drywall cracks, holes, or impact damage', and divergence_scan records promised_not_claimed = missing_incomplete for exactly this item. A correctly retrieved crude access panel could therefore be rejected at Terra on claim wording. That is a catalog_wording_or_specificity signal for whichever cluster owns damaged_drywall_or_cracks (in_worklist=true; it is implicated by CAP-014), not a new coverage gap. What actually happened on this photo: 2d routed 'Walls show patching and rough edges.' to wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents, i.e. the condition landed on a hidden degradation item rather than on the visible defect item. |
| gold:redfin_11000447:photo_008.jpg:g8 | Stone-pattern floor surface has white residue or staining and darkened grout/joint lines | out_of_catalog | —, — | supports_claim | covered_by_existing_item | floor_dirty_or_heavily_soiled | Reviewer supports the finding: chalky whitish residue in patches, and printed joint lines darkened with dirt, especially around the toilet and the doorway. 'Printed joint lines' means this is a stone-pattern resilient/sheet floor, not real tile with real grout - which rules out the tile items on subject grounds independent of their claims. floor_dirty_or_heavily_soiled is the cover: its subject is 'floor' (material-agnostic), its embed_text names grime, tracked-in dirt, darkened grout and widespread staining, and bathroom is in its scene_groups. Alternatives considered and rejected as the primary ref: worn_or_stained_flooring (claim 'flooring wear, staining, or discoloration without structural concern') is a defensible second cover whose claim has no 'heavy' threshold, but it is scope=replace and the reviewer describes dirt and residue - an upkeep condition - not material deterioration warranting replacement; vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained is the right material family but its deny_any contains 'tile', which the finding's own wording risks tripping, and it likewise asserts wear on the material rather than soiling on it; tile_or_grout_damage is wrong twice over (no real grout here, and its atomic claim covers only broken/loose/missing despite an embed_text promising 'stained'). Same claim-strictness caveat as g6: the covering item's atomic claim says 'heavy visible soiling' while the reviewer describes patchy residue and darkened joints. As with g6 the residual miss is upstream: the photo's 2a bullets cover the vanity, wall scuffs, the toe-kick, wall patching, an electrical cutout and 'the flooring appears dated' (resolved to older_flooring_style) - nothing observed the residue or the darkened joints, so there was no bullet for 2d to resolve. floor_dirty_or_heavily_soiled was reachable in the frozen candidates (rank 6 and rank 3 on two observations) but never selected. No photo opened. |

| CAP-006 | rc_6285e9a79e45:issue_9b611208c1823c13 | bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn | bathroom_paint_peeling_or_worn | 2 | Observation 'The ceiling and wall area near the tub look rough.' (redfin_125779232 photo_007) resolved to bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn by lexical_shortcut/support_phrase_hit with bathroom_paint_peeling_or_worn at rank 2 and wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents at rank 3, both kind_ok and scene_ok. The word 'tub' appears only as a location in the observation. The wall condition was independently billed on the same property via damaged_drywall_or_cracks and wall_scuffs_marks_or_dents, so again nothing was lost - the cost is one extra condition billed under the wrong subject. Same aggregation event as the entry above: four bullets naming three building elements were bound to one condition. |
| CAP-006 | rc_6285e9a79e45:issue_c2785d1cb9cf1e59 | bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn | vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained | 4 | Observation 'The floor has discoloration and staining around the tub and vanity.' (redfin_125779232 photo_007, kind degradation, scene bathroom) resolved to bath_fixtures_stained_or_worn by lexical_shortcut/support_phrase_hit while three flooring items sat in the same frozen top-8 (worn_or_stained_flooring 2, floor_dirty_or_heavily_soiled 3, vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained 4, all kind_ok and scene_ok). The reviewer identifies the yellowed blotched sheet vinyl as the genuinely deteriorated component. The correct item was in fact selected for the sibling floor bullets on the same two photos and billed (oc1_df18cf31cc724d4c), so 2d had the answer and used it elsewhere in the same run. Note for the 2d owner: support_phrase_hit only fires on the already top-ranked candidate, so the fix is selection or ranking, not the support list. |

### bathroom_dated_finish_subject

| proposal | case | selected | better item | rank | note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAP-017 | rc_45b9d2f6923c | dated_wallpaper_present | outdated_bathroom_finishes | 6 | Confusion family, stated in full: bathroom dated-finish subject confusion: a material-neutral 'wallcovering/finish is dated' observation resolved to a material-specific item that supplies an object word the observation never contained Issue fe4392d2f287d083, observation 'Wallcovering is dated.', scene bathroom, kind modernization, resolution_path llm, routing_reason exact_kind. Frozen top-8: dated_electrical_outlets_switches 1, dated_wood_paneling 2, vanity_countertop_dated 3, dated_window_treatment_valance 4, dated_wallpaper_present 5, outdated_bathroom_finishes 6, older_flooring_style 7, dated_bathroom_flooring_style 8. 2d took rank 5 over rank 6 although reachability_scan shows outdated_bathroom_finishes with kind_ok true and scene_ok true. The selection rule to examine: when the observation names no material (wallcovering, finish, surface), 2d should prefer the subject-general item (outdated_bathroom_finishes, subject 'bathroom finishes') over any item whose atomic_claim subject asserts a specific material (wallpaper, paneling, tile), because Terra will be asked to verify that material word and the observation gives no basis for it. Note for whoever works this: the most exact item for the true object, vintage_tile_pattern_style (subject 'bathroom tile'), was NOT in the candidate list at all - its require_any ['tile','tiles','tiled'] hard-gated it against a tile-free observation - so 2d could not have chosen it and this follow-up asks only for the rank-6 item it could have chosen. Single occurrence, one property, one photo; treat as a hypothesis to test against other neutral-observation cases, not as a measured rate. |

| proposal | case | selected | better item | rank | note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAP-019 | rc_7b23faa0bd8f (redfin_10740044, production run 20260821_233648_4cd603bb, issue 299f90fb6266878f, photo_001.jpg) | exterior_siding_discoloration_fading | exterior_door_paint_failure | 2 | Observation 'White-painted trim appears unfinished in spots.' on a solid limestone rowhouse with no siding anywhere. Frozen list: soffit_or_porch_ceiling_weathered#1, exterior_door_paint_failure#2, baseboard_wear_scuffs#3, shed_exterior_paint_failure#4, exterior_siding_discoloration_fading#5, fence_weathered#6, brick_weathered_or_discolored#7. resolution_path llm, routing_reason exact_kind; the scan records the rank-2 item as kind_ok true and scene_ok true. 2d skipped ranks 1 and 2 for rank 5. Cost was total: this was the only issue in condition oc1_30375086ad2fd9f6, Terra correctly answered the siding claim it was shown, and a human-graded exact/warranted condition was excluded (reason_code verdict_unsupported). Caveat 2d cannot fix by itself: exterior_door_paint_failure's atomic_claim says 'exterior door paint' while the reviewer records the door leaf as sound, so a correct selection would still have handed Terra a claim narrower than the defect - the residual is the catalog wording observation recorded in this cluster, not a further 2d fault. |
| CAP-019 | rc_a35f1b3a231e (redfin_166147710, canary run 20260817_224909_2f979a60, issue a79e27df53f01d60, photo_019.jpg) | exterior_siding_discoloration_fading | exterior_door_paint_failure | 1 | Observation 'Painted wood trim appears weathered in places.' Frozen list: exterior_door_paint_failure#1, soffit_or_porch_ceiling_weathered#2, deck_surface_weathering#3, fence_weathered#4, exterior_siding_discoloration_fading#5, shed_exterior_paint_failure#6, baseboard_wear_scuffs#7, patio_or_porch_surface_wear#8. 2d passed over its own rank-1 candidate and resolved to rank 5, the same landing as the other case. Logged even though it cost nothing: this house genuinely has painted wood siding, the condition's other two issues (33eb0f5e5ad3de52 and 81fdc4d589939872, both true siding observations) carried it, all three collapsed into condition oc1_b2e96c2b1020d48b, and the unit is adjudicated refuted with the item judged correct. It is logged because it is the second instance of the same selection behaviour and is the reason to treat the pattern as systematic rather than as one bad call - and because the collapse of three issues into one condition is exactly what hid it. |

### exterior_walking_surface_deck_vs_patio

| proposal | case | selected | better item | rank | note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAP-009 | rc_3a19f759f2d9 | patio_or_porch_surface_wear | deck_surface_weathering | 4 | Confusion family, stated in full: exterior walking-surface wear: bare grey weathered wood decking/stair treads vs aged or coating-failed concrete patio-or-porch slab Two issues on redfin_10735912 - 3cf3f267e567d48d 'The porch floorboards are heavily weathered' (photo_017) and 0916a53bbbd114f9 'The wood front porch is weathered' (photo_028) - resolved via resolution_path llm, routing_reason exact_kind, to rank-1 patio_or_porch_surface_wear while deck_surface_weathering sat at rank 4 with kind_ok true and scene_ok true and no deny_any trigger. The photos show bare unfinished grey wood with raised grain and no coating, so 'clean, sand, stain, refinish' (DECK_REFINISH) is the exact repair and 'resurface or reseal' (SURFACE_RESURFACE) is the approximate one. The selection is broad-but-true rather than wrong, and it is downstream-neutral (both items are severity 1, tier work, scope cosmetic, trade exterior_siding_trim, package_support in exterior_repair), so this is a low-severity 2d specificity follow-up, not a catalog defect. Do not fix it by widening either item: deck_surface_weathering already owns wood decking, and widening patio_or_porch_surface_wear would strand its concrete-slab controls. The third issue on this case, 55f86b711520696c 'Porch and entry details appear aging' on photo_001, is NOT part of this follow-up - photo_001 shows a rowhouse front with no porch, so it is a Pass 2a problem with no correct 2d answer. |

### flooring_dated_style_vs_wear

| proposal | case | selected | better item | rank | note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAP-005 | rc_9c702893a6ce | vinyl_linoleum_worn_or_stained | older_flooring_style | not in pool | redfin_80916010 photo_005, issue 14a0ed440898b159. Two defects in one case, both non-catalog. (a) Reachability: older_flooring_style was not in the pool and could not have been - routing_reason=exact_kind on a degradation bullet, all seven candidates degradation, older_flooring_style kind_ok=false (reachability_scan). better_rank is null for that reason, and the best in-pool alternative (worn_or_stained_flooring at rank 2) over-claims wear exactly as the selected item does, so no re-ranking rescues this. (b) Mechanism, now verified rather than assumed: 2d took resolution_path=lexical_shortcut with shortcut_reason=support_phrase_hit, and re-running the shipped matcher (tools/scene_classifier_passes.py::_resolve_candidate_via_lexical_shortcut with tools/pipeline_common.py::term_matches) over the real observation against the item's shipped support_any returns exactly one hit: the bare material token 'vinyl'. Not 'worn vinyl', not 'stained vinyl'; 'worn' is not a token of this item at all, so the case's attribution rationale is wrong about which token fired. The shortcut therefore skipped the LLM entirely and asserted 'staining, discoloration, or wear on intact material' on the strength of a material noun; Terra correctly rejected that claim and a real dated-flooring replacement went unbilled. The actionable owner is the shortcut rule - support_any is documented as a non-gating ranking hint and 125 of 128 items author bare subject nouns into it - not this item's tokens. Suggested 2d-lane fix, for the deferred Pass 2d work and explicitly NOT proposed as a catalog change: require a condition-bearing term match (or subject-plus-condition co-occurrence) before the shortcut may bypass the LLM. |

After the split the same observation ('The window and blinds are dated.') should resolve to window_blinds_dated_or_damaged, whose claim - basic, aged, bent, or unevenly hung blinds - those blinds arguably do satisfy, so I expect Terra to keep saying supported. Period millwork is era-specific, so a claim keyed on an era-specific finish could newly ACCEPT it and manufacture a false positive where the catalog is currently correct. The op's exclusion clause is meant to prevent that, but no atomic_claim in the 128-item catalog uses negation, so the clause is out of house style and its effect on Terra cannot be proven offline. Three independent verifiers flagged this. It must be settled by a live Terra check on these two photos before implementation. |
